# IT-1: The Wilson Loop as a Line Defect at the First Step

At the first RG step the Wilson loop is carried as a defect inside Bałaban's representation. On every labelled term F^Λ(1) is a dressed loop of the class of §4: the exact identity is §2 on covered terms and §3d on all terms, the per-traversal bound D1(ii) is §3a with §3d(4), and the analyticity D1(iii)′ in the background is §3e. The assembly into CRE's form ρ₁ = Σχ₁T₁[· exp A₁] is §3c. The locality statements that the later steps use are §3b, §3g, §3i, and §3j.

*Inputs.* V4 (WILSON_FIRST_RG_STEP_V4.tex, cited by label), with the enlarged retreat 𝒬⁺ of §3c(4⁗)(c) in place of V4's 𝒬(e) (V4 Lemma c5-3-1) and with the constant choices K and K′ of §3e(3.0) and §3e(3.6); Bałaban's CRE, RG1, RG2, LFR I, LFR II, VAR, PBG, REG, and UV3, read in the corrected transcriptions; the k = 1 construction check (RC_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md, Passes 4–6); and the j = 1 factor of (1.79) at the retreat-made data (RC_PAYMENT_PASS_2026_10_24.md, Sects. 2–4).

## 1. Framing: A Partition Function with a Defect

- **Two integrals.** E_ε[W_χ(C)] = Z₀(C)/Z₀, with Z₀(C) := ∫W_χ(C;U)ρ₀(U)dU. The RG is applied to numerator and denominator separately, each time preserving the integral, and no law of the block variables enters. Bałaban's densities preserve integrals only: the operation R is defined so that "integrals of the densities are unchanged" (LFR I p.176 l.4, (0.3)–(0.4)), and the density obtained "may not be equal to ρ_k, but is equivalent to it, in the sense that they have equal integrals" (LFR I p.200 l.45).
  - V4's branch identity is the first step: E[W] = E_h[F_ξ(1)], with E_h positive and normalised, defined at V4 (c3-6-1); the identity is V4 (c-one-step-branch-identity) with (native-source-physical-ratio).
- **Bałaban's first step.** ρ₁(V) = Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁} χ₁(Ω₁) T₁({Ω₁,Λ₁}) exp A₁(1/g₁², U₁) (CRE (1.28), p.253), with T₁ = Σ_{S₁} ∫dV₀|_{Ω₁ᶜ} δ(V̄₀V⁻¹)ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) ∫dA|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ} χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) exp[−½⟨A,C*Δ₁CA⟩ + ½⟨A,C*Δ₁CC^{(0)}(Λ₁)C*Δ₁CA⟩] (CRE (1.29), p.254). The integrand exp A₁ depends on the pending A and S₁ through 𝐁^{(1)}(U₁,A,S₁) of (1.30).
  - These integrals stay pending inside ρ₁: T₁ is "the integral operation" (p.254 l.2), and the later operations "are left undefined, only their basic general properties are formulated" (p.254 l.38–39). So contour links in Ω₁ᶜ are functions of the pending V₀, and links whose free inputs lie in Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ depend on the pending A and S₁. Their words are unitary, and D1's bounds hold on these terms by §3d.
- **Target form.** Z₀(C) = ∫dV Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁} χ₁ T₁[𝒲₁·exp A₁], with 𝒲₁ a dressed loop (§4). V4 works at the stage of CRE (1.15) with the cutoff (1.25): CRE's (1.26), its cluster expansion and the renormalisation (1.27)–(1.28) "are replaced by the construction of this section and are not used, except that the first-step coupling g₁(g₀) defined by (1.27) on Λ₁^{∼−1} enters, as a parameter depending on g₀ alone, the scale R₁ and the quantity ε₁" (V4 Section branch-data, at (native-source-physical-ratio)). So the identification of V4's 𝒪[𝗐_ξ·] with Σχ₁T₁[· exp A₁], with the loop carried through (1.26)–(1.28), is derived in §3c.

## 2. What V4 Gives on Covered Branches

All of the following is quoted from V4, for a labelled term with final region Λ and carrier D(Λ):

- **(a) Groups and families.** V4 Lemma c5-5-3(a): F^Λ(t) = Σ_{𝔅 compatible} A^t_Λ(𝔅), absolutely convergent. Compatible groups have disjoint intervals ℋ(W) in cyclic order, and a family containing a whole-contour group is a singleton (V4 Section joint-extraction, at (part1-group-weight)).
- **(b) Reference data.** V4 Lemma c5-5-3(b): U°₁ = id, V°₀ = id, A° = 0, Ψ° = id, exterior variable zero on J(Λ), so m° = 0.
  - The reference activities are a^Λ_W.
  - The Schur identity holds: the reference group matrix is a^Λ_W·id (V4 Lemma schur, via the constant gauge R_u).
  - S°_Λ = Σ_{𝔅 compatible} ∏_{W∈𝔅} a^Λ_W.
- **(c) Exact identity.** Lemma c5-5-3(c): F^Λ(t) = S°_Λ f_ξ(tm) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅ compatible} S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ R^t_Λ(𝔅).
- **(d) Resummation against the reference exclusions.** Lemma c5-5-3(d), via Kotecký–Preiss:
  - q°_Λ(𝔅) := S°^{[𝔅]}_Λ/S°_Λ = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅} Φ°_Λ(Γ));
  - |q°_Λ(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_{W∈𝔅} e^{θ°(W)}, with θ°(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16.
- **(e) Ordered-product form.** Lemma c5-5-7(b):
  - R^t_Λ(𝔅) = dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Υ^t_{W₁}U^t₁ ⋯ Υ^t_{Wₙ}U^t_n);
  - Υ^t_W := w^t_Λ(W)∫π(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)⁻¹) d⊗ν̂^{f,t} − a^Λ_W·id, an element of End(V_π);
  - the U^t_j are unitary background words.
- **(f) Bounds.**
  - ‖Υ‖_op ≤ K_χ·sup_h |dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Υπ(h))| (Burnside, Lemma c5-5-7(a));
  - ‖Υ^t_W‖ ≤ K_χ(M_Λ(W) + |a^Λ_W|) (Lemma c5-5-7(e));
  - |a^Λ_W| ≤ M_Λ(W) (Lemma c5-5-3(d));
  - the root weight: Lemma c5-5-7 takes only the first-order gain ρ_t = Cg₀(1+2r) per root. "Section c5-5R recovers the second order for the undifferenced slots at t∈{0,1} and for the activities a^Λ_W (Lemma c5-5R-3); the differenced slots and the marked part are unchanged" (V4, the paragraph after Lemma c5-5-7). So ρ = O(g₀²(1+r)⁴) holds for those two parts only. Prop. c5-5R-4 states the second-order rate ε̄_ph of the family-sum factor, (c5-5-9R).
- **(g) Identification of S°.** S°_Λ = κ_C e^{E°_Λ} with E°_Λ := E°_{D(Λ),C,T}: Lemma c5-7-1 and Cor. c5-7-2(a) at the retreated carrier (Remark c5-5-11, item (1); Section c5-5-0: "identified … with κ_Ce^{E°_Λ} by Corollary c5-7-2, under (c5-7-T)"), and Prop. c4c-5-1 with Cor. main-factorisation at I. The hypotheses are (c5-7-T):
  - (i) the collar conditions, and the contour a coarse translate of the counterclockwise (e₁,e₂) square of side nL;
  - (ii) the common small-coupling thresholds;
  - (iii) K_μ ≥ 3(n+2) for every coarse period, and 4ne^{−cι_T} ≤ 1 for the n-uniform form of Cor. c5-7-2(c).

  The split E°_{D,C_n,T} = (F_{D,C,T} − F_{C,T}) + E°_{T,n}, with E°_{T,n} := (F_{C,T} − F_{C,∞}(n)) + E^σ_n independent of D, is the definition at the end of the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction. At D = I it is (main-factorisation-parts), with E^∂_ξ := F_{I,C,T} − F_{C,T}; at D = D(Λ) it defines E^∂_Λ := F_{D(Λ),C,T} − F_{C,T}, bounded by (regional-square-boundary-comparison) with d_j(Λ).

**Consequence.** Exact per labelled term, on A^fin_cov, on the support of the term's indicators, under (c5-5-T)(iv) and (c5-7-T). Divide (c) by S°_Λ (S°_Λ ≠ 0 by the Kotecký–Preiss part of (d)) and use (d), (e), (g) at t = 1:

F^Λ(1) = κ_C e^{E°_Λ}·[ f_ξ(m) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅ compatible} q°_Λ(𝔅)·dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Υ^1_{W₁}U^1₁ ⋯ Υ^1_{Wₙ}U^1_n) ].

- **The bracket is a dressed loop.** Its parts are:
  - the leading word f_ξ(m) = W_χ(C;U_ξ(m)), where the shift m is the harmonic extension of the exterior data and decays away from the exterior (Lemma c5-5-1);
  - insertions Υ^1_W at pairwise disjoint intervals;
  - scalar interactions q°(𝔅), the exponential of a sum of local cluster coefficients.
- **Where n_C enters V4.** V4 does not keep the bracket. It bounds it in two pieces: the difference between t = 1 and t = 0, the exterior response B^Λ_ξ (Lemma c5-5-3(c), second form), and the t = 0 bracket through 𝔡⁰_W (proof of Prop. c5-6-def(a), steps (ii)–(iii); §3a).
  - Each family of the response is bounded by one differenced or marked slot times the undifferenced slots b̄_W := M_Λ(W) + |a^Λ_W| (Lemma c5-5-7(c),(e)).
  - Summing the undifferenced slots over compatible families gives at most ∏_W(1 + K_χ b̄_W e^{θ°(W)}) ≤ exp(Σ_W K_χ b̄_W e^{θ°(W)}).
  - This is the family-sum factor e^{2n_C ε̄_t} of (c5-5-10), improved to e^{n_C ε̄_ph} by Prop. c5-5R-4, (c5-5-9R).
  - In the response bound (c5-5-10)/(c5-5-9R), the only factor exponential in n_C is this family-sum factor, and Remark c5-5-11 attributes V4's restriction on n_C to it. Outside the response bound, n_C also enters V4 through n_C N_lat(μ'/2)e^{−μ'(b−1)/2} (Cor. c5-5-10), |R_n| ≤ e^{n_Cδ^♯_q} − 1 (Cor. main-factorisation), (c5-6-def-3), C_L S_c n_C(q + N_ret q_ϑ) (c5-3-coverage-average), and the torus conditions (c5-7-T)(iii).

## 3. The Reference Point: Υ Vanishes at Flat Data

**Claim.** Suppose the actual data of a labelled term are the reference data of Lemma c5-5-3(b): U₁ = id, V₀ = id, A = 0, Ψ = id, and exterior variable 0 on J(Λ). Then Υ^t_W = 0 for every group W and every t ∈ [0,1].

For t ∈ (0,1), Υ^t_W is read with the t-objects of Lemma c5-5-3(a), which are defined for all t ∈ [0,1]; Lemma c5-5-7 states it for t ∈ {0,1}.

**Proof.**
- **The objects are the reference ones.** The weights w^t_Λ, the root measures ν̂^{f,t}_{Λ,Z} and the words T^t_W, P^t_W are then the reference ones. Lemma c5-5-3(b) defines the reference data as "the same Ω₁, cubes, hosts D₀(Λ), constants and cutoffs" at these values. The reference row solutions are those of Lemma charts at U = id (the reference-data paragraph after V4 Lemma grouped-source); at U₁ = id the actual row solution is the same small solution, by its uniqueness in the small ball (Lemma row-locality; Lemma c2-uniform-chart(ii)).
- **The shift vanishes, and nothing depends on t.** m = 0 at these data: Lemma c5-5-3(b), "exterior variable zero on J(Λ) (so m°=0, ℓ°=0, and the reference objects are independent of t)". The linear term ℓ and any exterior inputs of primitives also vanish there, and by the previous bullet the t-independence applies to the actual objects.
- **Schur.** Hence w^t_Λ(W)∫π(T^t_W(B)(P^t_W)⁻¹) d⊗ν̂ = w°_Λ(W)∫π(P°_W(a)) d⊗ν̂°. This commutes with π(G) by the constant-gauge argument in the proof of Lemma c5-5-3(b), so by Schur's lemma for the irreducible π it is a scalar.
- **The scalar is a^Λ_W.** Its normalised trace against π(h) is a^Λ_W χ(h) by Lemma schur, so the scalar is a^Λ_W, and Υ^t_W = 0. ∎

**Literal equality.** The step "the objects are the reference ones" requires that each actual object, evaluated at these data, literally equals its reference counterpart. Examples:
- the Hessian at U₁ = id equals H° = (𝖧^{Ω₁}_{id})_{D(Λ)D(Λ)};
- f_ξ(a) = W_χ(C; e^{iq_{id}(a)}) at U₁ = id and Ψ = id.

Both hold by the definition of the reference data (the paragraph after V4 Lemma grouped-source, and Lemma c5-5-3(b)): the same regions, the same cutoffs, and a group index set that does not depend on the field (Lemma c5-5-3(a)). V4 uses this identity itself, at the carrier D(Λ), in the proof of Prop. c5-6-def, at (c5-6-def-3): "c_W(U_{W,0};h) = a^Λ_W χ(h), the t = 0 recipe being the reference data of Lemma c5-5-3(b) (the same D(Λ), Ω₁, 𝐃₀(Λ) and cutoffs) with its Schur identity".

**Consequence.** Υ^1_W is a deviation: a function of the data that vanishes at the reference point. D1.2 is the quantitative form of this.

## 3a. D1.2a: The Deviation Bound on Covered Branches

**Setting.**
- A labelled term with final region Λ and carrier D(Λ), on A^fin_cov, at t = 1.
- Any history: the far-history event 𝖭_b of (c4-2-1) is not assumed.
- V4's standing hypotheses of Prop. c5-6-def and §c4-12 are in force: the g₀ thresholds, (c5-5-T), ρ ≤ ρ_pin, and the radii τ_b and θ₀.
- Notation:
  - 𝔰(W), the size (c4-6-1); n(W), the cube count; ℋ(W), the interval;
  - Q_W, the enclosing comparison rectangle with its halos (§c4-8-4, §c4-11-4);
  - δ_Q(U) := max over plaquettes p ⊂ Q of |U(∂p) − id|_σ (Lemma c4-10-5);
  - M_Λ(W), the majorant of Lemma c5-5-7(d) with root weight ρ_t; M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W), the same with root weight ρ (proof of Prop. c5-6-def, paragraph *Parity and the source*).

**(1) Splitting.** Υ^1_W = (Υ^1_W − Υ^0_W) + Υ^0_W.
- The normalised traces against π(h) are c^1_W − c^0_W and c^0_W − a^Λ_W χ (the definition of Υ^t_W in Lemma c5-5-7).
- By Burnside (Lemma c5-5-7(a)): ‖Υ^1_W − Υ^0_W‖ ≤ K_χ 𝔡^diff_W and ‖Υ^0_W‖ ≤ K_χ 𝔡^0_W.
- Here 𝔡^0_W := sup_h |c^0_W(h) − a^Λ_W χ(h)| (proof of Prop. c5-6-def(a), step (ii)).

**(2) Exterior part.** (c5-5-8):

𝔡^diff_W ≤ M_Λ(W)·[Σ_{Z∈𝒜(W)} 𝔐_Λ(Z) + Σ_j Σ_{D∈𝔠_j} 𝔐_Λ(D) + C'_χ g₀ Σ_{b∈I_{ℋ(W)}} h(m_b)],

with 𝔐_Λ(Z) = Σ_{b∈E(Z)} 𝔪_b + Σ_{Y∈𝐃₀(Λ), Y⊆Z} 𝔪_Y (Lemma c5-5-6). Every mark sits on a bond or host of W's domains, or on a free input of a traversal of ℋ(W).

**(3) Background part, good groups.** Call W *good* if Q_W ⊂ Λ₁ and Q_W lifts injectively. Then

𝔡^0_W ≤ C_χ 𝔰(W)³ δ_{Q_W}(U₁) M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W).

*Derivation.*
- **What V4's proof uses.** V4 proves (c4-12-3) for its small interior class (c4-8-9), n(W) < b/(4C_box LM), on far histories. In the proof, the size condition and the far history serve only to give four facts:
  - Q_W ⊂ Λ₁ and Q_W outside S₁: "On a far history this box lies in Λ₁, hence in Ω₁, and outside S₁=(Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1}) ∪ R₁" (§c4-8-4). §c4-8-1 requires a box "lying in Λ₁ outside S₁" for the local-data comparison, and §c4-6-3 interpolates only boxes "contained in Λ₁ and disjoint from S₁";
  - the injective lift (§c4-11-4);
  - δ_{Q_W}(U₁) ≤ Cε₁, which V4 derives from Q_W ⊂ Λ₁ alone (§c4-11-5: each plaquette of Q_W has a vertex x ∈ Λ₁ and hence a bond of I within two units, and Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation applies).
- **"Outside S₁" follows from Q_W ⊂ Λ₁.** CRE p.251 line 5: Λ₁ = Ω₁^{∼−2} ∩ (R₁′^∼)ᶜ and S₁ = (Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1}) ∪ R₁, with R₁′ "the union of LMR₁-cubes intersecting the set R₁" (line 3).
  - R₁ ⊆ R₁′ ⊆ R₁′^∼, so Λ₁ ∩ R₁ = ∅.
  - Ω₁^{∼−2} ⊆ Ω₁^{∼−1}, since shrinking is monotone, so Λ₁ ∩ (Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1}) = ∅.
  - V4's Λ₁ is the filled region. The filling (CRE p.251 lines 9–13) replaces small components of Λ₁ᶜ "by the smallest rectangular parallelepipeds containing them". So it only shrinks Λ₁, and the two points above still apply.
  - Hence Λ₁ ∩ S₁ = ∅ on every branch, and Q_W ⊂ Λ₁ places Q_W outside S₁.
- **Where V4 imposes these facts.** §c4-8-4, §c4-8-1, §c4-6-3, §c4-9-2 (the lift), §c4-11-3, §c4-11-4 (the lifting argument), §c4-11-5 and §c4-12-2. At D(Λ), V4 states that "the small interior groups (c4-8-9), the rectangle Q_W, its injective lift (Section c4-11-4) and, on N_b^c, the inclusion of Q_W in Λ₁ outside S₁ (Section c4-8-4) concern the group's domains, the contour and Λ₁, not the carrier" (proof of Prop. c5-6-def, paragraph *Locality, covariance and endpoints*).
- **The rest of the path argument uses neither size nor history.** The Hessian bound (c4-7-7) needs only ‖B‖_∞ ≤ τ_b (Lemma c4-7-3; at D(Λ) by min–max, proof of Prop. c5-6-def, paragraph *Hessian along the coefficient path*). The chart condition along the path follows from the phase test alone (paragraph *Charts and primitives* there). §c4-9-1 and Lemma c4-9-1 use the box Q_W of a good group, the phase test and Lemma c4-7-3.
  - Lemma c4-9-2, §c4-10-1 to §c4-10-4, §c4-11-1 and §c4-11-2 use the fixed charts, the walk parameters, the Gaussian coercivity and the covariance identities, whose constants are "independent of the volume, the history and g₀" (§c4-10-1). Lemma c4-10-5 needs only an injectively lifted rectangle, and "covers growing good boxes without imposing their smallness as a hypothesis". §c4-11-3 uses (c4-8-9) only through the diameter bound, the lift and, on far histories, Q_W ⊂ Λ₁.
- **Phase test passed** (C_tg diam(Q_W)² δ_{Q_W}(U₁) ≤ τ_b/2).
  - The path is U_{W,t} = e^{itB_W} in the rooted tree gauge on Q_W, with ‖B_W‖_∞ ≤ C_tg diam(Q_W)² δ_{Q_W}(U₁) ((c4-9-1), (c4-10-17)).
  - V4 supplies, at D(Λ), the endpoint identities (c4-11-6) (paragraph *Locality, covariance and endpoints*) and the derivative bound |𝒟_ω c_W(U_{W,t};h)| ≤ C_χ‖ω‖_∞(n(W) + |ℋ(W)|)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) along the path (paragraph *Parity and the source*, the D(Λ) form of (c4-12-2)).
  - Together, by (c4-9-2): 𝔡^0_W ≤ C_χ‖B_W‖_∞(n(W) + |ℋ(W)|)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W).
  - With diam(Q_W) ≤ C_box LM n(W) ≤ C_box LM𝔰(W) (§c4-8-4, for a liftable rectangle) and n(W) + |ℋ(W)| ≤ CLM𝔰(W) (§c4-12-2), and LM fixed, this is ≤ C_χ𝔰³δ_{Q_W}M^{(ρ)}_Λ.
- **Phase test failed.**
  - Then 1 < (2C_tg/τ_b)diam(Q_W)²δ_{Q_W}(U₁).
  - The endpoint majorants |c^0_W(h)| ≤ M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) and |a^Λ_W| ≤ M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) (paragraph *Parity and the source*) give 𝔡^0_W ≤ 2M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) ≤ (4C_tg/τ_b)(C_box LM)²𝔰²δ_{Q_W}M^{(ρ)}_Λ, which is ≤ C𝔰³δ_{Q_W}M^{(ρ)}_Λ since 𝔰 ≥ 1.
- **Difference from V4's (c4-12-3).** δ_{Q_W}(U₁) is kept, not replaced by its bound Cε₁, and the class is "good groups, of any size, on any history" in place of "small interior groups on far histories".

**(4) Background part, other groups.** If W is not good, then 𝔡^0_W ≤ 2M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W), by the endpoint majorants ((c4-12-4); at D(Λ), paragraph *Parity and the source*), which hold on A^fin_cov on every history. Moreover one of the following holds.
- **(i) Q_W lifts injectively and Q_W ⊄ Λ₁.** The diameter bound below is for a liftable rectangle (§c4-8-4).
  - Write B_ξ = Λ₁ᶜ. For a traversal n put d(n) := dist_{d_T}(Γ_n, E(B_ξ)), with Γ_n the bonds and input templates of n (as in Γ_C, §c4-2 and §c4-9-2).
  - Q_W contains the bonds and stencils of every n ∈ ℋ(W) (the enlargement of §c4-11-4), and it contains a vertex x of B_ξ; a bond with initial site x lies in E(B_ξ).
  - So d(n) ≤ diam(Q_W) + 1 ≤ C_box LM𝔰(W) + 1, i.e. 𝔰(W) ≥ (d(n) − 1)/(C_box LM) for every n ∈ ℋ(W).
- **(ii) Q_W does not lift.** Then n(W) ≥ N_min/(C LM), so 𝔰(W) ≥ N_min/(C LM). This is the contrapositive of the two lifting conditions of §c4-11-4, C_lift LMn(W) < N_min/4 (the spanning traversal) and 2C_box LMn(W) < N_min (the rectangle), so C = max(4C_lift, 2C_box) ≤ 4C_box, since "C_box is chosen to include these constants" (§c4-11-4).

On 𝖭_b^c this recovers V4's class (c4-12-4), s ≥ T_b = b/(4C_box LM): there d(n) ≥ b + 1, since d_T takes integer values, so (i) gives 𝔰 ≥ b/(C_box LM); and (ii) gives 𝔰 ≥ N_min/(4C_box LM) > 8b/(C_box LM), because N_min > 32b (c4-9-3).

**(5) Gauge.**
- δ_Q(U) is invariant under site gauges, since each U(∂p) is conjugated.
- V4 compares with the reference in the rooted tree gauge on Q_W, using the covariance of the coefficient (§c4-8-1, §c4-11; at D(Λ), paragraph *Locality, covariance and endpoints*). The data are not gauge-fixed.
- So the deviation of a good group is gauge-invariant. For a group that is not good, no deviation is claimed.
- *The matrix coefficient.* Put X_W := Υ^1_W + a^Λ_W·id = w^1_Λ(W)∫π(T^1_W(B)(P^1_W)⁻¹) d⊗ν̂^{f,1}_{Λ}, based at the initial site o of ℋ(W), and X^0_W := Υ^0_W + a^Λ_W·id, so that c^0_W(h) = dim(π)⁻¹tr(X^0_Wπ(h)). V4 (c4-11-5), c_W(U^g; g(o)hg(o)⁻¹) = c_W(U;h), holds at D(Λ) (paragraph *Locality, covariance and endpoints*). It says tr([π(g(o))⁻¹X^0_W(U^g)π(g(o)) − X^0_W(U)]π(h)) = 0 for every h, so by Burnside (Lemma c5-5-7(a)) X^0_W(U^g) = π(g(o))X^0_W(U)π(g(o))⁻¹. The covariance of X_W at t = 1, with the exterior data transformed, is map #MD2.2.4.8–#MD2.2.4.11.

**(6) Per-traversal form.** For every traversal n ∈ ℐ_C:

Σ_{W: n∈ℋ(W)} K_χ e^{θ°(W)} 𝔡^0_W ≤ C_χ ρ [δ*_n(U₁) + e^{−c(d(n)−1)/(LM)} + e^{−cN_min/(LM)}],

where:
- δ*_n(U) := Σ_{k≥1} (k+1)³ e^{−(31/8)ηk} Δ_n(C_box LM(k+1));
- Δ_n(R) := max{δ_p(U) : p ⊂ Λ₁ a plaquette within distance R of n};
- c := 31η/(8 max(C_box, C)).

*Proof.*
- **Weights.** e^{θ°(W)} ≤ e^{η𝔰(W)/8} (proof of Prop. c5-6-def(a), step (i)). The contour pin (c4-6-5), at D(Λ) with root weight ρ by Lemma c5-5-8(a) (paragraph *Pins* of that proof), gives Σ_{W∋n} M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W)e^{4η𝔰(W)} ≤ Cρ.
- **Good groups.**
  - Q_W lies in Λ₁ within diam(Q_W) ≤ C_box LM𝔰 of n, so δ_{Q_W} ≤ Δ_n(C_box LM(⌊𝔰⌋+1)).
  - Split by k = ⌊𝔰⌋ and use 𝔰³e^{η𝔰/8} ≤ (k+1)³e^{η/8}e^{−(31/8)ηk}e^{4η𝔰}.
- **Other groups.**
  - 𝔰 ≥ s_n := min((d(n) − 1)/(C_box LM), N_min/(C LM)).
  - So e^{η𝔰/8} ≤ e^{−(31/8)ηs_n}e^{4η𝔰}.
- **Constants.** These are V4's constants of §c4-12, which may depend on the group, the character, L, M and the fixed expansion and chart choices, "but not on the coupling, the square length, the compatible torus size or the history" (§c4-12).

*Count check.*
- δ_p(U₁) ≤ Cε₁ for p ⊂ Λ₁ (§c4-11-5), so δ*_n ≤ C_ηε₁.
- On 𝖭_b^c, d(n) ≥ b + 1 for every n, and N_min > 32b. Summing over the n_C traversals overcounts each group |ℋ(W)| ≥ 1 times, which is allowed in an upper bound.
- The sum is at most C_χ n_C ρ(ε₁ + e^{−cb/(LM)}). This is V4's (c5-6-def-3) up to the value of the constant c (V4 has c = η/(2C_box), from e^{−2ηT_b}).
- Per traversal, the rate is ρ·(local curvature) ≤ Cρε₁ inside Λ₁.
- The whole-contour groups sit inside every pin. Their total is at most Cρn_C e^{−25ηn_C/(LM)} ((c4-8-8), Lemma c4-8-2(c), a step of the summation of Lemma c4-6-2, which holds at D(Λ) by Lemma c5-5-8(a)).

**(7) Exterior marks per traversal.**
- **Envelope decay.** A group with n ∈ ℋ(W) and a cube Δ ∈ F(W) has σ(W) ≥ dist(Δ, n)/(C_F LM). This is the envelope argument of Lemma c5-5-8(b): the envelope 𝔈(W) ⊇ F(W) ∪ π₁(ℋ(W)) is connected, contains Δ and a cube containing a vertex of n, and has ℓ^∞ diameter ≤ A_E LM σ(W).
- **Marks per cube.** Fix an assignment of each bond mark to the LM-cube containing the bond's initial site, and of each host mark 𝔪_Y to the cube containing a chosen site of Y. If b ∈ E(Z) or Y ⊆ Z, the assigned cube is a cube of Z. Write 𝔪̂_Δ for the sum of the marks assigned to Δ; then 𝔐_Λ(Z) ≤ Σ_{Δ⊆Z} 𝔪̂_Δ. The number of domain occurrences in W is at most σ(W) (Lemma c5-5-8), and their cubes lie in F(W), so the first two mark sums of (2) are at most σ(W)Σ_{Δ∈F(W)}𝔪̂_Δ.
- **Result.** With θ°(W) ≤ η𝔰(W)/8, σ e^{ησ/8} ≤ η⁻¹e^{4ησ − ησ}, and the pins of Lemma c5-5-8(a):

  Σ_{W∋n} K_χ e^{θ°(W)} 𝔡^diff_W ≤ Cρ_t [Σ_Δ 𝔪̂_Δ e^{−η dist(Δ,n)/(C_F LM)} + C'_χ g₀ Σ_b h(m_b) e^{−η dist(b,n)/(C_F LM)}].

  The last sum runs over the free inputs b of the traversals of ℋ(W), whose cubes lie in F(W) ⊆ 𝔈(W), so σ(W) ≥ dist(b, n)/(C_F LM) for them as well. The first sum uses dist(Δ, n) and the envelope decay, and both use Σ_{W∋n} M_Λ(W)e^{4ησ(W)} ≤ C_pinρ_t.

**(8) What D1(ii) Has.** On covered branches, for every traversal n, Σ_{W∋n} e^{θ°(W)}‖Υ^1_W‖ is at most the sum of (6) and (7):
- a gauge-invariant local curvature term C_χρδ*_n(U₁), which is ≤ Cρε₁ inside Λ₁;
- a term attached to the large-field region B_ξ, of size Cρ, decaying from B_ξ at rate c/(LM);
- a wrapping term e^{−cN_min/(LM)}, which is already ≤ e^{−2cR₁} by N_min ≥ 2LMR₁ (c4-9-3), on any compatible torus;
- exterior marks attached to the collar data, decaying from them. A mark 𝔪_Y is a fixed constant when a primitive has an input in J(Λ) (Lemma c5-5-6), so this term does not vanish at the reference point.

The second and fourth terms are not small, and they carry no deviation: they are attached to B_ξ and to the collar data. So §3a gives the deviation form of D1(ii) only for the curvature term, and D1(ii) is stated in §4 in this per-traversal form.

## 3b. D1.3: The Carrier Part as a Local Sum

**What is to be shown.** Let D be a covered carrier: I, or D(Λ) on a labelled term. (Uncovered carriers: §3d(2)(h).) Write E^∂_D := F_{D,C,T} − F_{C,T}.
- At I this is the split of Cor. main-factorisation, (main-factorisation-parts).
- At D(Λ), Cor. c5-7-2 sets E°_Λ := E°_{D(Λ),C,T}. V4 defines E°_{D,C_n,T} := (F_{D,C,T} − F_{C,T}) + (F_{C,T} − F_{C,∞}(n)) + E^σ_n for every carrier D (the end of the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). The last two terms are E°_{T,n} (main-factorisation-parts). So E°_Λ = E^∂_{D(Λ)} + E°_{T,n} by these definitions alone.

The claim:
- E^∂_D = Σ_j Σ_{X∈𝒳_j} e_{j,X}. Here j runs over the 4n coarse segments and 𝒳_j is a family of finite bond sets;
- each X ∈ 𝒳_j contains a bond of Dᶜ and meets the free-input stencil K_j;
- e_{j,X} depends on D only through D ∩ X;
- *(anchored bound)* for every j, Σ_{X∈𝒳_j} |e_{j,X}| e^{2a·diam X} ≤ Cτ² e^{−c''d_j};
- *(bound near a segment)* with e_X := Σ_j e_{j,X}, for every j, Σ_{X: d_T(X,K_j) ≤ R_0} |e_X| e^{a·diam X} ≤ Cτ² e^{−c'd_j}.

Here a, c'', c' > 0 and C are fixed before the coupling, and d_j is as in (regional-square-distances).

V4 proves only |E^∂_D| ≤ Cτ²Σ_j e^{−cd_j} ((regional-square-boundary-comparison)). That bound does not say how E^∂ depends on D; the decomposition below does, and §3i gives its thin form.

**V4's Structure.**
- *(Q1) The expansion at a covered carrier.* "Apply the protected-label expansion with supports intersected with D, keeping every original label" (proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). "The estimates for the defined flat integral use its covered stencils, spectral bounds and small-coupling conditions" (the statement of that proposition), and "the required quantitative bounds hold uniformly for these restricted labels" (its proof). So F_{D,C,T} = Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_D} φ_D(Γ), over the source clusters 𝒞_D of carrier D, absolutely convergent by the rooted majorant (regional-square-rooted-majorant): Σ_{Γ: j∈J_Γ} U(Γ)e^{η'ℓ(Γ)} ≤ Cτ².
- *(Q2) The flat data.* The scalar is taken at the conditional reference data: "the background and exterior determining fields are the identity", and "the row precision entries are the flat torus entries on D" (proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). (regional-square-scalar) and (regional-periodic-weight) define S°_{D,C_n,T} by a formula in D, C_n, T, g₀ and σ. So F_{D,C,T} is a function of the set D alone, and no physical field enters.
  - At a carrier D' ⊇ D other than D(Λ) or I, §3b uses only this formula, the per-cluster bound |φ_{D'}(Γ)| ≤ U(Γ) and (1) below. These rest on the uniform bounds for restricted labels and the derivative bounds of the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction, which are generic in the carrier. When D is covered, every free-input stencil lies in D'. At an uncovered D this is not used (§3d(2)(g), (h)).
  - *At D = T*, the torus free carrier E_{*,T}: i_{T,T} is the identity, so C_{T,T} = H_T⁻¹. P_{T,T} is the sum of all periodic primitives, and Θ_T^σ is the full bond product: (regional-weight) defines Θ_D^σ(B) = ∏_{b∈D}1{|B_b|_σ < r}. V4 defines F_{C,T} := log(N^σ_{C_n,T}/D^σ_T) (first line of the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). By Lemma identity-primitives (the primitives agree with the local terms "label by label", and "the cutoff is the same product of bond tests"), the ratio (regional-square-scalar) at D = T is N^σ_{C_n,T}/D^σ_T of (marginal-literal-ratio). So F_{C,T} = F_{T,C,T} = Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_T} φ_T(Γ), by (Q1) at D = T.
- *(Q3) Interpolation.* "On D, interpolate between (C_∞)_{D,D} and C_D" (proof of Prop. regional-coefficient; at T, the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction, "Interpolate the principal covariances as in (regional-density-derivative)").
  - Spectrum: a principal block of H_T has spectrum in [γ_L, h_L] by the Rayleigh quotient, so its inverse has spectrum in [h_L⁻¹, γ_L⁻¹]. Principal blocks of these inverses and their convex combinations stay in that interval.
  - Decay: "Principal restriction and inversion preserve the spectral bounds, and the finite-range Neumann series gives uniform exponential covariance decay in the periodic metric" (proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction).
  - The derivative: h_A := e^{−a₀ d_T(A,Dᶜ)} on a carrier A of size k. "Summing the centre and edge derivatives costs at most C(k+|F|+1)²h_A", and "the product rule over repeated object occurrences" gives the cluster bound (both in that proof).
  - A cluster activity depends on the carrier only through its restricted labels and the covariance restricted to its carrier. V4 uses this for its own D-versus-T comparison: short clusters have "identical labels and cyclic intervals in both expansions … Their covariance derivative is bounded by the preceding estimate" (the proof of (regional-square-boundary-comparison)).
- *(Q4) The covariance comparison.* The periodic resolvent identity C_{D,T} − (C_T)_{D,D} = C_{D,T}(H_T)_{D,Dᶜ}(C_T)_{Dᶜ,D} gives ‖C_{D,T}(b,c) − C_T(b,c)‖ ≤ Ce^{−a₀(d_T(b,Dᶜ)+d_T(c,Dᶜ)+d_T(b,c))} (proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction, *the boundary comparison*).
- *Notation.* a₀ is V4's exponent a of (Q3) and (Q4); the letter a is the new weight of (4). R_* bounds "the label diameters and source-to-stencil distances", and R_0 = 4(1+R_*+R_H+L) "includes the label, reconstruction and precision ranges" (the paragraphs before (regional-distance) and after (regional-square-distances)). The metric d(b,c) = ‖x−y‖_∞ + 𝟏{i≠k} (before (regional-distance)) is integer-valued, with d(b,c) ≥ 1 for b ≠ c.

**(1) Pairs of carriers.** Let D₂ ⊆ D₁ be carriers and E := D₁ ∖ D₂. The covariance statements below need no coverage. The cluster comparison at the end of (1) is made for clusters Γ with E ∩ supp Γ = ∅, supp Γ as in (2); this holds for every use in (3).
- The block identity H₁H₁⁻¹ = id on D₂ × D₂, with H₁ := (H_T)_{D₁D₁}, gives (H_T)_{D₂D₂}(C_{D₁})_{D₂D₂} + (H_T)_{D₂E}(C_{D₁})_{ED₂} = I_{D₂}. Multiplying on the left by C_{D₂} gives C_{D₂} − (C_{D₁})_{D₂D₂} = C_{D₂}(H_T)_{D₂E}(C_{D₁})_{ED₂}.
- This is (Q4) with T replaced by D₁ and Dᶜ by E. Both inverses are principal-restriction inverses, so they have the spectrum and the uniform decay of (Q3). Neither needs coverage.
- The convolution argument of (Q4) gives ‖C_{D₂}(b,c) − C_{D₁}(b,c)‖ ≤ Ce^{−a₀(d_T(b,E)+d_T(c,E)+d_T(b,c))} for b, c ∈ D₂.
  - Take u ∈ D₂ and w ∈ E with d_T(u,w) ≤ R_H, and s := d_T(b,u) + d_T(w,c).
  - Then d_T(b,E) ≤ d_T(b,u) + R_H, d_T(c,E) ≤ d_T(c,w) and d_T(b,c) ≤ s + R_H. The sum is at most 2s + 2R_H.
  - Only w ∈ E is used. The shell count is carrier-independent: "Periodic shells have at most the infinite-lattice shell counts, so this sum is uniform even for wrapping or disconnected D" (*the boundary comparison*).
- *Same index.* If E ∩ supp Γ = ∅, then every label support and every marked stencil of Γ meets D₁ and D₂ in the same set. So the restricted labels coincide, and so do the link functions of the marked traversals: the link of r at i_{D,T}B depends on B through the stencil of r intersected with D only. Hence Γ ∈ 𝒞_{D₂} iff Γ ∈ 𝒞_{D₁}, with the same index, and only the covariance changes.
- Interpolating on D₂ between (C_{D₁})_{D₂D₂} and C_{D₂}, as in (Q3), gives for such a cluster Γ ∈ 𝒞_{D₂} ∩ 𝒞_{D₁}:

  |φ_{D₁}(Γ) − φ_{D₂}(Γ)| ≤ C(|Γ| + 1)² e^{−a₀·d_T(A_Γ, E)} U(Γ).

  *One U(Γ) for all carriers.* U(Γ) is the pinned-tree majorant of (regional-square-rooted-majorant), "the absolute Ursell coefficient times the positive object majorants". It is built from the web-tree majorants u_p, which are functions of the restricted labels and of constants common to all carriers: s_*, q_*, R_* ("Restriction preserves the support-size, overlap and range bounds"), K_2, and the edge majorant B_{bc} = A_0e^{−μ_0d_T(b,c)} (all in the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). So U(Γ) is a function of the index of Γ, which is common to every carrier used in (1) and (3).

  Here A_Γ is the carrier of Γ, and |Γ| := Σ_γ (k_γ + |F_γ| + 1), summed over the component occurrences γ of Γ with multiplicity, with k_γ = |A_γ|. Each occurrence costs C(k_γ+|F_γ|+1)²h_{A_γ} ((Q3) with E in place of Dᶜ). The product rule over repeated occurrences sums these, and Σ_γ(k_γ+|F_γ|+1)² ≤ (|Γ|+1)².
- *|Γ| against ℓ(Γ).* ℓ(γ) = |A_γ| + Σ_{F_γ} d_T(b,c) ≥ k_γ + |F_γ| ((marginal-extent), d ≥ 1), and ℓ(γ) ≥ 1. So k_γ + |F_γ| + 1 ≤ 2ℓ(γ) and |Γ| ≤ 2ℓ(Γ).

**(2) Three classes of clusters.** Write supp Γ for the union of the *original* supports of the labels of Γ and of the free-input stencils of the traversals marked by Γ. Each label support has diameter at most R_*, and each stencil lies within R_* of its source label ("R_* bound[s] the label diameters and source-to-stencil distances", before (regional-distance)).
- *Stencil reach.* Each free-input bond of a traversal r lies within ℓ^∞ distance 2L+1 of r: the forward influence set ℛ_b lies within 2L+1 of b (V4 Section c5-5-0, the definition of I_C). So it lies within c_st := 2L+2 of r in the metric d, and a traversal stencil has diameter at most 2c_st. c_st depends on L only. Put R_K := R_* + 2c_st. Then every point of a marked stencil is within R_K of its source label, whether the "source-to-stencil distance" is read to the traversal bond or to the nearest stencil bond.
- *Support near the carrier.* With label diameters ≤ R_*, every point of supp Γ lies within c_supp := R_* + R_K of the carrier A_Γ, which the network joins ("A network joins its carrier, stencil edges and contour intervals", proof of (regional-square-boundary-comparison)).
- *Which links and labels enter.* Only marked traversals enter a cluster: in (regional-wilson-polynomial) 𝒢_S varies only the links of S and uses the background link U₁ = id elsewhere at the reference data. A cluster of 𝒞_{D'} has only labels whose original support meets D', since primitives whose support misses D' are not in P_{D',T} (regional-periodic-weight). Call Γ *D-interior* if supp Γ ⊆ D.
- A D-interior cluster has the same index (labels, forest, web interval) in 𝒞_D and in 𝒞_T. Its restricted labels are the original ones, its marked links are the torus links (their stencils lie in D), and the grouping ("the manuscript's shortest cyclic intervals, with its fixed tie-breaking base", proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction) is geometric.
- With F_{C,T} = Σ_{𝒞_T} φ_T(Γ) from (Q2), and absolute convergence from (Q1), this gives the exact identity

  E^∂_D = Σ_{Γ D-interior} [φ_D(Γ) − φ_T(Γ)] + Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_D, not D-interior} φ_D(Γ) − Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_T, not D-interior} φ_T(Γ).
- In the last two sums supp Γ contains a bond of Dᶜ.

**(3) Localising the dependence on D.** Fix Γ. Put B_m := {bonds b with d_T(b, supp Γ) < m}, so B_1 = supp Γ, and D^{(m)} := (D ∩ B_m) ∪ (T ∖ B_m).
- D^{(m+1)} ⊆ D^{(m)}, and D^{(m)} ⊇ D.
- Γ is D^{(m)}-interior whenever it is D-interior.
- φ_{D^{(m)}}(Γ) depends on D only through D ∩ B_m.
- D^{(m)} = T for m ≤ m₀ := d_T(supp Γ, Dᶜ), and D^{(m)} = D once B_m covers the torus.
- D^{(m)} ∖ D^{(m+1)} = Dᶜ ∩ (B_{m+1} ∖ B_m), the bonds of Dᶜ at distance exactly m from supp Γ.

*Interior clusters.* Telescoping,

φ_D(Γ) − φ_T(Γ) = Σ_{m≥m₀} [φ_{D^{(m+1)}}(Γ) − φ_{D^{(m)}}(Γ)].
- The m-th term depends on D only through D ∩ B_{m+1}.
- It vanishes unless Dᶜ meets B_{m+1} ∖ B_m: otherwise the two carriers coincide.
- Apply (1) with D₁ = D^{(m)}, D₂ = D^{(m+1)} and E = Dᶜ ∩ (B_{m+1} ∖ B_m). Here m ≥ m₀ ≥ 1, so E ∩ supp Γ = ∅, as (1) requires. Since A_Γ ⊆ supp Γ, d_T(A_Γ, E) ≥ m, so the term is at most C(|Γ|+1)²e^{−a₀m}U(Γ).
- Put X := B_{m+1}, which contains supp Γ and a bond of Dᶜ.

*Other clusters.*
- Γ ∈ 𝒞_T, not D-interior: φ_T(Γ) does not depend on D. Only the indicator "Γ is not D-interior" does, and it is a function of D ∩ supp Γ. Put X := supp Γ.
- Γ ∈ 𝒞_D, not D-interior. Its index, restricted labels and marked links depend on D ∩ supp Γ only. For m ≥ 1, D^{(m)} ∩ supp Γ = D ∩ supp Γ, so Γ ∈ 𝒞_{D^{(m)}} with the same index. Telescope from m = 1:

  φ_D(Γ) = φ_{D^{(1)}}(Γ) + Σ_{m≥1}[φ_{D^{(m+1)}}(Γ) − φ_{D^{(m)}}(Γ)].
  - The first term depends on D ∩ supp Γ, with X := supp Γ, and is at most U(Γ).
  - The m-th term obeys the bound of the interior case, with X := B_{m+1}.

**(4) Summation.**
- *Anchoring.* As in the proof of (regional-square-boundary-comparison), allocate each source cluster Γ to its marked segments j ∈ J_Γ with weight |J_Γ|⁻¹. Index the terms of (3) by (Γ, type, m): type "supp" (X = supp Γ, no m, for the clusters that are not D-interior) or type "shell" (X = B_{m+1}, kept only when Dᶜ meets B_{m+1} ∖ B_m). 𝒳_j is the set of X produced from the clusters with j ∈ J_Γ, and e_{j,X} := Σ |J_Γ|⁻¹ t_{Γ,type,m}, over the indices with j ∈ J_Γ that produce X. Here t is the term of (3), with a sign.
  - Every source cluster has J_Γ ≠ ∅, so E^∂_D = Σ_j Σ_{X∈𝒳_j} e_{j,X}. Each kept X contains a bond of Dᶜ, so e_{j,X} = 0 whenever X ⊆ D.
- *Position.* supp Γ contains the stencil of the marked traversal in segment j, which lies in K_j. So each X ⊇ supp Γ meets K_j.
- *Diameters.* Every label of Γ meets the carrier, and "a network joins its carrier, stencil edges and contour intervals with length at most C_net ℓ(Γ)+R_0" (proof of (regional-square-boundary-comparison)). Every point of supp Γ is within c_supp = R_* + R_K of the carrier ((2)). So diam(supp Γ) ≤ C_net ℓ(Γ) + R_0 + 2R_* + 2R_K, and diam X ≤ C_net ℓ(Γ) + R_0 + 2R_* + 2R_K + 2m for X = B_{m+1}. R_K = R_* + 2(2L+2) is fixed before the coupling ((2)).
- *Weights.* Take a ≤ min(a₀/8, η'/(8C_net)). Then e^{2a·diam X} ≤ Ce^{(η'/4)ℓ(Γ)}e^{a₀m/2}. By |Γ| ≤ 2ℓ(Γ) (from (1)), (|Γ|+1)² ≤ (2ℓ(Γ)+1)² ≤ C_{η'}e^{(η'/4)ℓ(Γ)}. So

  Σ_{X∈𝒳_j} |e_{j,X}| e^{2a·diam X} ≤ C Σ_{Γ: j∈J_Γ} U(Γ) e^{(η'/2)ℓ(Γ)} [e^{−(a₀/2)m₀(Γ)} + 𝟏{Γ not D-interior}].

  The bracket comes from the geometric sum over m ≥ m₀ (interior clusters) or m ≥ 1 (the others), plus the terms with X = supp Γ.
- *Decay in d_j (V4's split at ℓ = d_j/(2C_net), proof of (regional-square-boundary-comparison)).* Put c'' := min(a₀/4, η'/(4C_net)).
  - From the position, d_T(K_j, Dᶜ) ≤ diam(supp Γ) + m₀(Γ). With d_j ≤ d_T(K_j, Dᶜ) − R_0 when d_j > 0, this gives m₀(Γ) ≥ d_j − C_net ℓ(Γ) − 2R_* − 2R_K.
  - Short clusters, ℓ(Γ) < d_j/(2C_net), with d_j > 4(R_* + R_K): m₀ ≥ d_j/2 − 2(R_* + R_K) > 0. So Γ is D-interior, and its term is at most CU(Γ)e^{(η'/2)ℓ(Γ)}e^{−a₀d_j/4}.
  - Long clusters, ℓ(Γ) ≥ d_j/(2C_net): e^{(η'/2)ℓ(Γ)} ≤ e^{η'ℓ(Γ)}e^{−η'd_j/(4C_net)}.
  - Non-interior clusters have m₀ = 0, so they are long when d_j > 4(R_* + R_K).
  - Summing with the rooted majorant (regional-square-rooted-majorant) at D and at T, Σ_{Γ: j∈J_Γ} U(Γ)e^{η'ℓ(Γ)} ≤ Cτ², gives the anchored bound Cτ²e^{−c''d_j}. Bounded d_j ≤ 4(R_* + R_K) are covered by enlarging C. At an uncovered D the rooted majorant at D holds by §3d(2)(g).
- *Near a segment.* An X ∈ 𝒳_{j'} meets K_{j'} (*Position*). So if d_T(X, K_j) ≤ R_0, then dist(K_j, K_{j'}) ≤ R_0 + diam X, and e^{a·diam X} ≤ e^{aR_0}e^{2a·diam X}e^{−a·dist(K_j,K_{j'})}.
  - d_j ≤ d_{j'} + dist(K_j, K_{j'}) + diam K_{j'}. K_{j'} is the union of the free-input stencils of the contour bonds of the coarse segment j′, which has length L, so diam K_{j'} ≤ L + 2c_st is fixed.
  - Put c' := min(c'', a/2). Then Σ_{X: d_T(X,K_j) ≤ R_0} |e_X|e^{a·diam X} ≤ Cτ² Σ_{j'} e^{−c''d_{j'}}e^{−a·dist(K_j,K_{j'})} ≤ Cτ²e^{−c'd_j} Σ_{j'} e^{−(a/2)dist(K_j,K_{j'})}.
  - Each point of K_{j'} lies within c_st of a bond of segment j′, and distinct segments contain distinct contour bonds. Fix a bond b_j of segment j; every point of K_j lies within L + c_st of b_j. So if dist(K_j, K_{j'}) < r, some bond of segment j′ lies within r + L + 2c_st of b_j, and the number of such j′ is at most the number of bonds within r + L + 2c_st of b_j. Periodic shells are at most the infinite-lattice shells, so that number is at most C(1+r)⁴. The last sum is therefore bounded uniformly in n and the torus.

**(5) Scope of the Decomposition.**
- E^∂_D is an exact sum of activities attached to Dᶜ. Each depends on D only inside its own set X, and each decays from the contour at the rate c' = min(c'', a/2) of (4), not V4's rate c. This meets the diameter-weighted clause of the class 𝔇₁ (§4) for the carrier part of E.
- *Shape.* The sets X = B_{m+1} are balls around supp Γ: |X| ≍ m⁴ bonds, while the terms that produce them decay only like e^{−a₀m}, so these estimates give a diameter weight and no weight in |X|. The thin form is §3i.
- *Check.* Summing the anchored bound over j gives |E^∂_D| ≤ Cτ²Σ_j e^{−c''d_j}. This is V4's bound (regional-square-boundary-comparison), with c'' in place of c.
- *The carrier.* At a labelled term the carrier is D(Λ), which depends on the history and the labels.
  - The decomposition is local in D(Λ).
  - D(Λ) is built by the retreat of §c5-3, not by Lemma c5-6-2. Lemma c5-6-2 compares activities at D(Λ) and at I for domains with no cube of 𝒩_lab; it belongs to §3g(1), in its "marked" form (Remark c5-6-5: "what is local is the marking, not the domain").
  - Position: every site of (Λ₁^{(0)})ᶜ is within 100ℓ of CRE's unfilled bad region (Lemma c5-3-0(b)). Every site of Λ₁^{(0)} ∖ Λ₁^fin is within 103ℓ of x_e for a bond e found bad, and at most N⁺_ret = 4(209ℓ)⁴ bonds are removed on account of one bad bond (Lemma c5-3-1(c), (d) with 𝒬⁺ in place of 𝒬(e), §3c(4⁗)(c)).
  - D(Λ) ∩ Y is not determined by the history and labels near Y: Fill acts on components inside cubes of side 100ℓ, but its fixed point and the rounds are defined globally. The later steps use only the polymer form of the label sum (§3g(2)).
- *Not covered by §3b.*
  - E°_{T,n}, which depends only on n and the torus (Cor. main-factorisation) and is a constant of the contour.
  - The carrier dependence of the group activities a^Λ_W and q°, through H°, 𝖫_Z, 𝐃₀(Λ), E₀ and C_{E₀} (Remark c5-5-11(1)): §3g(1) for the reference species and §3j for the t > 0 species.
- *V-independence.* By (Q2), E^∂_{D(Λ)} is a number on each labelled term. It needs no analytic continuation in V (D1.2b).

## 3c. D1.0: The Assembly at Step 1, with the Choice (P)

*Setting.* The first step. The branch data are ξ = (Ω₁, R₁, V, V₀, A_e) (V4 Section branch-data). The outer operation is 𝒪 of (native-source-explicit-outer-operation). The branch weight is 𝗐_ξ = ∫_{𝔤^I} ρ_ξ da_old, and the conditional loop is F_ξ(1) = 𝒥^a_ξ[f_ξ]/𝒥^a_ξ[1] with f_ξ = W_χ(C;U_ξ(a)) (V4 Section c5-6, first paragraph; (c-exterior-response)). This section treats the covered labelled terms; the uncovered ones are §3d.

**(1) At Step 1, CRE's ρ₁ Is Tρ₀ by Equalities.**
- CRE (0.1), p.243: (Tρ)(V) = ∫dU δ(ŪV⁻¹)ρ(U).
- The first-step section rewrites Tρ₀ by equalities:
  - (1.6), p.247, and (1.11), p.248, each introduced by "This yields the equality";
  - (1.15), p.249 ("After these operations we obtain"), and the decomposition of unity (1.18), p.250;
  - the change of variables (1.22), p.251, and its Jacobian (1.24), p.252: "After the change of variables we obtain an expansion of the form (1.15)";
  - (1.26), p.253: "The above equalities define the functions V^{(0)} and E^{(1)}";
  - the renormalisation (1.27), p.253, and the collection of all constants into E₁: "Thus E₁ depends on Ω₁, Λ₁" (p.254).
  - Three steps are Bałaban's statements, accepted: dropping χ₀, χ_Ax on p.248 "if A₀ is sufficiently larger than A₁" (V4 (B3)(i)); omitting the second factor of (1.19) on the cubes of Ω₁^{∼−1}∖R₁ (p.250: "Thus the second function in the product (1.19) is equal to 1, and we can omit it"; V4 (B3)(ii)); and separating 𝐁^{(1)} on p.253.
- *No 𝐑 at step 1.* CRE (1.6), p.247, writes ρ₁(V) as T applied to ρ₀, with partitions of unity. Of the 𝐑-operation p.244 says "We will not describe it here", and its terms enter only in the generalisation of (1.28)–(1.30) (p.254). So ρ₁ of (1.28) is Tρ₀.
- *V^{(0)}.*
  - p.252: "Expanding the terms of the effective action … with respect to the last two perturbative terms in (1.22) up to the first order, we obtain a new expression V^{(0)}(S₁,A,𝐇_{1,Ax})".
  - Here V^{(0)}(S₁) is defined as the exact change of that exponent under (1.22). V4 does the same: "The definition retains p_ξ∘Ψ_ξ without a truncation of the boundary terms described after [CRE (1.24)]" (the paragraph after (retained-positive-kernel)).
  - The first-order Taylor formula with integral remainder, f(A′+h) − f(A′) = ∫₀¹ ∂f(A′+th)·h dt, with h = −g₀⁻¹(g𝐇_{1,Ax} + 𝐅), writes this exact change in first-order form.
  - So CRE's stated properties hold for it, for CRE's reasons: a sum over b ∈ S₁* (h is supported there); almost local, by "the almost local character of the action in (1.15)"; analytic in 𝐇_{1,Ax}; and small, since |𝐇_{1,Ax}| < O(1)B₃e^{−δMR₁}ε₁ (p.251). The intermediate points A′+th stay in the analyticity domain, since |h| ≪ g₀⁻¹δ₀.
  - The Jacobian (1.24) is exponentiated exactly ("can be exponentiated", p.252).
  - (1.22) is taken on V4's set: its second form on "the other bonds of S₁*, a set differing from the one printed at p.252 as recorded in Section boundary-law-collar" (the paragraph before (retained-change-domain)). The identifications of A₁, V^{(0)} and the Jacobian are with V4's version. F_ξ(1) is unaffected on covered branches, since it uses A_e only where Ψ_ξ = id.
- So ρ₁ of (1.28) and V4's ρ₁ = T[ρ₀] = M_*(ρ₀dU) ((first-step)) are equal as measures, for the averaging choice (global-average) permitted after (0.1): "Bałaban's transformations of (0.1) into (1.11)–(1.15) are identities for the integrals of bounded Borel functions of V against these measures under the branch hypotheses (B1)–(B4)" (V4, the paragraph after Lemma global-average). No density is asserted: "No absolute continuity is asserted or needed" (ibid.).
- LFR I p.200: after 𝐑-operations the density "may not be equal to ρ_k, but is equivalent to it, in the sense that they have equal integrals". This enters from step 2 on, not at step 1.

**(2) The Integrands Agree Label by Label.**
- V4 (native-source-explicit-outer-operation): 𝒪[𝔐](V) = Σ_{Ω₁,R₁} χ₁ ∫δ(V̄₀V⁻¹)[∫ι_ξζ_ξχ_{ξ,e}κ_ξ𝔐_ξ dA_e|_J] dV₀|_{Ω₁ᶜ}, with κ_ξ the prefactor (native-source-outer-prefactor), that of (1.15).
- CRE (1.28)–(1.29): Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁} χ₁ Σ_{S₁} ∫dV₀|_{Ω₁ᶜ} δ(V̄₀V⁻¹)ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) ∫dA|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ} χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁)·G_{Λ₁}(A)·exp A₁. Here G_{Λ₁} is the exponential of the two quadratic forms in (1.29).
- *Labels.* (Ω₁,R₁) determines (Λ₁,S₁) by (1.20) and the filling (p.251), and "the set S₁ determines the set R₁" (p.253), since R₁ = S₁ ∩ Ω₁^{∼−1} (R₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−1}, p.250). So the label sums agree: the Σ_{Λ₁}Σ_{S₁} of (1.28)–(1.29) is the regrouping Σ_{R₁} = Σ_{Λ₁}Σ_{S₁: Λ₁(Ω₁, S₁∩Ω₁^{∼−1}) = Λ₁} of the R₁-sum of (1.25), an identity.
- *Variables.* V4's J is the set of free bonds of Ω₁ outside I = Λ₁*: "Put J = E_{Ω,*}∖D", with D = I = Λ₁* (V4, the subsection The Conditional Integral of Section remaining-physical-comparison). CRE integrates A|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ}. Both use the variables of V4's version of (1.22) ((1)). CRE's (1.26)–(1.30) are taken at V4's split, I = Λ₁* and J = E_{Ω,*}∖I. That is exact, since the split (1.26) holds whichever free bonds are called interior (§3c(4″)).
  - The split is also [I] Sect. 0's bond convention, so CRE's statements about 𝐄^{(1)} and 𝐁^{(1)} apply to it as printed: a set determines "a set of bonds, i.e. intervals b connecting nearest neighbor points b₋, b₊, which intersect it" (RG1 p.251 l.27), and a bond interval lies in Δ(b₋) ∪ Δ(b₊), so it meets a union of cubes exactly when an endpoint belongs to it. The bonds of Λ₁ are those with an endpoint in Λ₁, and their free ones are V4's I = Λ₁*, "the free bonds with an endpoint in Λ₁" (V4 Section c5-0).
- *Integrands.* At each label and outer datum, ι_ξζ_ξχ_{ξ,e}κ_ξ𝗐_ξ and ζ(Ω₁ᶜ)χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁)·ι_ξ·G_{Λ₁}·exp A₁, with A₁ as modified in (a) (it carries Σ_c log(z_c(U₁)/z_c(1)) over all rows) and ι_ξ one on the support by (e), are the integrand of (1.15) with the cutoff (1.25), in the variables of (1.22), integrated over the fluctuation field on Λ₁. V4 integrates a ∈ 𝔤^I over the box, the young fibre (retained-change-domain) on a branch (Lemma collar-coverage(iv); Lemma c5-3-3(iv) at the retreated carrier). CRE integrates A|_{Λ₁} with χ^{(0)}(Λ₁) in (1.26).
- *Bookkeeping of the identification.* Some placements are not displayed in CRE (1.26)–(1.30). They are fixed in items (a), (b), (c) and (e) below; item (d) lists the inputs. Each placement is made in an exact identity, not an estimate.
  - (a) *z^{(0)}(U₁).* By V4 (the paragraph after (c-large-field-factor)), z^{(0)}(U₁) = c_{Ω₁}·Π_{c∈Ω₁^{[1]}} |det_𝔤 α_c(U₁)|⁻¹, with a constant c_{Ω₁} fixed by the normalisation of the δ-functions and by |Ω₁^{[1]}|. Each factor depends on U₁|_{𝓔_c} only, so on the two blocks of c (Lemma two-block).
    - It is gauge invariant: "Under a site gauge transformation u, α_c(U^u) = Ad_{u(c_−)}α_c(U)Ad^{−1}_{u(b₀(c)_−)}" (Lemma two-block), and Ad maps are orthogonal for the Ad-invariant inner product, so |det| is unchanged.
    - Two-sided bound. At every row there is a site gauge on the two blocks of c with U₁ = e^{iφ}, ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2 on 𝓔_c (V4, the Remark under (B2)). For ‖A‖_∞ ≤ τ_L, α_c is "analytic in A with smallest singular value at least L^{−4}/2" (Lemma two-block), so |det α_c| ≥ (L^{−4}/2)^{d_G}; the upper bound is continuity on the compact set. So z_c := |det α_c|⁻¹ has |log(z_c(U₁)/z_c(1))| ≤ 2C, with C depending on L and G. det α_c is real, nonzero on the connected set ‖A‖_∞ ≤ τ_L and equal to L^{−4d_G} at A = 0, so it is positive and |det α_c| = det α_c is analytic there.
    - z^{(0)} sits in κ_ξ ((native-source-outer-prefactor)), outside 𝒥, so it never enters F_ξ(1), F^Λ(1) or any ratio of D1.
    - *Placement.* The constant c_{Ω₁}Π_c z_c(1) goes into E₁ (p.254: "E₁ is obtained from E by subtraction of all the constants which have appeared in the procedure"). p.254's "i.e." lists only the constants in the first exponential of (1.15) and 𝐄^{(1)}(Λ₁,g₀,1), and z^{(0)} stands outside that exponential. So the placement defines E₁, a constant depending on the labels ("Thus E₁ depends on Ω₁, Λ₁", p.254).
      - *The one property of E₁ used later still holds.* It is p.264's bound ("E_k, except the terms logarithmic in coupling constants, has the same bound as above, only with a different constant", after (2.48), with (2.49); map #MD2.2.3.8 (item 3)). c_{Ω₁} = c₀^{|Ω₁^{[1]}|}, with c₀ the constant of one row: the δ of (1.14) is a product over the rows, and c₀ is the normalisation of one row's δ-function on 𝔤 times the factor L^{−d_G} of δ(LQ_{U₁}B) = L^{−d_G|Ω₁^{[1]}|}δ(Q_{U₁}B) (V4, the operations listed after (native-source-physical-ratio)).
      - z_c(1) = L^{4d_G}, since α_c(id) = L^{−4}id (Lemma two-block). So |log(c_{Ω₁}Π_c z_c(1))| ≤ (|log c₀| + 4d_G log L)|Ω₁^{[1]}| ≤ 2d(|log c₀| + 4d_G log L)|Γ₁|. This is a constant per row, of the kind of (1.15)'s (L⁴−1)|Ω₁^{(1)}|log z, which p.254 places in E₁, and p.264's bound holds with a changed constant. The rest, Σ_c log(z_c(U₁)/z_c(1)), is placed as follows.
      - *Rows of Λ (Λ₁ for CRE's labels), into A₁ as part of E^{(1)}(Λ).* As an identity no reading is needed: A₁ is defined to contain these terms. What is used later is that 𝐄^{(1)}(Λ) + Σ_{Λ-rows} log(z_c(U₁)/z_c(1)) has the properties CRE states for 𝐄^{(1)}(Λ). This follows from Bałaban's own statements:
        - p.254 combines log Z^{(0)}(Λ₁) with the old 𝐄^{(1)}, and the result "coincides with the corresponding function constructed in [I], if the last is suitably restricted to the domain Λ₁".
        - In RG1 (1.3)–(1.4) (p.260) the zeroth-order term paired with −β₁A is [log Z^{(0)}(U) − log Z^{(0)}(1)], with Z^{(0)}(U) = ∫dB δ(Q̃B) exp[−½⟨B,Δ^{(0)}(U)B⟩]; p.260 displays it separately from [𝐄^{(1)} − …], and [I] writes its action "not … separat[ing] the terms of zeroth order" (RG1 (1.6), p.261). And "the expansion of the log Z^{(j−1)}(U_j) constructed in [16], implies, that we can represent this term in the form (1.7)" (p.261), the local form.
        - *The Λ₁-row placement:* integrating the δ-function of RG1 (1.4) over the designated variables gives the factor c·Π_c|det α_c(U)|⁻¹ times the free-variable normaliser.
        - RG1's Q̃ is V4's Q_U. RG1 (2.4) defines Q̃ by M(V′V^{(k)})M(V^{(k)})⁻¹ = exp iQ̃(B′), and p.267 l.26 splits it as LQ̃B′ + C̃(B′). At step 1 the argument of (1.11)'s δ(ŪV⁻¹) at U = e^{iA′}U₁ is M(e^{iA′}U₁)M(U₁)⁻¹ ((1.12): Ū₁ = V on Ω₁^{(1)}; (1.13)). Its logarithm is V4's 𝒬_{U₁}(A′) = LQ_{U₁}A′ + 𝒩_{U₁}(A′) ((native-source-constraint-normalisation), with Bałaban's averaging 𝖬). So RG1's Q̃ is Q_{U₁}, and C̃ = 𝒩_{U₁}.
        - The δ of (1.4) is a product over the rows c of δ_𝔤((Q̃B)(c)), and (Q̃B)(c) = α_c(U)B(b₀(c)) + Σ_{b∈𝓔_c∩E_*}Q̃(c,b)B(b), since "b₀(c) is the only designated bond in 𝓔_c" (Lemma two-block). With α_c(U) invertible (Lemma two-block), ∫_𝔤dx δ_𝔤(αx + y) = c₀|det α|⁻¹ on each row gives the factor. This is RG1 p.268 l.1–2's elimination ("using δ(Q̃B′) we eliminate the variables B′(b₀(c))"), with h(c) "an inverse of a coefficient at the variable B′(b₀(c)) in (Q̃B′)(c), multiplied by L⁻¹" (p.267 l.22–23).
      - *All other rows (those with a block in Λᶜ), into A₁ as 𝐁^{(1)}-terms.* Each f_c := log(z_c(U₁)/z_c(1)) is a term of CRE's format (2.41)–(2.42). Its domain X_c is the union of the cubes of π₁ meeting the two blocks of c, a localisation domain of 𝐃₁ (RG1 p.257 l.7–11), with d₁(X_c) ≤ 2^d. The checks:
        - X_c ∩ Ω₁ ≠ ∅ and X_c ∩ Z₁^∼ ≠ ∅, since c has a block in Ω₁ and one in Λᶜ = Z₁.
        - (i) f_c depends on U₁ on the two blocks of c only (Lemma two-block), and not on A. These two blocks form a connected set that meets Ω₁ and lies in Ω₀ = Ω₁^∼, which gives the component clause after (2.42).
        - (iii) f_c is invariant under G-valued gauge transformations: the identity α_c(U^u) = Ad_{u(c_−)}α_c(U)Ad^{−1}_{u(b₀(c)_−)} (above) extends to complex U by analyticity, and det Ad_u = 1 on the connected group G.
        - (ii) f_c extends analytically to Ũ^c₁(X_c). At (𝐔,𝐉) there, 𝐔 = U′U with U real satisfying (2.34) and U′ = e^{iξA′} satisfying (2.39). A site gauge on a rectangle containing the two blocks gives U = e^{iA} with phases at most μ̂ → 0 as g₀ → 0 (RC_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md Pass 6 (f), "The pointwise bound at the real base", from (2.34) with V4 Lemma c2-local-gauge). With α_{0,1}, α_{1,1} = g₁C_{0,1}(log g₁^{−2})^{q_{0,1}} → 0 (p.259 l.47), 𝐔 = e^{iÃ} on the row with complex Ã and |Ã| → 0.
          - det α_c is analytic near 0 with det α_c(0) = L^{−4d_G} > 0. On a complex polydisc around 0, of a radius depending on L and G, w := L^{4d_G}det α_c satisfies |w − 1| ≤ ½. There f_c = −log w, by the principal logarithm, is the extension, and |f_c| ≤ −log(1 − ½) = log 2.
        - (ii′) The crossing rows, those with a block in Ω₁ᶜ. On that block f_c reads U₁ = V₀ ((1.12), p.248 l.31), and Ũ^c₁(X_c) bounds nothing on X_c ∖ Ω₁. The configurations there are fixed by forced identification.
          - CRE's 𝐁^{(1)} are functions of U₁ (p.253), which may depend on the fields of X ∩ Ω₀ (p.261 l.68–71). U₁ is defined only in the domain of integration of (1.11) (p.248 l.37–38). There are no fluctuation variables off Ω₁, since the integral is ∫dA|_{Ω₁} (p.249 l.31). So the configurations on X ∩ (Ω₀ ∖ Ω₁) are real and lie in that domain, for CRE's terms and for f_c alike.
          - In that domain the cubes within Ω₁^∼ carry the step-0 small-field tests, since the large-field regions lie at distance at least 2LMR₁ from Ω₁ (p.248; V4, the paragraph after (native-source-outer-prefactor)). With (2.34) on the Ω₁ part, the site gauge of V4 Lemma c2-local-gauge on a rectangle containing the two blocks, applied as in V4's Remark under (B2) (at every row, near or far), gives a real part e^{iφ} on 𝓔_c with ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2. The complex part from (2.39) tends to 0 on the Ω₁ part, and there is none off Ω₁, so 𝐔 = e^{iÃ} on 𝓔_c with ‖Ã − φ‖_∞ → 0 as g₀ → 0.
          - On the real set ‖A‖_∞ ≤ τ_L, det α_c is positive and |log(z_c/z_c(1))| ≤ 2C (the two-sided bound above). So det α_c is analytic and nonzero on a complex neighbourhood of the compact convex set K = {‖A‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2}. Its δ-neighbourhoods are convex, so for small δ, depending on L and G, f_c = −log(L^{4d_G}det α_c), real on K, extends analytically to the δ-neighbourhood and is uniformly continuous on its closure. Shrinking δ, |f_c(Ã) − f_c(φ)| ≤ 1 for φ ∈ K and ‖Ã − φ‖_∞ ≤ δ, so |f_c| ≤ 2C + 1 there. So (iv) holds for these terms with log 2 replaced by 2C + 1.
        - (iv) The row terms with a given domain X are at most the coarse bonds meeting X, a number N_{d,M}. So (2.42) holds for them with B₀ ≥ e^{κ2^d}N_{d,M}(2C + 1), by (ii) and (ii′). This constant of d, L, M and G is added to the step-1 B₀ (RC_PROOF_PASS Pass 4 (4): B₀ := C_{d,L,M}(…); Bałaban's own terms carry B₀ = O(p₀³(g_k)), p.278 l.33).
      - The placement is an exact identity: exp A₁ carries Π_c z_c(U₁)/z_c(1) over these rows, and T₁ keeps the printed form (2.21). The terms enter CRE's 𝐁 count (2.47)–(2.48) and (2.49)'s O(1)Σ|Γ_j| with a changed constant, and the large-field bounds treat them as every 𝐁-term ("controlled by the large field estimates", p.253).
    - The Λ-rows term is a function of U₁ = U₁(V,V₀), like −A(1/g₁²,U₁), and enters through exp A₁, which depends on all variables. CRE's T-operations factorise as operations (p.258: "involves integrations and variables restricted to this region"), so neither placement touches (2.19).
  - (b) *ζ(Ω₁ᶜ).* It depends on V, V₀ and on A_e at the bonds of Ω₁ within 2L²M₂R₁+1 of Ω₁ᶜ (V4, the paragraph after (native-source-outer-prefactor)). These bonds lie in Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1} ⊂ S₁. It does not depend on a ((c-large-field-factor)).
    - It is a nonnegative factor of the T₁-integrand. It depends on A on S₁-bonds, so it stands inside ∫dA|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ}; (1.29)'s print is read with that scope. Inside the joint integral its position is then free: by Tonelli for the nonnegative denominator integrand, and by Fubini for the F-weighted numerator (|f_ξ| ≤ 1, integrability from V4's measurability paragraph in Section branch-data).
    - Under (P) it groups over the regions X of (4⁗)(d). This follows from CRE (2.19) for CRE's T₁ at Λ₁ (accepted), since every component of Λ₁ᶜ lies in one ∗-component of Λᶜ ⊇ Λ₁ᶜ (CRE's adjacency is no coarser than ∗). A product over components, grouped by a coarser partition, is again a product. Two steps are used here:
      - *The single-ζ factorisation.* (1.29) carries ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) once. A product of factors of the form (2.21), each carrying the unrestricted "ζ(Ω_{j+1}ᶜ)", would carry it n times. So under (2.19) (accepted) the factor of T^{(0)}(Z₁∩X) is ζ's part on X, and ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) = Π_X ζ_X. CRE's components are no coarser than ∗-components. Bałaban connects families of cubes through common walls (RG1 p.257 l.8–10), and a topologically connected closed union of cubes is ∗-connected. In either sense a component is ∗-connected, so it lies in one ∗-component of Λᶜ ⊇ Λ₁ᶜ.
      - *The bond-by-bond transfer of (1.22).* V4's change of variables and the printed (1.22) both act bond by bond. Each bond's map depends on its own variable and on 𝐇_{1,Ax}(b). This is a function of the background U₁ and of V near ∂Λ₁ ((1.21), p.251 l.32–33), the same parameter in both versions, with the status of the U₁-dependent coefficients of (1.29) ("all the above expressions depend on the configuration U₁", p.253).
        - V4's version differs from the printed one only on bonds of S₁* (the paragraph before (retained-change-domain); Ψ_ξ "acts bond by bond and is the identity off S₁*", the paragraph after (c-reconstructed-field)). The composite of one with the inverse of the other is again bond by bond with these parameters. So it maps a product over the components of Λ₁ᶜ in the sense of (2.19), each factor a function of its component's variables and of U₁, to such a product, and its Jacobian is a product over bonds. Hence (2.19) holds in V4's variables.
  - (c) *The J-bond parts of χ^{(0)}(Λ₁).*
    - A sup-test is a product of single-bond tests: χ({sup_{b∈X}|A(b)| < r}) = Π_{b∈X} 1{|A(b)| < r}. Indicators are idempotent, so the product over the cubes of R₁ᶜ in (1.18) equals Π_{b∈𝔅} 1{|A(b)| < r} over the union 𝔅 of the bond sets (□′^{∼2})*.
    - So χ^{(0)} splits exactly into the tests at I-bonds and at J-bonds. In the variables of (1.22) the J-part is a function of A_e and the branch data (V4 Lemma collar-coverage(ii), with the exact composition (retained-cutoff-composition); (ii) is applied off branches too, as in (e), since its proof uses only (i), the standing conditions and (c-bondwise-shift)). The I-part is the box {Θ_ξ = 1} (Lemma collar-coverage(iv)).
    - The cubes of R₁ᶜ∩Λ₁ᶜ next to Λ₁ also test I-bonds, through (□′^{∼2})*; these factors sit in CRE's χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁). At an I-bond each is 1{|a_b| < r}, a factor of the box, so by idempotence the box times them is the box. So the I-part of the whole product of good-cube tests, over all cubes, is the box, and χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) is read as its J-part. T₁'s cutoff then does not depend on A|_{Λ₁}.
    - The large-fluctuation tests of the cubes of R₁ read no I-bond. Their bonds lie within 2L²M₂R₁ < ℓ of R₁, while Λ₁ ⊂ (R₁′^∼)ᶜ.
    - Placement: the J-part goes into χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) of T₁. CRE's χ^{(0)}(Λ₁) in (1.26) is read as the I-part. Both groupings give the same function, so (1.28) is unchanged.
    - Under (P) the box factors at I∖D(Λ) are single-bond tests. Such a bond has no endpoint in Λ, so both endpoints lie in one region X of (4⁗)(d).
  - (d) *V4's (B1)–(B4), and the inputs of the identification.*
    - (B1)–(B4) record what V4 takes from CRE's own derivation of (1.11)–(1.25): "The following facts about the branch density are taken from [CRE] as stated there, except where an item records a reading, a derivation or a proof" (V4 Section branch-data).
    - The identification with (1.28) uses, beyond them: CRE (1.26)–(1.30), taken at V4's split as in *Variables* (V4 does not use them: "(1.26)–(1.29) are not used, except that (1.27) defines the first-step coupling g₁(g₀)", The Conditional Integral); the definition of V^{(0)} in (1); the separation of 𝐁^{(1)} (p.253, accepted); the Λ₁-row placement (derived), the single-ζ factorisation (forced), the bond-by-bond transfer of (1.22) (derived) and the E₁ placement (a definition) above; and three identifications:
      - the constraint Λ₁(Ω₁, S₁∩Ω₁^{∼−1}) = Λ₁ in (1.29)'s Σ_{S₁} (*Labels*), forced: (1.28) is printed as equal to the R₁-sum of (1.25), whose terms are indexed by (Ω₁,R₁); Λ₁ and S₁ are functions of (Ω₁,R₁) ((1.20), p.251), and "the set S₁ determines the set R₁" (p.253); so at fixed Λ₁ the S₁ of the sum are those with Λ₁(Ω₁, S₁∩Ω₁^{∼−1}) = Λ₁;
      - the scope of ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) in (1.29), inside ∫dA|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ} ((b)), forced: ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) depends on A at bonds of S₁ ((b)), and a factor depending on an integration variable stands inside its integral;
      - χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) as the J-part of the good-cube tests, and χ^{(0)}(Λ₁) as their I-part ((c)), derived: by idempotence the two groupings are the same function, and with this one T₁'s cutoff does not depend on A|_{Λ₁}, as p.252 states ("After these changes of variables the characteristic functions depend on the new variables … restricted to the large field domain Λ₁ᶜ").
    - (P) changes none of (B1)–(B4), since "S₁, R₁, Ψ_ξ, U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V) and χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) are those of the original Λ₁ throughout" (V4 Section c5-3-1, the convention after Lemma c5-3-0).
    - CRE's statements are accepted. The readings V4 names in (H4) (Section main-result) are settled as follows; the bond convention is printed (*Variables*).
      - *The undisplayed constants* (B₃, δ_c, the O(1) of (B3)(iii), the O(L²) of p.247, the O(1) of (B1)(iv), the O(1), B₃ and δ of p.251's 𝐇_{1,Ax} bound) *are independent of the coupling and of the branch.* Printed in the sources CRE invokes:
        - B₃ and VAR's O(1): "The constants a₀, a₁, B₃, depend on d and L only" (VAR p.279 l.28); its O(1) is "an absolute constant" (p.291 l.16), "depending on d and L only" (p.299 l.26); and its bounds hold for every V satisfying (7).
        - δ_c and the δ of p.251, which cites "(190) Sect. G of [15]" (p.251 l.34–35): the rate δ₀/8 of VAR (190) (p.308 l.42). VAR takes its bounds on H, with B₀ and δ₀, from "the Theorem 3.12 from [5]" (VAR p.285 l.24–27; [5] is PBG), and PBG calls δ₀ "a common, best possible, decay rate for all these operators … a positive, absolute constant depending on d and L only" (PBG p.423 l.39).
        - The O(L²) of p.247: CRE argues "by the same reasoning as in the proof of Lemma 1 [14]" (p.247 l.36), and that lemma gives "|V′ − 1| < 4d²α₀ + α₁" (REG p.79 l.45–47).
        - The O(1) of (B3)(iii): p.250 l.44–47 gives the bound because U₁ and U_{1,□′} "are determined by the same configuration V", and the general step carries out this argument with displayed constants (CRE (3.17)–(3.19), p.268 l.38–53): 44d²B₃, and AVG (106)–(108), which are identities (AVG p.33), and (159)–(163), whose constants are 2, 8d, e^{2α₁dL^{j+1}η} and Σ_{j<k}L^{j+1}η ≤ L/(L − 1), with η = L^{−k} (AVG p.42 l.44–54).
        - The O(1) of (B1)(iv): "(2d+1)LdLα₀ = O(1)L²α₀" (AVG p.25 l.11), so it is d(2d + 1). The bound on 𝐇_{1,Ax} is derived in V4 Lemma axial-boundary-field, with constants fixed before g₀.
      - *The representative of the local minima.* Bałaban's claim, accepted. CRE's statements are made for U_{1,□′} "taken in the axial gauge" (p.247 l.35) without a further choice, so they hold for each axial representative, the Borel-chosen one included.
      - *U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V) as a minimal configuration, and its dependence on (V,V₀).* Printed. "A minimal configuration, i.e., an element of the unique minimal orbit, is denoted by U_𝐁(V)" (p.256 l.49–50, (2.12)), with the localised notation (2.13)–(2.15) (p.257), which (1.21) uses. The crossing rows carry V₀ through the constraint U = V₀ of (1.12).
      - *The rows with one block off Ω₁* ((B2)). Printed. A set's bonds of a given scale are those "which intersect it" (RG1 p.251 l.26–27), so such a row c lies in Ω₁^{(1)}; UV3, which CRE follows (p.248 l.26), keeps for Ω₁ᶜ only the δ-functions of the rows b′ ⊂ Ω₁ᶜ (UV3 (13), p.259 l.14). The designated bond b₀(c) lies in the corridor B(c), with one endpoint in each block (RG1 p.267 l.19–20), so it is a bond of Ω₁ and an A-variable. p.249 l.20–21 solves "(Q̃A′)(c) = 0 on Ω₁^{(1)}" for "the variable A′(b₀(c))", and on the outer block U = V₀ by (1.12), as in UV3 (12) (p.258 l.47).
      - *The bonds crossing from Ω₁ to Ω₁ᶜ as variables of the integral over Ω₁ (V4's own reading), and the bonds and rows of Ω₁ᶜ.* Printed for the crossing bonds, forced for Ω₁ᶜ. ∫dA|_{Ω₁} (p.249 l.7) integrates over the bonds of Ω₁, which are those that meet it (RG1 p.251 l.26–27), those with an endpoint in Ω₁ (Ω₁ is a union of unit cells, L and M being odd, and a bond meets only the cells of its endpoints); (1.14)'s |Ω₁*| counts them so (p.249 l.16–17). ∫dU = ∫dV₀∫dA integrates each bond once (Fubini), so V₀ lives on the bonds with both endpoints off Ω₁. The rows split in the same way.
      - *D̃ = φ, and the defining equation of D̃ as the row system* ((B4)(i)). Derived. The equation LQ̃B′ + C̃(B′) = LQ̃B (RG1 p.267 l.26) is an equality of functions on the rows, so it is one equation per row. With RG1's Q̃ = Q_{U₁} and C̃ = 𝒩_{U₁} (the Λ₁-row placement) it is V4's row system. RG1 p.267 l.28–29 states "there exists exactly one solution of this equation, and that it is an analytic function of B" (accepted). φ is an analytic solution with φ_c(0) = 0 and Dφ_c(0) = 0 (V4 (B4)(i)), so D̃ = φ.
      - *"In the domain of integration in (1.11)" as the support of the characteristic factors of (1.11)* ((B1)(iii)). Printed. RG1 defines its characteristic function "in such a way that the domain of integration in (2.1) is restricted to configurations V for which U_k(V) ∈ U_k(ε₀)" (RG1 p.265 l.18–19), and CRE uses the phrase for a set cut out by the characteristic functions' inequalities: "A domain of this type, with O(L²) replaced by a small constant, is also contained in the domain of integration in (1.6)" (p.247 l.37–38). p.248's existence of U₁ in that domain is then Bałaban's claim, accepted.
      - *δ₀ = (A₁/A₀)ε₀.* The printed last member of p.247 l.50 (corrected file, with its note: the first member is fixed by ε₀ = g₀p₀(g₀) after (1.1), the definition after (3.4) and (3.7)–(3.9)).
      - *χ₁(□′) = 0, χ₁ᶜ(□′) = 1 (and so for χ₀′) where U_{1,□′}(V) does not exist.* Forced. χ is the indicator of the set where its condition on U_{1,□′}(V) holds, and the condition fails where the configuration does not exist; (1.4) and (1.8) are decompositions of unity (p.246 l.46–47, p.247 l.39), so χᶜ = 1 − χ.
      - *v read from (1.14), and (1.5)'s "z ≠ y" as x ≠ y.* Forced by the print.
        - p.249 l.41–42 defines v as "the sum of the first two terms under the second exponential in (1.15)". In (1.15) these are −½⟨A,C*Δ₁CA⟩ and v itself, while in (1.14) they are log σ and Tr log. The same sentence gives v "the same meaning as in (27) [16]", and UV3's υ is the sum of the exponents of its (20) and (21), Tr log(I − δD̃/δA) and Σ_b log(σ/σ₀)(A(b) − D̃) (UV3 p.261 l.13–25; UV3's own (27) is B(c), p.263 l.24).
        - The raw p.247 l.4 prints Π_{x∈B(y), z≠y}, where z is the normaliser 1/z and binds nothing, and (1.7) prints x ≠ y.
      - *"Compatible" partitions as nested.* Printed. "The above partitions have to be compatible, and compatible with the partition π₀ as well" (p.245 l.38–39; "compatible with all other partitions", p.257 l.43), and Bałaban's sense of compatibility with a block structure is "for every j the cube □_j is a sum of the big blocks of the lattice T_{L^{−j}}" (REG p.98 l.20–21): each cube of the coarser partition is a union of cubes of the finer.
      - *σ as the normalised Haar density.* Printed. CRE linearises "by the transformations analyzed between (16)–(18) [16]" (p.248 l.52–53). There UV3 writes "dU′ = σ(A′)dA′ = σ₀ σ/σ₀ (A′)dA′, σ₀ = σ(0)" (UV3 p.260 l.21–22), keeps log σ₀|Ω₁*| in the exponent and (σ/σ₀)(A − D̃(A)) under the integral ((18)), and exponentiates the latter as Σ_b log(σ/σ₀) ((21), p.261 l.20–23). (1.14) carries |Ω₁*| log σ₀ separately, so its log σ is UV3's log(σ/σ₀).
      - *The Q̃ of CRE (1.14)–(1.15) as LQ_{U₁}.* Forced by (1.14). At step 1 the constraint of (1.11) at U = e^{iA′}U₁ is exp i𝒬_{U₁}(A′) (the Λ₁-row placement in (a)), each row's δ on G is σ₀⁻¹δ_𝔤 of its logarithm (UV3 (16)), and A′ = A − hD̃(A) gives 𝒬_{U₁}(A′) = LQ_{U₁}A identically (V4 (native-source-local-implicit), RG1 p.267 l.26). So the δ becomes δ(LQ_{U₁}A), the Jacobian is the Tr log term, and no constant arises beyond |Ω₁*| log σ₀. (1.14) shows none, and its E is the normalisation of ϱ₀ (p.243 l.29–30), so Q̃ = LQ_{U₁}, RG1's LQ̃.
        - This is UV3's convention, which CRE cites for these expressions (p.248 l.50–53): the linearised δ is δ(QA), with Q = Q(U₁) the linear part of Q(A′) = QA′ + C(A′) (UV3 (14)–(18), p.259 l.28–38).
      - *X^∼ with the cubes meeting X at an edge or a vertex.* Printed. For a cube RG1 defines □̃ⁿ as "a cube of the size (1+2n)M and with a center at the center of □" (RG1 p.257 l.6–7). For n = 1 this is □ with the 3^d − 1 cubes of the partition having a common point with it, which is CRE's "adding one layer of M₂R₀-cubes touching □" (p.245 l.41). For a set, "adding one layer of cubes taken from this family" (p.245 l.42–43) agrees with this when the set is one cube, so it adds the cubes having a common point with X.
      - *M₁ as a lower bound on the cube side.* Printed. "M₁ is the size of large cubes for which all the theorems of the previous papers, especially those concerning the variational problem, are valid" (p.245 l.35–37), and CRE applies them on cubes of sides MR₀ and LM₂R₀ with M₁ < M₂ < M (p.245 l.33–35). M₁ "was fixed in [4]" (REG p.77 l.16; [4] is PBG), whose Theorem 3.1 holds "for M ≥ M₁" (PBG p.397 l.37–38).
      - *"Regular" as membership in VAR (6).* Quoted and checked (inventory, "Regular" in CRE (2.12): CRE p.256 refers (2.12) to [15], whose space (6) is its regular configurations).
      - *B(P₁¹)ᶜ.* Not needed. The print defines P₁¹ = ((P₀′^∼)ᶜ ∩ (P₀′^{∼2})ᶜ)^{(1)} (raw p.246 l.63, "so printed"), so B(P₁¹)ᶜ = P₀′^{∼2}; with P₁′^∼ in the first slot, as the corrected file has it, B(P₁¹)ᶜ = P₀′^{∼2} ∪ P₁′^∼. V4 uses only B(P₁¹)ᶜ ⊆ P₀′^{∼2} ∪ P₁′^{∼1} (proof of V4 Lemma c3-7-2), which both give.
  - (e) *The branch indicator ι_ξ.* ι_ξ(A_e) = 1 exactly when (A_e, 0) ∈ 𝒟_ξ, "a Borel condition" (V4 Section branch-data, after (c-new-variable-domain)). It is a factor of V4's integrand (native-source-explicit-outer-operation) and has no displayed counterpart in CRE.
    - *Its content.* The proof of V4 Lemma collar-coverage(iv) lists what membership B ∈ 𝒜_ξ means: (1.25) at B is nonzero; every row equation has a solution at g₀𝖢_UB; and the tests of (1.11) at U(B). At a = 0, B = Ψ_ξ(A_e). There:
      - (1.25) at B equals Θ_ξ(0)χ_{ξ,e}(A_e) = χ_{ξ,e}(A_e) (Lemma collar-coverage(ii); its proof uses only (i), the standing conditions and the bondwise shift (c-bondwise-shift)).
      - The near rows, within 2R_c + 2L of I, are solvable by Lemma charts once |B| < 2+4r on their two-block sets. On J-bonds within 3R_c of I this is |A_e(b)| < r, "a factor of χ_{ξ,e}(A_e)" (proof of (iii)). So on the support of χ_{ξ,e} the near rows impose nothing.
      - Each far row is solvable or not according to B|_{𝓔_c∩E_*} alone (Lemma row-locality), and Ψ_ξ "acts bond by bond and is the identity off S₁*" (the paragraph after (c-reconstructed-field)). So its condition is a function ι_c of A_e on 𝓔_c and the branch data.
      - The constraint on the rows and the restriction V₀ off Ω₁ follow from the row equations, and the rows of Ω₁ᶜ from admissibility (same proof). χ₁(Ω₁) and ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) are factors of the integrand already. The other cube factors χ'₀(□′) are one by (B3)(ii).
    - *The far rows are solvable on the support.* The far-row indicators ι_c cannot be placed in χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁): ι_c depends on V through U₁|_{𝓔_c}, against CRE's localisation (p.252: after the change of variables "the characteristic functions depend on the new variables V restricted to the large field domain Λ₁ᶜ. It is the desired localization property"). Instead, ι_c = 1 on the support of the other factors:
      - Let B = Ψ_ξ(A_e) on J and B = 0 on I. Take a free bond b of a far row; it lies in J (far rows miss I). Let □′ ⊂ Ω₁ be the L²M₂R₁-cube containing an endpoint of b in Ω₁, chosen in S₁ when b ∈ S₁*. Then b is a bond of □′^{∼2}.
      - If □′ ⊂ S₁: the factor χ′₀(□′) of (1.25), in the variable of (1.15) (V4 composes the cutoff there, (retained-cutoff-composition)), gives |e^{ig₀B(b)}U₁(b)U_{1,□′}⁻¹(b) − 1| < 2δ₀ ((1.19), second factor; it stays for □′ ⊂ S₁, p.250). V4 (B1)(ii)–(iii) gives |U₁U_{1,□′}⁻¹ − 1| < O(L²)ε₀ on □′^{∼2} (CRE p.247), boundary cubes of Ω₁ included.
      - So |e^{ig₀B(b)} − 1| < 2δ₀ + O(L²)ε₀, by the triangle inequality used in the proof of V4 Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(ii) (there with two terms O(L²)ε₀). g₀B(b) lies in the chart ball, since the condition A_e(b) ∈ 𝒲_b is a factor of χ_{ξ,e} ((retained-cutoff-composition)).
      - If □′ ⊂ R₁ᶜ: then b ∉ S₁*, Ψ_ξ = id at b, and the good test of □′ gives |B(b)| < g₀⁻¹δ₀.
      - Hence h(g₀B(b)) ≤ c_Gc_h(2δ₀ + O(L²)ε₀) on every free bond of the row (V4's norm comparisons, h ≤ |·|_σ ≤ c_h h and the chart bound h(A′) ≤ c_G‖U′ − id‖_op in the proof of Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(ii)). A|_{𝓔_c} = g₀𝖢_{U₁}B|_{𝓔_c} has these entries on the free bonds and, at b₀(c), the linear elimination (regular-stencil-elimination), bounded by a constant C_𝖢(L,G) through |α_c(U₁)⁻¹| ≤ 2L⁴ (Lemma two-block).
      - So ‖A|_{𝓔_c}‖ ≤ n_𝓔^{1/2}(1 + C_𝖢)c_Gc_h(2δ₀ + O(L²)ε₀). This is < ϑ_*/2 once g₀ is below a threshold depending on L and G only (δ₀, ε₀ → 0 with g₀). That is one more smallness condition on g₀, of the kind of threshold item 6 of V4 Section app:thresholds, which V4's list does not contain.
      - Then A ∈ 𝒩_Θ, and Lemma c2-fibre-smallness(iv) gives the small chart-class solution φ_c at every row, with Θ chart-valued. So the reconstructed field U(B) = e^{iΘ}U₁ is defined. Its (1.11) tests are (1.25) at B for the χ′₀ part, and χ₀, χ_Ax are one on the support of the rest (p.248, accepted; (B3)(i)).
      - So, at an admissible pair, χ₁ζχ_{ξ,e}·ι_ξ = χ₁ζχ_{ξ,e}: ι_ξ is one on the support and is omitted from T₁. The membership list of the proof of Lemma collar-coverage(iv) is covered item by item: (1.25) at B is χ_{ξ,e}(A_e) (collar-coverage(ii), applied off branches too, since its proof uses only (i), the standing conditions and (c-bondwise-shift)); the Ψ-domain is the factor 𝒲_b of χ_{ξ,e}; the chart class of the free bonds and the solvability of every row are the items above; the averages are defined on D^{(11)}.
      - *1_adm.* Admissibility of (V, V₀) is CRE's own implicit domain of U₁, a function of (V, V₀) present in CRE's T₁ through the V₀-integral. Its placement is CRE's; it is covered by accepting (2.19) for CRE's T₁, as the single-ζ factorisation does for ζ.
    - Off the branches CRE's conditional integral over A|_{Λ₁} is over an empty fibre and vanishes. With the item above, the branches contain the support of χ₁ζχ_{ξ,e} at admissible pairs, so the identity below holds with ι_ξ omitted.
- *Consequence.* Let N₁ = M_*(W_χρ₀dU). As measures, using 𝗐_ξF_ξ(1) = 𝒥^a_ξ[f_ξ] (𝗐_ξ = 𝒥^a_ξ[1], V4 Section c5-6, first paragraph) and (native-source-physical-ratio):
  - N₁ = 𝒪[𝗐_ξF_ξ(1)] = Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁} χ₁ T₁[F_ξ(1)·exp A₁], with the placements (a)–(e) above (A₁ includes the Λ-rows of log z^{(0)}(U₁); T₁'s integrand includes the other rows; ι_ξ is one on the support of χ₁ζχ_{ξ,e}, (e)). F_ξ(1) is defined on branches, which contain that support.
  - On the right, F_ξ(1) is a function of (Ω₁,Λ₁,S₁,V,V₀|_{Ω₁ᶜ},A|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ}). T₁ integrates V₀|_{Ω₁ᶜ} and A|_{Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ} and sums S₁; V and the outer labels are parameters.
  - So F_ξ(1) is the loop factor in the form (1.28). It is a π_ξ-average of terms of §4's form (see (3)), not itself of that form.

**(3) The Label Sum and the De-Younged Bonds (V4 Lemma c5-6-1).**
- 𝒥^a_ξ[f] = Σ_lab 𝒥^{a,lab}_ξ[f], with Σ_lab 1_lab = 1 pointwise (V4 Section c5-6, first paragraph; Section c5-3-4(c)).
- (c5-6-1): 𝒥^{a,lab}_ξ[f_ξ] = ∫_{𝔤^{I∖D(Λ)}} 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1F^Λ(1) da_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}. 𝗐^lab_ξ is the same integral without F^Λ(1).
- Hence F_ξ(1) = Σ_lab ∫π_ξ(lab, da_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}) F^Λ(1), with π_ξ := 1_lab e^{c^Λ_ξ}I^Λ_1 da_old/𝗐_ξ.
  - π_ξ ≥ 0, since ρ_ξ ≥ 0 ((retained-positive-kernel)), and it has total mass 1.
  - π_ξ is the law of (lab, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)}) under the normalised conditional measure ρ_ξda_old/𝗐_ξ on 𝔤^I.
- With CRE's labels, a_old|_{I∖D(Λ)} would be integration variables of the step-1 conditional integral over 𝔤^I, inside CRE's integral over A|_{Λ₁}. Under the choice (P) of (4) they are variables of T₁^lab, and §4's "outer variables … the de-younged bonds" is this reading.
- The round-0 collar tests read A_e on J, which is an outer variable (collar bonds have A′_e(e) = A_e(e), V4 Section c5-3-1, the paragraph after Lemma c5-3-0). So 1_lab is partly a function of ξ.

**(4) The Choice (P): The Labels as Domain Structure.** The labels are made part of ρ₁'s domain structure: CRE's labels are refined by V4's. A label average under ρ_ξ at fixed ξ would need a cluster expansion of the label law, while V4's label bounds (Section c5-3-4, Prop. c5-2-6, Lemma c5-3-0(b)) hold after the history average and its group expansion (Lemma c5-5-3) at a fixed label.
  - The step-1 output becomes Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁,lab} χ₁T₁^lab[F^Λ(1)·exp A₁^Λ], with:
    - Λ = Λ₁^fin(lab) as the domain of the conditional integral;
    - the de-younged bonds, their box factors and the collar tests moved into T₁^lab;
    - A₁^Λ given by CRE's (1.26)–(1.30) with Λ₁ replaced by Λ.
  - Since Σ_lab 1_lab = 1, the same refinement applied to the denominator is again an equality for Tρ₀. Then 𝒲₁ = F^Λ(1) on each refined term, and the per-term identity of §2 is used without averaging.
  - *The refined data have the form of (2.1) at k = 1.*
    - Λ is a filled union of ℓ-cubes with Λ ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)} ⊂ Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁ (Lemma c5-3-0(a), Lemma c5-3-1(c)), so it satisfies (2.3).
    - S₁ ⊂ Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ ⊂ Ω₁∩Λᶜ, because V4 keeps the S₁ and R₁ of the original Λ₁ (Section c5-3-1, the convention after Lemma c5-3-0).
    - The new characteristic functions on Ω₁∩Λᶜ are the box factors on I∖D(Λ) and the collar tests: good, |A_e(e)|_σ < ϑr, or bad, ≥ ϑr.
  - *What (P) needs.* CRE's inductive description (Sect. 2) and Theorem 1 must accept the refined labels: at step 1 this is D1.0(P), (4′)–(4⁗); at the later steps it is RC_PAYMENT_PASS_2026_10_24.md Sects. 2–3 for the large-field objects and the 𝐑-terms, and map #MD2.2.3.2–#MD2.2.3.23 for the small-field objects.
    - CRE's large-fluctuation threshold is sup|A′(b)| ≥ g₀⁻¹δ₀ ((1.23), p.252). With δ₀ = g₀A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀} (p.247, corrected transcription), this is V4's r = δ₀/g₀ = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀} (Section main-result; Section c5-0).
    - A bad collar test is at ϑr, with ϑ ≤ 1/16 (Remark c5-1-3), on a bond where |A_e(e)|_σ < r also holds (Lemma collar-coverage(iii) at the round of testing, or the box factor; (4⁗)(b); Lemma c5-3-3(iii) concerns the final carrier and is not used here). It is therefore *not* a CRE large-fluctuation function. It refines the small-field set, and its suppression comes from the Gaussian weight in T₁ ((4⁗)(b)).

**(4′) D1.0(P) against CRE pp.256–259 and Sect. 3.**
- *Domains (p.256).*
  - The domains Ω_j, Λ_j "determined by the j-th renormalization transformation" are unions of MR_j-cubes in T_{L^{−j}}. For j = 1 these are ℓ-cubes, and Λ = Λ₁^fin is a union of them.
  - They satisfy "the distance between their boundaries is at least equal to 2MR_j". Λ ⊂ Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−2}, so dist(Λ, Ω₁ᶜ) ≥ 2ℓ.
  - (2.3) holds because Λ is filled.
