  - p.256 adds "They satisfy also other conditions, e.g., the distance between their boundaries is at least equal to 2MR_j, which will be determined inductively by the operations in the k+1-st step". p.256 covers domains "determined by the j-th renormalization transformation", and p.255 covers domains modified by 𝐑; Λ is made by the retreat, a third kind.
  - The conditions that the later operations impose on a step-1 domain are those they use: at step 1 the construction at Λ is CRE's construction at an admissible domain ((4″), (4⁗)(c)); LFR I Sect. 1 uses (P1), (P2) through (G3), (P4), CRE (3.5) at Λ, and Λ a union of M₁-cubes (RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.2-17); CRE's steps 2, …, h use (P1) and the regularity of V₁ on Ω₁ (#RP.3-1–#RP.3-1-5); the small-field objects are map #MD2.2.3.2–#MD2.2.3.23. Each holds at Λ.
- *The T-operation (pp.257–258, (2.19)–(2.22)).*
  - T_k is supported in the large-field region Z_k and factorises over its components and over the scales.
  - The one-step factor (2.21) integrates V_j on Ω_{j+1}ᶜ and A_j on Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}, with a characteristic function χ(Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}∩X) and the quadratic form with C^{(j)}(Λ_{j+1}).
  - The refined T₁^lab has this form at j = 0, with Z₁ = Λᶜ. Its characteristic function is χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) times the box factors on I∖D(Λ) times the collar tests.
  - The quadratic form "couples only the fields A_j in the same component of Z_{j+1}∩Ω_{j+1}"; the other terms go into 𝐁 (p.258). A retreated region joins the component that contains its bad bond, so its couplings to that component stay in T₁^lab. Only cross-component blocks go to 𝐁₁. Their size is bounded in (4⁗)(a).
- *Thresholds.*
  - CRE's own thresholds are unchanged under (P): its characteristic functions keep A₁. ϑA₁ enters only where the bad-test factor is spent ((4‴), (4⁗)(b)).
  - The conditions on A₁ in the pages read (pp.247, 248, 251, 266, 268), for completeness:
    - upper bounds relative to A₀: A₀ "much larger than A₁" (p.247), "sufficiently larger" (p.248), "A₁/A₀ … sufficiently small" (p.266, twice);
    - lower bounds of the form A₁/A₀ ≫ O(1)e^{−cR}: (3.19), O(1)44d²B₃²(1+β₀)(A₀/A₁)e^{−R_k} < 1 (p.268);
    - p.251: |𝐇_{1,Ax}| < O(1)B₃e^{−δMR₁}ε₁ is "much smaller than δ₀, for MR₁ large enough".
    - Each holds with ϑA₁ in place of A₁ for small g, since R_k → ∞.
- *The two items of D1.0(P):*
  - (i) the Gaussian cross terms at the retreated regions, routed to 𝐁₁: (4″) and (4⁗)(a);
  - (ii) the uses of the large-fluctuation factor in CRE Sect. 3 and in the 𝐑-operation papers, with the constant of the exponent: (4‴) and (4⁗)(b).
  - Count check for (ii): a retreat region has at most N⁺_ret = 4(209ℓ)⁴ bonds ((4⁗)(c)), with ℓ = LMR₁ ≍ (log g₀⁻²)^𝔯. A boundary cost of O(1) per bond ((4⁗)(a)) is at most e^{CN⁺_ret}. The bad-test factor is the integrand factor e^{−cϑ²A₁²(log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀}} at threshold ϑδ₀ (the ϑδ₀ bad-cube factor of §3f(2) R1; at a step k ≥ N + 1 its per-component part is the j = 1 factor of (1.79), bounded in (D2) and RC_PAYMENT_PASS Sect. 7), not the history bound q_ϑ.
    - Since 2p₀ ≥ 10𝔯 > 4𝔯 (CRE p.246 l.14: "p₀ ≥ 5r"), it pays for its region for small g₀; the bookkeeping inside (1.79) is §3f(2) Minor 4.

**(4″) D1.0(P)(i): The Gaussian at the Retreated Regions.**
- *V4's split at Λ is CRE (1.26) with Λ₁ replaced by Λ.*
  - V4 Lemma c5-2-5 splits the exponent at Λ = Λ₁^fin. c^Λ_ξ holds the J(Λ)J(Λ) block of H_ξ and the far primitives. K^Λ_{ξ;1} holds the D(Λ)D(Λ) block, the cross term −⟨a, H_{D(Λ)J(Λ)}A^{Λ,Ψ}⟩ and the near primitives. The proof is an exact rewriting of the CRE (1.15) density, whichever free bonds are called interior.
  - H_ξ = 𝖧^{Ω₁}_{U₁} is CRE's C*Δ₁C of (1.15): "𝖧_ξ = 𝖢_ξ*Δ_{1,ξ}𝖢_ξ, with Δ_{1,ξ} given by [CRE (1.16)]" (V4, The Conditional Integral). In both, V collects the terms of order higher than two (CRE p.249; V4 Lemma primitive-sum).
  - So K^Λ is the integrand of CRE (1.26) with Λ in place of Λ₁, in V4's variables, up to the a-independent factor exp Σ_far 𝔭, which sits in c^Λ. The cross term is −⟨ΛᶜA, C*Δ₁CΛA⟩, and 1_{𝒟^Λ} is the box on D(Λ) wherever the fibre is nonempty (Lemma c5-3-3(iv)).
- *Completing the square.*
  - In (1.26), C^{(0)}(Λ₁) is the inverse of the Λ₁-block of the form and the covariance of a Gaussian measure. So H_II is positive definite. (p.248 also has "a minimum of (1.12), with a strictly positive second order differential".)
  - H_DD, with D = D(Λ) ⊂ I, is a principal block of H_II. By Cauchy interlacing its smallest eigenvalue is at least that of H_II. So C^{(0)}(Λ) := H_DD⁻¹ exists, and ‖C^{(0)}(Λ)‖ ≤ ‖C^{(0)}(Λ₁)‖.
  - Decay: V4 (I3) of Section c5-0 gives (γ_L/2)id ≤ H_{ξ,DD} ≤ m_L id for every principal restriction, and it passes to D^fin (Remark c5-3-2). Lemma c5-1-1 then gives ‖C^{(0)}(Λ)(b,b′)‖ ≤ γ⁻¹e^{−μ_H d(b,b′)}, with μ_H = γ_L/(8Lm_L).
  - The two equalities of (1.26) then hold with Λ in place of Λ₁; the logarithm is finite because the fibre is nonempty on the support. They define Z^{(0)}(Λ), 𝐕^{(0)} and 𝐄^{(1)}(Λ, g₀, ΛᶜA), as in CRE: "The above equalities define".
  - The quadratic form left in T₁^lab is that of (1.29) at Λ: −½⟨A, H_JJA⟩ + ½⟨A, H_JD C^{(0)}(Λ) H_DJ A⟩ on J = J(Λ).
  - Ψ_ξ differs from the identity only on S₁*. d(I, S₁*) ≥ ℓ − 2 ((c-collar-distance)) exceeds the entry range ρ_L = 4L of H_ξ ((I1) of Section c5-0). So the D(Λ) blocks are the same in the old and the new variables. The change on S₁* goes into V^{(0)}(S₁) by its definition in (1), as for Λ₁.
- *Routing to 𝐁₁.*
  - (2.21) keeps in T only the blocks within one component of Z₁∩Ω₁ = Ω₁∩Λᶜ. The cross-component blocks are moved into 𝐁₁ by an equality: at k = 1, (1.29) displays the full form, and the component split is the general statement of p.258.
  - These blocks are of two kinds:
    - blocks of H_JJ between components within its range (H_ξ is a stencil sum, (regular-stencil-quadratic-decomposition));
    - the Schur term H_JD C^{(0)}(Λ) H_DJ, which couples components through Λ.
- *What is new relative to CRE's Λ₁.*
  - The new T-variables are the de-younged bonds I∖D(Λ). The collar bonds of J are already T₁-variables; the collar tests are new functions of them.
  - Collar bonds are within ρ_L = 4L < R_c of D(Λ) ⊂ I (R_c ≥ 2R_loc + 1 = 4L + 5). For e ∈ J_col(Λ), d(e,I) ≤ d(e,D(Λ)) ≤ ρ_L, so by collar coverage (i) every one of these bonds lies in 𝒢_ξ∖S₁* (Lemma c5-2-3(a)), away from R₁ and S₁*.
  - The fields are bounded by r on all of them: on I∖D(Λ) by the retained box factor (Lemma c5-2-5), and on the J bonds by Lemma collar-coverage(iii), since d(e,I) ≤ d(e,D(Λ)) ≤ ρ_L ≤ 3R_c (also Lemma c5-2-3(b)).
  - So the boundary data of the conditional integral on Λ are small fields, as for Λ₁: on Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ by CRE's χ^{(0)}(R₁ᶜ∩Λ₁ᶜ), and on Λ₁∖Λ by the box factors.
  - The new characteristic functions split [0, r) at ϑr on the tested collar bonds. No field above r is introduced. For a bond tested at an earlier round k, the same bound holds with D^{(k)} in place of D(Λ): d(e,I) ≤ d(e,D^{(k)}) ≤ ρ_L (proof of Lemma c5-3-1(a)), and e ∈ I∖D^{(k)} ⊂ I∖D(Λ) in the box case.
  - The cost of the new 𝐁-terms and its payment are in (4⁗)(a), (b).
- *The k = 1 construction check.* CRE's k = 1 construction for Λ₁ (the cluster expansion of 𝐄^{(1)}, the 𝐁 representation; accepted) uses these properties of Λ₁:
  - Λ₁ is a filled union of ℓ-cubes, inside Ω₁ at the required distance;
  - its boundary data are bounded by r;
  - (α) "On the set Λ₁^∼ we have only the small fluctuation field characteristic functions from (1.18)" (p.251; p.269 at the general step);
  - (β) its boundary lies near R₁ or Ω₁ᶜ, so that 𝐁^{(1)} "is controlled by the large field estimates" (p.253);
  - (γ) the large-fluctuation functions in χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) are "associated with the set R_j" (p.255), with the (1.21)–(1.22) localisation around them;
  - (δ) the 𝐇_{1,Ax} bound uses the distance > MR₁ between □′ ⊂ S₁ and ∂Λ₁ (p.251).
  - (4⁗)(c) checks each of these for Λ = Λ₁^fin with the enlarged retreat. (α) is stated in sublevel-set form and checked against every use of Λ^∼ in CRE pp.243–285 (p.251 l.8, l.23–24; p.269 l.18) and of LFR I (1.9) (pp.179, 183, 187) in §3f(3) S1. The second use on p.251, the background U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V), is item (δ). (γ) is needed at Λ: p.255's "If Ω_j∩Λ_jᶜ is nonempty, then S_j is nonempty too" fails for Λ when Ω₁ = T.
- *The construction at Λ.* CRE pp.277–278 concern Bałaban's own Λ_{k+1}, so the transfer of the k = 1 construction to Λ is proved, not quoted. RC_PROOF_PASS_2026_10_23.md reads CRE pp.243–285 and the proofs it cites, and its Pass 4 writes the step-1 conditional expansion at Λ out, each piece with its hypotheses checked at Λ:
  - PBG Thm 3.15 at Λ and at Z₀∩Λ, with (P1), (P2) and R·M < 2LMR₁; PBG Thms 3.9–3.10 for the X̃⁵-locality of the walk terms; RG2 Sects. 1–2 (Theorems GLT and GCT of RG2_NOTES.md, with (L1)–(L4) at Λ); and the exact identities of CRE pp.270–275 at k = 0. Terms with X̃⁵ ⊂ Λ (five cubes of side 2M, 10M ≤ MR₁ < ℓ) coincide with the whole-lattice terms, so the 𝐁^{(1)} format (2.41)–(2.42) and the count (2.47) hold at Λ with Λ-independent constants.
  - Pass 5 derives the outer-field expectation values of CRE (3.26), (3.28) at Λ, the R₁ coupling vanishing by the block-locality of the k = 0 action, and Pass 6 derives the Mayer expansion of the shifted cutoffs on the collar (Pass 5 (e)) from V4's Remark c5-3-2 and (c5-4-1).
- *The payment of (β) and (γ)* is RC_PAYMENT_PASS_2026_10_24.md Sect. 5 (#RP.5-7), with the j = 1 factor of Sect. 7 and the gauge compensation of LFR I p.197 through V4's Ad-invariant cutoff norms (#RP.2-12-2). So (i) holds at step 1.

**(4‴) D1.0(P)(ii): The Large-Fluctuation Factor (CRE pp.259–264, 269–270; LFR II pp.380–389).**
- *Where the factor is spent.*
  - The inductive assumption (CRE Sect. 2, pp.255–262) states domains, the T-form, the localised 𝐄, 𝐑, 𝐁 with (2.26)–(2.28), (2.31) and (2.41)–(2.42), and E_k. It contains no large-field estimate. So at k = 1, (P) has to meet only these, which (4′) and (4″) check, with p.256's "other conditions … determined inductively" as in (4′).
  - The small factors are used in two places:
    - In Corollary 3, through (2.49): "The first term on the right-hand side yields the small factors for large field characteristic functions", with the combinatorics "as in [6]", Sect. 3, (3.42) (p.264). [6] is Higgs_{2,3} II, CMP 86 (1982), which is not transcribed.
    - In the 𝐑-operation: LFR II Sect. 1, pp.380–389, the "fundamental large field factors".
- *LFR II's mechanism (pp.380–383, read).*
  - χ^{(j−1)c}(R_j) yields exp(−γ₀g_j⁻²δ_j²(LM₂R_j)^{−d}|R_j|) "by the same positivity bound for the quadratic form" (p.380). This is one factor per cube of the large-fluctuation set.
  - The MR_j-cube cover of P_j∪Q_j∪R_j has components Z_j^{(i)}. Each yields exp(−γ₀min{½B₃⁻²A₀², 2A₁², A₁²}p₀²(d_j′+1)) ≤ exp(−½γ₀A₁²p₀²(d_j′+1) − 2p₀) (p.381). Here d_j′ is the linear size in MR_j-cubes.
  - The costs are O(1)M^dR_j^{d+1}d_j′(Z_j) per large-field domain (p.380) and exp O(1)(MR_j)^{−d}|Z_j| for the sums over admissible sequences (p.383). They are summarised in (1.79).
  - The geometry is (1.76): Z_j ⊂ Z′_{j−1}^{∼10} ∪ ⋃_i(Z_j^{(i)})^{∼2}.
- *(P) in this mechanism.*
  - A bad collar bond (|A_e(e)|_σ ≥ ϑr) yields its factor from the T₁^Λ form, jointly with the R₁ cubes: §3f(2) R1, derived pointwise in (D1) and (D1⁺). At a step k ≥ N + 1 its per-component part is the j = 1 factor of (1.79), with the bad cubes as a fourth generator W₁ of the cover, bounded factor by factor in (D2); T′_k is formed at Λ as printed (RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.2-17-2), and #RP.7 gives the factor for every T-form.
  - A retreat moves Λᶜ up to 103ℓ from the bad bonds, so (1.76) holds at Λ in the modified form Z ⊂ ⋃_{G⊂Z}G^{∼107}, and p.385's constant (14)^{−d} becomes d^{−1}(434)^{−d}. The labels at fixed Λ₁ and Λ cost a volume term of the form of (1.79)'s first factor, and the j = 1 condition holds with c₄ϑ² in γ₀'s place (§3f(2) R2 and Minor 4).
- *The conditions on A₁ in the pages read.*
  - The other conditions on A₁ (p.381 l.24; CRE pp.247, 248, 266 ((4′)); CRE (3.19), p.268) concern CRE's own thresholds, which keep A₁ under (P); ϑA₁ enters only the factor of §3f(2) R1.
  - p.382 l.28 (A₁²48⁻²R_j^{−8}p₁² ≥ R_j^{−8}p₁²) and p.383 l.25–29 concern LFR II's own threshold δ_j′ = g_jA₁p₁(g_j), which (P) does not change.
- *The k = 1 construction check checked against CRE p.269.*
  - At the general step, Λ_{k+1} is built from Ω_{k+1} and R_{k+1} by (3.20) and then filled. CRE uses that "On the domain Λ_{k+1}, in fact on Λ_{k+1}^∼ also, there are only the small field characteristic functions … their product is χ^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})".
  - For Λ = Λ₁^fin: every field on Λ^∼ is below r. On Λ₁^∼ this is CRE's; on Λ₁∖Λ it holds by the box factors ((4″)).
  - With V4's retreat 𝒬(e) a bad bond can lie within ρ_L + 2 ≪ ℓ of Λ, so inside Λ^∼. The enlarged retreat 𝒬⁺(e) of (4⁗)(c) puts every bad bond outside Λ^∼. Then Λ^∼∖Λ carries only small-field functions: CRE's, the box factors, and the good collar tests at ϑr. These are functions of T-variables only. They do not enter the conditional integral, whose cutoff is the box on D(Λ).
  - Apart from the collar tests, the cutoff is box(D(Λ))·χ^{(0)}(R₁ᶜ∩Λᶜ), the step-1 form at Λ of the first factor of (3.21) (§3f(2) Minor 5). Only the good collar tests, at the threshold ϑr, are of a new kind on Λ^∼.
- *LFR II pp.386–389 (the induction j → j+1 for κ_j and the polymer bound).*
  - A₁ enters only through the new-region terms γ₀A₁²p₀²(g_{j+1})(d′+1) of (1.85). For a single new region the condition is the j = 1 one.
  - The induction (1.86)–(1.88) needs 2(1+β₀)^{−1}p₀ ≥ O(1)3(100M)^dR_{j+1}^{d+2} "for p₀ large and γ small enough".
  - (1.92)–(1.97) need only conditions on p₀(g_k), κ, R_k and α (p.389 l.17–19, l.40).
  - So these pages put no condition on A₁ beyond the j = 1 one. No further retreat occurs at the later steps (map #MD2.2.3.24-3).
- *[6] is not needed.* LFR II p.387 l.37–39: (1.80) and the improved (1.89) "implies the inequality (2.50) [III], hence Corollary 3". LFR II asserts this implication; it is accepted as Bałaban's result.

**(4⁗) The Cross Blocks, the Bad Tests, the Enlarged Retreat and the Label Sum.**
- *(a) Size of the cross-component blocks.*
  - p.253's bound is for 𝐁^{(1)}: the clusters within MR₁ of Λ₁ᶜ and the expanded V^{(0)}(S₁). It is printed "O(1)|Λ₁∩Λ₁ᶜ|". It is not stated for the quadratic cross-component blocks of (2.21), and nothing below uses a per-bond reading of it.
  - *Components.* In (2.21), take the components of Ω₁∩Λᶜ for the adjacency "bonds at distance < ℓ/2". CRE leaves the adjacency unspecified; the component split is read on p.258 directly. A coarser adjacency only merges components, so (2.19) still holds over them, and more of the form stays in T. At the level of the ℓ-cube cover this is ∗-adjacency (the adjacency of each use: §3f(3) M9).
  - H_JJ then has no cross-component block, since its range is 4L < ℓ/2.
  - The Schur term couples bonds x ∈ 𝒦 and y ∈ 𝒦′ of distinct components only through H_JD C^{(0)}(Λ) H_DJ, with x, y within 4L of D(Λ). By the choice of adjacency, d(x,y) ≥ ℓ/2. By the decay of C^{(0)}(Λ) ((4″), Lemma c5-1-1) the entry is at most Ce^{−μ_H(d(x,y)−8L)}. C contains (164L⁴m_L)², since the entries of H_{JD} have norm ≤ m_L and each row has at most 164L⁴ of them ((I1)–(I2); §3f(3) M8).
  - The fields on these bonds are below r ((4″)). So the cross-component part at a boundary bond x is at most Cr² Σ_{y: d(x,y) ≥ ℓ/2} e^{−μ_H(d(x,y)−8L)} ≤ Cr²e^{−μ_Hℓ/4}.
  - Here μ_Hℓ = γ_LMR₁/(8m_L) and r² = A₁²(log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀}. Since R₁ grows like (log g₀⁻²)^𝔯, the bound is at most 1 for small g₀. So the cross-component blocks cost O(1) per boundary bond. Their localisation, a sum of terms each localised in a ball with exponential decay in its diameter, is the Neumann series of §3f(3) M8.
  - *Their 𝐁-format.* The format of 𝐁-terms is (2.41)–(2.42) (p.261): dependence on U, A restricted to X, an analytic extension to Ũ^c_j(X), gauge invariance, and |𝐁^{(j)}(X)| < B₀e^{−κd_j(X)}. p.258 moves the cross-component blocks into 𝐁 in CRE's own construction, and at Λ that construction is CRE's at an admissible domain (the k = 1 construction check). So CRE supplies the format; the computation above gives the size and the locality only.
  - *The total 𝐁 cost at Λ.* Under (P), 𝐁^{(1)} is formed at Λ: its clusters are those within MR₁ of Λᶜ, and its expanded V^{(0)}(S₁) is CRE's at Λ₁ (§3c(4)). CRE bounds all boundary terms of the k-th density by (2.48), derived from (2.42): "Bounds for the boundary terms (2.40) are even more elementary" (p.263).
    - At k = 1, (2.47) with j = n = 1 and (2.48) give |𝐁₁| ≤ 2B₁|Γ₁∖Λ₁⁰|. Here Γ₁ = Ω₁^{(1)} (p.255: Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)}), Λ₁⁰ is Λ₁^{(1)} with one layer of MR₁-cubes removed (p.259), and volumes are numbers of points of T^{(1)} (p.263).
    - At Λ (RC_PROOF_PASS Pass 4 (4)) the same bound reads |𝐁₁| ≤ 2B₁|Ω₁^{(1)}∖Λ⁰|. The MR₁-cubes of T^{(1)} are the ℓ-cubes, since ℓ = LMR₁.
    - Every ℓ-cube of Λ∖Λ⁰ is ∗-adjacent to an ℓ-cube meeting Λᶜ. That cube lies in Ω₁, since Λ ⊂ Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−2}. So |Ω₁^{(1)}∖Λ⁰| ≤ 3^d|Z₁∩Ω₁| with Z₁ = Λᶜ.
    - This is a volume cost of the kind LFR II p.380 l.27–29 places in the first exponential of (1.79): "every large field domain Z_j contributes the constant O(1) log g_j⁻²|Z_j| ≤ O(1) log g_j⁻²(MR_j)^d d_j′(Z_j) ≤ O(1)M^dR_j^{d+1}d_j′(Z_j)". Here it holds with Z₁ = Λᶜ, and it is paid by (1.82) with the modified (1.76) (§3f(2) Minor 4).
    - Relative to CRE's Λ₁, the volume added by the retreats and the round-0 fill lies within 103ℓ+1 of a bad bond or within 100ℓ of CRE's unfilled bad region ((c)(β)). That is at most C·N⁺_ret points of T^{(1)} per bad bond, which is the input of the count check in (4′). No per-bond reading of p.253 is used.
    - The change from C^{(0)}(Λ₁) to C^{(0)}(Λ) changes the 𝐁 values but not (2.48), which CRE states for every term of the expansion.
- *(b) The bad collar tests.* They are refinements of the small-field set, not CRE large-fluctuation functions; in LFR's accounting only, their cubes are a fourth generator of the cover (§3f(2) Minor 4).
  - On a bad bond, ϑr ≤ |A_e(e)|_σ < r, by Lemma collar-coverage(iii) for e ∈ J, or by the retained box factor for e ∈ I∖D(Λ). The distance is taken at the round k in which e was tested, e ∈ J_col(Λ₁^{(k)}): d(e,I) ≤ d(e,D^{(k)}) ≤ ρ_L ≤ 3R_c (proof of Lemma c5-3-1(a), "A bad e ∈ J_col(Λ₁^{(k)}) has dist(e,D^{(k)}) ≤ ρ_L"), and in the box case e ∈ I∖D^{(k)} ⊂ I∖D(Λ). The distance to the final D(Λ) exceeds ρ_L by (c)(α).
  - No (1.21)–(1.22) localisation is needed around them. V4 keeps U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V), S₁ and Ψ_ξ of the original Λ₁ (Section c5-3-1, the convention after Lemma c5-3-0), so the background and the change of variables are CRE's at Λ₁. The tests read T-variables only.
  - Their suppression is the factor exp(−½c_bϑ²A₁²p₀²(g₁)|𝒲|) of the ϑδ₀ bad-cube factor (§3f(2) R1 (D2)). It is an integrand factor, derived pointwise in (D1) from H ≥ 0 (CRE p.248) and V4's local gap near I, and the labelled integral is bounded factor by factor ((D2), with the R₁ cubes in (D1⁺)). It needs no lower bound of S on 𝔤^J.
- *(c) The enlarged retreat and the properties (α)–(δ).*
  - *Definition.* 𝒬⁺(e) := the union of the ℓ-cubes meeting the closed ℓ^∞-ball of radius ℓ + ρ_L + 1 around x_e, and Λ^{(k+1)} := Fill(Λ^{(k)} ∖ ⋃_{e∈B^{(k)}} 𝒬⁺(e)). The ball has side 2ℓ + 2ρ_L + 3 < 3ℓ, so it meets at most four aligned ℓ-intervals in each direction, and 𝒬⁺(e) has at most 4⁴ = 256 cubes; every point of 𝒬⁺(e) is within 2ℓ + ρ_L + 1 ≤ 3ℓ of x_e. 𝒬⁺(e) replaces the 𝒬(e) of V4 Section c5-3-1 (radius ρ_L + 1).
  - *Lemma c5-3-1 with 𝒬⁺.*
    - (a) is unchanged: the removed cube lies in 𝒬⁺(e), since ρ_L + 1 ≤ ℓ + ρ_L + 1.
    - (b) is unchanged, since the ball is larger.
    - (c) holds with 103ℓ in place of 102ℓ.
    - (d) holds with N⁺_ret := 4(209ℓ)⁴, since 4(206ℓ+3)⁴ ≤ 4(209ℓ)⁴ and 4(206ℓ+4)(206ℓ+3)³ ≤ 4(209ℓ)⁴ for ℓ ≥ 2.
    - Lemma c5-3-3 is unchanged.
    - Every other use of N_ret and 102ℓ in V4 (Section c5-3-4, (c5-3-1), the proof of Cor. c5-5-10, Remark c5-5-11, Prop. c5-6-4 and its proof, Remark c5-6-5, Lemma c5-7-3, Lemma c5-7-expmoment, Cor. c5-7-4 and Remark c3-8-1) is an upper bound on a count or a distance, or the comparison log N_ret = O(log log g₀⁻²) of (c5-3-1); each holds with (103ℓ, N⁺_ret). The count in the proof of Lemma c5-7-expmoment, 4(204ℓ+5)⁴ ≤ N_ret, becomes 4(206ℓ+5)⁴ ≤ N⁺_ret for ℓ ≥ 2. The count 2⁴ = 16 cubes of Section c5-3-1 becomes 256; V4 uses it nowhere else (its other 2⁴ = 16 counts the cubes met by D_b).
  - *(α).* Every bad bond e lies outside Λ^{fin,∼}.
    - Λ^fin ⊂ Λ^{(j)} ∖ 𝒬⁺(e) for the round j in which e was found bad.
    - A cube ∗-adjacent to a cube that contains an endpoint of e has a point within ℓ + 1 of x_e, so it meets the ball and is not in Λ^fin. Hence e ∉ Λ^{fin,∼}, where ∼ is one layer of ℓ-cubes (p.251, as in R₁′^∼).
    - *Localisation convention.* A cube test of (1.18) is located by its index cube □′, as on p.251 ("On the set Λ₁^∼ we have only the small fluctuation field characteristic functions from (1.18)"), although it reads the bonds of (□′^{∼2})*. A single-bond test (box factor, collar test) is located by its bond. With this convention (α) holds for CRE's own Λ₁ as printed: a large-fluctuation cube of R₁ can read bonds inside Λ₁^∼, but its index cube lies in R₁ ⊂ R₁′, outside Λ₁^∼.
    - On Λ^{fin,∼} ⊂ Λ₁^∼ the characteristic functions are then CRE's (1.18) small-field functions, the box factors on I∖D(Λ), and the good collar tests at ϑr. All are sublevel indicators {|A(b)| < t} with t ≤ r, which is (α) in the sublevel-set form of the k = 1 construction check; every use of (α) is checked in §3f(3) S1. The far-row indicators ι_c are not factors of T₁: they are one on the support (§3c(2)(e)). The z^{(0)} rows off Λ are positive factors, not indicators ((2)(a)).
  - *(β).* The new boundary of Λ^fin lies within 103ℓ+1 of a bad bond ((c) above; +1 for boundary bonds) or within 100ℓ of CRE's unfilled bad region (Lemma c5-3-0(b)). Both cases are in the modified (1.76), Z₁ ⊂ ⋃_G G^{∼107}, and their 𝐁-terms are volume costs inside the first exponential of (1.79), paid by (1.82) (§3f(2) Minor 4, which also covers V4's iterated Fill; RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.5-7).
  - *(γ).* The bad tests are not CRE large-fluctuation functions ((b)): they need no (1.21)–(1.22) layer, and CRE's R₁, S₁ and their layers are unchanged. In LFR II's accounting their cubes are the fourth generator W₁ of the cover (§3f(2) Minor 4); that is bookkeeping of the bound, not a change of R₁.
  - *(δ).* Λ^fin ⊂ Λ₁. So □′ ⊂ S₁ is at least as far from ∂Λ^fin as the relevant distance for Λ₁, and V4 keeps U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V). The 𝐇_{1,Ax} bound is CRE's.
- *(d) The label sum factorises.*
  - *Λ₁ stays an outer label.* CRE's outer labels are (Ω₁, Λ₁), and its S₁-sum is inside T₁: ρ₁ = Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁} χ₁(Ω₁)T₁({Ω₁,Λ₁}) exp A₁ with T₁({Ω₁,Λ₁}) = Σ_{S₁}∫… ((1.28)–(1.29), pp.253–254). The kernel of T₁ depends on Λ₁ through χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁), U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V), Ψ_ξ and 𝐇_{1,Ax}. 𝐇_{1,Ax} has only "the exponential decay property" (p.251), so moving Λ₁ inside T₁ would break the factorisation (2.19). Λ₁ therefore stays outer, and Λ₁^{(0)} = Fill(Λ₁) and 𝔅₀ = (Λ₁^{(0)})ᶜ are functions of the outer label Λ₁.
  - *Index by Λ.* Given the field and (Ω₁, Λ₁), exactly one label has 1_lab = 1, namely the actual run of the retreat. So χ_Λ := Σ_{lab: Λ^fin(lab) = Λ} 1_lab = 1{Λ^fin = Λ}. At fixed Λ₁ the new outer label Λ ranges over Λ ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)} (χ_Λ = 0 otherwise, as Λ^fin ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)} by V4 Lemma c5-3-3(i)); this is a range of the outer sum, since both Λ₁ and Λ are outer.
    - F^Λ(1) and A₁^Λ depend on the label only through Λ (and on Ω₁, and on S₁ inside T₁ as in CRE). So the refined output is Σ_{Ω₁,Λ₁,Λ} χ₁ T₁^{Λ₁,Λ}[F^Λ(1)·exp A₁^Λ]. T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} is CRE's T₁({Ω₁,Λ₁}) with the conditional integration at Λ ((4″)), the de-younged variables and their box factors, the collar tests and χ_Λ.
  - *The extra label.* Λ plays the role of CRE's Λ₁ for the next step (Z₁ = Λᶜ). The original Λ₁ is a parameter of T₁^{Λ₁,Λ}, as Ω₁ and Λ₁ are of CRE's T₁ and E₁ (p.254). So the step-1 sequence of domains is (Ω₁, Λ₁, Λ), one more than CRE's (2.1). The later steps accept the extra label (RC_PAYMENT_PASS Sect. 3; the label sums localise in the components, #RP.2-16-1). The sum over Λ₁ is CRE's own, and the sum over Λ at fixed Λ₁ is the label entropy of §3f(2) R2.
  - *Regions.* (2.19) is the factorisation over a decomposition of Z₁ = Λᶜ into disjoint unions of components ("e.g. as a union of connected components", p.258). Take for the regions X the ∗-components of Λᶜ (ℓ-cubes sharing a point). Distinct regions are at ℓ^∞-distance ≥ ℓ. Each ∗-component of Ω₁∩Λᶜ (the components of (a)) lies in one X, and so does each component of Ω₁ᶜ and of 𝔅₀ = (Λ₁^{(0)})ᶜ.
  - *Claim.* On the range Λ ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)}, χ_Λ = Π_X χ_{Λ,X}. Here χ_{Λ,X} is the indicator that the retreat run started from the full 𝔅₀, with every test at a bond without both endpoints in X declared good, ends with complement X ∪ (𝔅₀∖X). So χ_{Λ,X} depends only on X, on 𝔅₀ (a function of the outer label Λ₁) and on the test variables in X.
  - *Locality in 𝔅₀.* At fixed outer Λ₁ the dependence on all of 𝔅₀ is a dependence on an outer parameter, which (2.19) allows. In fact χ_{Λ,X} depends on 𝔅₀ only through 𝔅₀ ∩ X; §3g(2) uses this.
    - On the range, 𝔅₀∖X lies in the other regions, at distance ≥ ℓ+1 > 2ρ_L + 2 from X.
    - While the local complement stays inside X ∪ (𝔅₀∖X), its X-part and the rest are not ∗-adjacent. So the collar near X, the removals 𝒬⁺(e) for bonds in X and the Fill hulls of the components in X are computed from the X-part alone.
    - The first change that adds a cube outside X ∪ (𝔅₀∖X) is made from X-part data: a hull, or a box 𝒬⁺(e), that meets X and contains a cube outside X. A ∗-path inside it from X to that cube leaves X through a cube ∗-adjacent to X and not in X, which lies in Λ. So the final complement contains a cube of Λ, and χ_{Λ,X} = 0, whatever 𝔅₀∖X is.
  - *Proof.* Let C^{(k)} be the complement at round k, increasing in k. Say that C^{(k)} *splits* if C^{(k)} ⊂ ∪_X X and its part in each X is the part in X of the complement at round k of the local run for X (frozen once that run stops).
    - Collar bonds are exterior, so both endpoints lie in C^{(k)}, in one X. A collar bond has dist ≤ ρ_L to D^{(k)}. Parts of C^{(k)} in different X are ≥ ℓ > 2ρ_L + 2 apart. So the collar near X, and its tests, are those of the local run.
    - A bad bond e is assigned to the X containing x_e. 𝒬⁺(e) is face-connected and contains the cube of x_e. Fill_1 acts on each face-component of the complement, each inside one X, and a hull is face-connected and contains its component. So every change made near X is a face-connected set meeting X.
    - (⇒) If Λ^fin = Λ, every C^{(k)} ⊂ Λᶜ = ∪_X X. A face-connected set inside ∪_X X lies in one X. By induction over the rounds and the Fill passes, C^{(k)} splits, and the local run for X ends with complement X ∪ (𝔅₀∖X). The local run agrees with the global run near X, and it leaves 𝔅₀∖X unchanged, since 𝔅₀ is filled (Lemma c5-3-0(a)) and its tests outside X are declared good. The two agree near X by the ℓ-separation of the regions.
    - (⇐) If every local run ends with complement X ∪ (𝔅₀∖X), the same induction shows that C^{(k)} splits at every round; its base case 𝔅₀ ⊂ ∪_X X is the range Λ ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)}. Each change near X lies in the final local complement, and so in X. The global run stops at the last local stop, so Λ^fin = Λ.
  - *The whole cutoff.*
    - The box factors at I∖D(Λ) and the J-tests of (2)(c) are single-bond tests, each in one X.
    - CRE's χ(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,S₁) (including χ′₀(S₁) and χ^{(0)c}(R₁), which are not single-bond tests) and ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) factorise over the components of Λ₁ᶜ by CRE's (2.19) for CRE's T₁ at the outer Λ₁ (accepted). Each component of Λ₁ᶜ lies in one X ((2)(b); §3f(3) M9), so they group over the X.
    - The z^{(0)} rows off Λ are row-local, each in one X ((2)(a)). ι_ξ is one on the support ((2)(e)).
    - So the whole cutoff of T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} factorises over the regions X, as (2.19) requires. With the quadratic-form blocks of (a) above, this places T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} in the form (2.19)–(2.21) at j = 0, with Λ₁ as an extra outer parameter, and with one new integrand factor: the off-Λ rows of z^{(0)} ((2)(a)). That factor is analytic, positive, gauge-invariant and row-local in U₁, like the coefficients of (1.29).
      - Step 2, the later steps and the 𝐑-operations on other components leave T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} untouched (CRE (2.22) at m = 1, (2.19); RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.3-1-5), and LFR II uses its integrand only through positivity and gauge invariance (#RP.2-10-2, #RP.2-12-1), which the new factor has.
  - The argument uses only that 𝒬⁺(e) is face-connected and contains the cube of x_e, so it also holds for V4's 𝒬(e).

## 3d. D1.1: Uncovered Labelled Terms and the Closed Contour

**The route.** A labelled term is uncovered when a contour bond lies in Ω₁ᶜ, or a free input of a contour link lies in J(Λ) = J ∪ (I∖D(Λ)) (V4 Cor. c5-5-10). The contour is kept closed and is not split into arcs. The exposed links are constants of the young integral, and they sit inside the words.

**(1) Claim.** On every labelled term, covered or not, for exterior data in the support of the term's collar indicators and exterior cutoffs. Hypotheses: those of Lemmas c5-4-0 and c5-4-2, the thresholds of Lemma c5-4-4, threshold item 3 of V4 Section app:thresholds (used in Lemma c5-5-7z), (c5-5-T), (c5-5R-T) and (c5-7-T), and the period condition of Section c5-3-1; for D1(ii) in (4), also those of Prop. c5-5-9 and the hypotheses of §3a and Section c4-12. The domain includes the box factor |a_{old,b}|_σ < r on I∖D(Λ) (Lemma c5-6-1). Then:

F^Λ(1) = κ_C e^{E^∂_{D(Λ)} + E°_{T,n}}·[ f_ξ(m) + Σ_{𝔅≠∅ compatible} q°_Λ(𝔅)·dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Υ^1_{W₁}U^1₁ ⋯ Υ^1_{Wₙ}U^1_n) ],

the formula of §2. In it:
- a contour link in Ω₁ᶜ is V₀(r), a constant of the term (V4 Lemma c5-5-7: "links off Ω₁ equal to their background value");
- a contour link with a free input in J(Λ) is the reconstructed link at the young variable on 𝓕_r ∩ D(Λ) and at the actual exterior variable on 𝓕_r ∩ J(Λ). V4's Wilson factor is "the reconstructed fine field … at the actual retained data, including the change of variables; it is not interpolated" (the paragraph after (convex-response-operands)). So the exterior inputs of links do not depend on t;
- these constants enter f_ξ(m), the words U_j and the words T_W inside Υ_W. At t = 0 the source still carries them: f_ξ(0) ≠ W_χ(C;U₁) in general, and c^0_W carries them too. "Exterior parameter zero" (proof of Prop. c5-6-def) refers to the kernel only. On the T₁ side they are the pending V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ and the pending A on Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁ᶜ (§1), and under (P) also the de-younged bonds of T₁^lab.

**(2) The inputs.** For each one: where V4 uses coverage, and why the use is not needed.
- **(a) Groups, Lemma c5-5-3(a).** V4 assumes A^fin_cov for the whole of Section c5-5 (Section c5-5-0). There it serves to say that f_ξ depends on a only through a|_{I_C}. The lemma needs less:
  - every contour link depends on a only through a|_{𝓕_r ∩ D(Λ)}, and is constant in a otherwise. At carrier I this is the paragraph after (convex-response-operands): "each contour link U_ξ(a)(r) depends on a only through a|_{𝓕_r∩I}, through a row within 2R_c of I when 𝓕_r meets I, and is constant in a otherwise";
  - links off Ω₁ have 𝓕_r = ∅, by the convention stated in Lemma c5-5-3.
  - The unmarked-domain argument (proof of Prop. centred-expectation, after (part1-contact-set)) and the grouped source (Lemma grouped-source, "On every branch") use only this. The t-family objects of Section c5-4 come from statements without coverage: Lemma c5-4-2 is stated "on every labelled term"; Cor. c5-4-3 holds "Under the hypotheses of Lemma c5-4-2"; Lemma c5-4-4 is stated "on every labelled term … and on the covered event A^fin_cov". Only the definitions and thresholds of Lemma c5-4-4 are used here; its proof uses coverage to note that D^Λ_ξ then sums over all of I_C: "the intersection in its definition records this".
- **(b) Reference data and Schur, Lemma c5-5-3(b).** The reference data are defined on every term: the exterior variable is zero on J(Λ), and links off Ω₁ equal id (Lemma c5-5-3). Constant conjugation fixes both, so the Schur identity holds. The proof of Lemma c5-5-3(b) uses the constant gauge R_u acting bondwise, with "the reconstruction covariance q_id(R_uB) = R_uq_id(B)", field-independent hosts and partitions, and "Schur's lemma and the Fubini evaluation of the group product are verbatim". Each of these holds at the reference data on every term: a link with inputs in J(Λ) is evaluated at zero exterior variable, and a link off Ω₁ is id.
- **(c) The exact identity (Lemma c5-5-3(c)) and the ordered product (Lemma c5-5-7(b)).** Both are algebra from (a) and (b). The factorisation over groups needs only that a traversal outside every ℋ(W) depends on no root variable. That is the definition of the contact sets.
- **(d) The link bounds, Lemma c5-5-7z.**
  - *The two uses of coverage.* The preamble of Lemma c5-5-7z: "on A^fin_cov the row c of every traversal n of C has its free-input set 𝓕_{r_n} ⊆ D(Λ) ⊆ I", so "the exterior variable A^{Λ,Ψ} … does not enter a traversal link at all", and every input bond has |a_b|_σ ≤ 2r < 2+4r, so the input lies in the row polydisc 𝒰_c and ‖A‖ ≤ 2C_𝖢g₀(1+2r) ≤ ϑ_*/4. The proof of (a) then writes the row phase without exterior input: "(on A^fin_cov, where no exterior input enters the row, as in the preamble)". With an exterior input, the row phase is g₀𝖢_UΨ_ξ(a + A_e)|_{𝓔_c}.
  - *Why neither is needed.* The input is V4 Lemma charts. Its statement holds "On every branch … for every row c ∈ Ω₁^{[1]} within 2R_c+2L of I", and it says: "Every a+tΨ_ξ(A_e) with a in the enlarged box and t∈[0,1] lies in 𝒰_c and 𝒰_y for every such row and centre (Lemma collar-coverage(i),(iii): Ψ_ξ(A_e)=A_e and |A_e(b)|<r on the bonds of 𝓔_c, 𝓔_y, which are within 2R_c+4L−1 ≤ 3R_c of I …)".
    - *The rows.* Take a traversal n whose link has 𝓕_{r_n} ∩ D(Λ) ≠ ∅. Then 𝓕_{r_n} meets I, so the link depends on a "through a row within 2R_c of I" (the paragraph after (convex-response-operands)). That row is a row of Ω₁^{[1]} within 2R_c+2L of I, so it is one of the rows of Lemma charts, which defines its rows by distance.
    - *The values.* On D(Λ): |a_b|_σ ≤ 2r (enlarged box). On I∖D(Λ): |a_old|_σ < r (the box factor of the a_old-integral, Lemma c5-6-1). On J: |A_e(b)| < r and Ψ_ξ(A_e) = A_e, by the parenthesis in Lemma charts (Lemma collar-coverage(i),(iii)), for exterior data in the support of the collar indicators. So a′ := (a, a_old) lies in the carrier-I enlarged box, and the row input at the term is the carrier-I input a′ + Ψ_ξ(A_e) of Lemma charts at t = 1. Its inclusion covers every bond of 𝓔_c at once.
    - *Conclusion.* The input lies in 𝒰_c. Every input bond has |·|_σ ≤ 2r, so the preamble's bound ‖A‖ ≤ 2C_𝖢g₀(1+2r) ≤ ϑ_*/4 holds as written. The Cauchy estimates of the proof hold with the same constants, the derivatives being taken in the D(Λ) directions only; the inputs on I∖D(Λ) and J are constants of the term, independent of t ((1)). A row that misses D(Λ) gives a fixed unitary factor, which (b) of the lemma allows with ‖N‖ = 1.
  - *Consequences.* Lemma c5-5-7(d),(e), (c5-5-8) and Lemma c5-5R-3 hold on every term with V4's constants. In the proof of Lemma c5-5R-3 the parity step uses only that the term linear in B_{b_i} is odd and that the t = 0 measures are even. Constants inside the links do not affect either.
  - *The endpoint majorants.* The endpoint-majorant paragraph of the proof of Prop. c5-6-def is not used off coverage. Its last clause, "on A^fin_cov the source is the carrier-I source with zero on I∖D(Λ)", fails there, since a_old ≠ 0. In its place, Lemma c5-5R-3 gives, for every group W, h ∈ G and t ∈ {0,1}, |c^t_W(h)| ≤ M^ph_Λ(W) and |a^Λ_W| ≤ M^ph_Λ(W). With |χ| ≤ 1 (normalised character) this gives 𝔡^0_W ≤ 2M^ph_Λ(W). Lemma c5-5-8 holds with (M^ph_Λ, ρ_ph, ε̄_ph) (Lemma c5-5R-3). Here ρ_ph ≤ ρ_t ≤ ρ_pin and M^ph_Λ ≤ M_Λ (Rem. c5-5-11).
    - The proof of Lemma c5-5R-3 uses no coverage. Its inputs are Lemmas c5-5-2(a),(d), c5-5-3(a), Cor. c5-4-3, c5-5-6(b), c5-5R-1(b), c5-5R-2, and the g_t bound sup|g^{(p)}| ≤ 2^{−p}u_t^{|J′|+2p} from Lemma c5-5R-1(b). Lemmas c5-5-2(a),(d), c5-5-6(b), c5-5R-2 and Cor. c5-4-3 are kernel statements at D(Λ) ((2)(i); Lemma c5-5R-2 is the parity of the split measures on 𝔤^{E₀}). Lemma c5-5-3(a) is (2)(a).
      - Lemma c5-5R-1 extends the link bounds of Lemma c5-5-7z(a),(b), and with the g_t bound it holds on every term by the argument of *Why neither is needed* above. Lemmas c5-5R-1 and c5-5R-2 inherit Lemma c5-5-2's hypotheses, which are kernel statements ((2)(i)).
    - The path bound for good groups is in (4).
  - *The pins (Lemma c5-5-8).* They use (c5-5-T)(iv), a condition on g₀ alone, and "the field-independent domain/cluster/interval geometry" (Lemma c5-5-8). There is no coverage input.
- **(e) Kotecký–Preiss, Lemma c5-5-3(d).** It uses the reference majorants, (b) and (d), only.
- **(f) S°_Λ = S°_{D(Λ),C,T} (Lemma c5-7-1).** Its proof uses coverage at two places.
  - *Qualification step.* "D(Λ) is covered on A^fin_cov …; what (I2) needs is Λ_r ⊆ D(Λ) (Remark c5-7-5(2))". Here (I2) is Prop. regional-square-extraction, whose hypothesis is "every free-input stencil of the contour lies in D" (its setting, V4 Section regional-square-scalar). This use is discharged by (g): the proposition holds at every carrier D ⊆ D₊ with the collar. The other parts of the qualification step (D(Λ) ⊆ I, stencils meeting D(Λ) in the regular region, de-younged bonds as exterior variables) do not use coverage.
  - *(F4).* Coverage is used to say that "the exterior variable (zero) does not enter". (F1)–(F3) concern D(Λ) and the reference data. At the reference data the exterior variable is zero on every term. A contour link r off Ω₁ is id on both sides:
    - on V4's side by Lemma c5-5-3;
    - on the torus side by distance. The torus-side free-input stencil of a traversal link r is the coverage stencil Λ_r (Rem. c5-6-5). Λ_r = {r} for a free bond, and Λ_{b₀(c)} = 𝒮_c ∩ E_* lies in the two endpoint blocks of the row (Remark c5-7-5(2)), so Λ_r lies within 2L of r. (The 2L+1 of Section c5-5-0 bounds 𝓕_r, not the torus link.) D(Λ) consists of bonds with an endpoint in Λ₁ ⊆ Ω₁^{∼−2}, so dist(D(Λ), Ω₁ᶜ) ≥ 2ℓ − 1 > 2L, and Λ_r misses D(Λ). So the torus phase of r at i_{D,T}B is q_id(0)(r) = 0, and the link is id.
    - A partly exposed link (inputs in J(Λ) and in D(Λ)) is evaluated at zero exterior variable on both sides, and (I3) fact 2 gives agreement.
    - A link r ∈ Ω₁ with all free inputs in J(Λ): Λ_r ⊆ 𝓕_r (Remark c5-7-5(2)) and 𝓕_r ∩ D(Λ) = ∅, so the torus link at i_{D,T}B is id. On V4's side the link is evaluated at zero exterior variable and zero young input, so it is id too.
  - So the lemma holds on every term.
- **(g) Prop. regional-square-extraction at an uncovered carrier.**
  - *Where the hypothesis enters.* The hypothesis is "every free-input stencil of the contour lies in D" (the setting of Section regional-square-scalar). The proposition says that "The estimates for the defined flat integral use its covered stencils, spectral bounds and small-coupling conditions; the collar enters at the identification". Its proof uses the hypothesis as follows, paragraph by paragraph:
    - Identification: "The collar contains every stencil touching D". This is the collar, not contour coverage.
    - Common chart domain: small-coupling conditions on the box ‖B‖_∞ ≤ r; no stencil condition.
    - Expansion: "supports intersected with D, keeping every original label". Valid for any D. A traversal link at i_{D,T}B is a function of B on its stencil ∩ D, so it is a restricted label of the same kind.
    - Bounds: "Restriction preserves the support-size, overlap and range bounds"; the loads are per-bond and per-site suprema; the pinned-tree bound (proof of (marginal-pinned-bound)) is tree combinatorics plus "Equation (marginal-KP-payment) implies inductively T_p^{(k)} ≤ |z̃_p|e^{a(p)}". None uses coverage.
    - Covariance comparison and derivatives: generic in D; "Constant-conjugation invariance survives differentiation".
    - Short clusters: "Short clusters have their original stencils inside D". For d_j > 2R_* + 2R_0 this follows from d_j alone: a short cluster's network has length ≤ d_j/2 + R_0 and is anchored within R_0 of K_j, while d_T(K_j, Dᶜ) = d_j + R_0. So it is local, not global coverage.
    - So the global hypothesis is used nowhere else in the proof. The words "its covered stencils" refer to the stencils of the short clusters at large d_j. Two inputs from elsewhere:
      - The protected-label expansion is applied "with supports intersected with D, keeping every original label", "replacing every free-input support Λ by Λ ∩ D" (the paragraph after (regional-integrals)). So its inputs lie in D by construction, and no stencil-inclusion assumption can enter through it.
      - V4 Sections c4-10-1 to c4-10-3, used for the path bound, contain no coverage condition. Their stencil statements are the finitely many row/primitive stencil types (Section c4-10-1) and the walk domains D_b of Section fixed-first-kernel (Section c4-10-2), both local constructions independent of D. The coverage clause "On covered far histories" of Section c4-9-4 concerns history sums and is not used here.
  - *The exposed segments.* At a segment whose stencils meet Dᶜ, d_j = 0, and "Bounded d_j are covered by the same rooted bound after enlarging C" (proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction) applies. The rooted bound is per segment (Σ_{Γ: j∈J_Γ}), so the enlargement factor is fixed per segment and uniform in N_exp.
  - *The rooted majorant (regional-square-rooted-majorant)* holds at any carrier, by the list above. Constant-conjugation invariance survives restriction and differentiation (proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction), so a single marked component keeps its quadratic gain.
  - *Constant links.* A traversal whose stencil misses D has a constant link, id at i_{D,T}B, equal to its reference link. Every 𝒢_S with that traversal a in S vanishes, since in (regional-wilson-polynomial) the terms R ∌ a and R ∪ {a} are equal and carry opposite signs. So it contributes no source variable.
  - *The corner extraction* compares F_{C,T} with F_{C,∞} and does not involve D.
  - So (regional-square-extraction)–(regional-square-error) hold at every D ⊆ D₊ with the collar, with d_j = 0 at the exposed segments. "No side-endpoint terms occur because both contours are the same closed square" (after (regional-square-boundary-comparison)) stays true.
- **(h) §3b at an uncovered D.**
  - *Link functions.* §3b(1) compares covariances. At an uncovered intermediate carrier D^{(m)}, the set E = Dᶜ ∩ (B_{m+1}∖B_m) could meet a marked traversal's stencil, and then the link function itself would change between D^{(m)} and D^{(m+1)}. Since supp Γ includes the free-input stencils of Γ's marked traversals (§3b(2)), E ∩ supp Γ = ∅ for every m ≥ 1, the link functions at D^{(m)} and D^{(m+1)} coincide, and §3b(1) applies as written ("Same index"). The diameter of supp Γ grows by at most 2R_K, which the position and diameter constants absorb (§3b(4), short-cluster threshold 4(R_* + R_K)).
  - (Q1) at the carriers D^{(m)}, which may be uncovered, holds by (g): the rooted majorant and the per-cluster bound hold there. So the anchored decomposition holds, with d_j = 0 at the exposed segments, where the anchored bound is Cτ².
- **(i) The remaining statements under V4's standing coverage hypothesis.** Section c5-5-0 places Section c5-5 on A^fin_cov. What the coverage does in each statement used here:
  - *Lemma c5-5-7z, preamble and (a):* (d) above.
  - *The endpoint-majorant clause of the proof of Prop. c5-6-def:* not used ((d)).
  - *Lemma c5-7-1, qualification step:* (f), discharged by (g).
  - *Cor. c5-7-2.* Its proof of (a) is "Lemma c5-7-1 and (I2) at D = D(Λ), whose hypotheses are (c5-7-T) and coverage". With (f) and (g), (a) holds on every term, with the same formula: d_j(Λ) = max(0, d_T(K_j, D(Λ)ᶜ) − R_0) is already V4's definition (Cor. c5-7-2), and d_j(Λ) = 0 at exposed segments. (b) concerns the covered leading term only. Its uncovered analogue is (1) with (4).
  - *Lemma c5-4-4:* the intersection I_C ∩ D(Λ) is in the definition of D^Λ_ξ (proof of Lemma c5-4-4). No change.
  - *Lemma c5-5-1:* the display Σ_{b∈I_C} h(m_b) ≤ C_mϑrN_lat(μ_H/2)Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)} e^{−μ_H dist(e,I_C)/2} is (c5-5-2) "restricted to b ∈ I_C ⊆ D(Λ)" (proof of Lemma c5-5-1). The shift m lives on D(Λ). On every term the display holds with I_C ∩ D(Λ) on the left, and with dist(e, I_C ∩ D(Λ)) ≥ dist(e, I_C) the right side may keep I_C. This is the sum used in (c5-5-8) and §3a(7).
  - *Cor. c5-4-3, Lemmas c5-5-2, c5-5-5 and c5-5-6:* stated under the hypotheses of Lemma c5-4-2, or for bonds of D(Λ) and hosts of 𝐃₀(Λ). They concern kernel objects at D(Λ): selected integrals, the Gaussian disintegration along a coordinate, mark weights. No contour object enters, except through the constant C′_χ of Lemma c5-5-7z. From Lemma c5-4-2 to Lemma c5-5-6 the coverage appears only in Lemma c5-4-4 and its proof, in the standing hypothesis of Section c5-5-0, and in the reference S°_Λ there, which is (f).
  - *Lemmas c5-5R-1, c5-5R-2:* (d), endpoint majorants.

**(3) What the exposed segments cost.**
- *In the scalar:* Cτ² per coarse segment whose stencils meet D(Λ)ᶜ. This sits inside E^∂_{D(Λ)} as local terms e_X, with X within R_0 of K_j and containing a bond of D(Λ)ᶜ: activities attached to D(Λ)ᶜ, the form the class needs (§4).
- *No arc-end factors.* The contour stays closed. At the reference data a fully exposed link (off Ω₁, or with all its inputs in J(Λ)) is id. A partly exposed link is e^{iq_id(a|_{D(Λ)}, 0)}, a link of the reference integral like the covered ones, not id.
- *In the words:* unitary constants, of norm 1.

**(4) D1(ii) on every labelled term (derived).** §3a goes through with two changes.
- **Good groups.** To "Q_W ⊂ Λ₁ and Q_W lifts injectively", add: every free input of every traversal of ℋ(W) lies in D(Λ). On covered terms this holds automatically.
  - For a good group the exterior variable enters no link of ℋ(W), and at exterior parameter zero it enters no kernel (proof of Prop. c5-6-def). So c^0_W carries no exterior constant and depends only on U₁ inside Q_W (Section c4-11-4).
  - *Phase test passed: the path bound.* §3a(3) uses the bound |𝒟_ω c_W(U_{W,t};h)| ≤ C_χ‖ω‖_∞(n(W)+|ℋ(W)|)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) of the proof of Prop. c5-6-def. That proof rests it on "the source remainder bounds of Section c4-10-4 hold on A^fin_cov, where every contour row has its free inputs in D(Λ) and the exterior variable does not enter a link". Section c4-10-4 uses "Only the source f_W(a;h)=χ(T_W(a)P_W⁻¹h) … untouched factors are unitary in its representation": only the rows of the group's own traversals, with the row constants (c4-10-4), which need only the protected-input condition g₀‖𝖢_Za‖ < θ₀/2 (Section c4-10-1).
    - For a good group the added condition puts every input of those rows in D(Λ), so the remainders are those of the covered case. The kernel side along the path (Cor. c5-4-3, Lemma c5-5-2(b),(c) at U_{W,t}, (c4-7-9), Lemma c4-7-1(c)) concerns D(Λ) at exterior parameter zero ((2)(i)). So the path bound holds.
  - *Phase test failed:* 𝔡^0_W ≤ 2M^ph_Λ(W) by Lemma c5-5R-3 ((2)(d)), in place of the endpoint majorant of the proof of Prop. c5-6-def. The rest of §3a(3) is unchanged, with M^ph_Λ in place of M^{(ρ)}_Λ.
  - *Endpoint identities (c4-11-6) off coverage.* The path bound needs both endpoints, c_W(U_{W,0};h) = a^Λ_Wχ(h) and c_W(U_{W,1};h) = c⁰_W(h). V4 restricts (c4-11-6) "to covered far histories and the small interior groups" (Section c4-11-4). Its proof uses three things:
    - (a) covariance (c4-11-5), under the simultaneous transformation of the background and the free variables (Section c4-11-2);
    - (b) finite support: every background bond the coefficient consults has both endpoints in Q_W, by the enlargement of the rectangle to every Hessian stencil, row set, block-mean path, plaquette and interval or closing-path bond (Section c4-11-4);
    - (c) the t = 0 recipe is the regional reference recipe with the same carrier, Ω₁, cutoffs and constants (Section c4-11-3; at D(Λ), Lemma c5-5-3(b) and the proof of Prop. c5-6-def).
    - Coverage enters only in Section c4-11-1: on a covered branch every contour link of the source is the reconstruction from a alone at U₁. For c_W the source is f_W, whose links are those of ℋ(W) (Section c4-10-4). For a good group their free inputs lie in D(Λ) by the added condition, so f_W consults no outer variable (V₀, A_e, a_old).
    - Every selected measure and vacuum occurrence in c_W is built from the kernel at exterior parameter zero: near primitives at a, Hessian H_{DD}, cutoff on D(Λ) (Section c4-11-1 at D(Λ); proof of Prop. c5-6-def). None consults an outer variable.
    - Hence (a) applies with R_g acting on U₁ and a only, (b) is a property of Q_W, and (c) uses Lemma c5-5-3(b)'s reference data, which are the same on every term.
    - The far-history facts are replaced by Q_W ⊂ Λ₁. Q_W then misses S₁ (Λ₁ ∩ S₁ = ∅, CRE p.251) and lies in Ω₁^{∼−2}, the regular region; and δ_{Q_W}(U₁) ≤ Cε₁ is a statement about U₁ on Λ₁ (proof of Prop. c5-6-def).
    - So (c4-11-6) holds at D(Λ) for good groups on every labelled term, and the passed-test path bound above has both endpoints.
  - A link off Ω₁ cannot occur in ℋ(W), since Q_W contains the bonds of every n ∈ ℋ(W) (Section c4-11-4) and Q_W ⊂ Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁.
- **Other groups.** 𝔡^0_W ≤ 2M^ph_Λ(W) by Lemma c5-5R-3 ((2)(d)). Put d^Λ(n) := dist_{d_T}(Γ_n, E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ))). The complement is taken inside the bonds, so the non-free bonds of Λ₁ are not in the target set and the decay is not vacuous.
  - d^Λ(n) ≤ d(n), since E(B_ξ) is part of the target set.
  - If Q_W ⊄ Λ₁ and Q_W lifts, §3a(4)(i) gives d^Λ(n) ≤ d(n) ≤ diam Q_W + 1.
  - If some n₀ ∈ ℋ(W) has a free input b ∉ D(Λ), and Q_W lifts (the diameter bound is for liftable rectangles, §3a(4)(i)), then b ∈ Q_W (Section c4-11-4, the row-set enlargement). Also b ∈ J(Λ) = J ∪ (I∖D(Λ)), and J ⊆ E(Λ₁ᶜ) = E(B_ξ) (Cor. c5-5-10), so b is in the target set. So d^Λ(n) ≤ diam Q_W + 1 for every n ∈ ℋ(W).
  - Either way 𝔰(W) ≥ (d^Λ(n) − 1)/(C_box LM). The non-lifting case is unchanged.
- **Result.** The per-traversal bound of §3a(6) holds on every labelled term, with d^Λ(n) in place of d(n) and ρ̂ := max(ρ, ρ_ph) ≤ ρ_pin in place of ρ: the pins are Lemma c5-5-8(a) with M^{(ρ)}_Λ for the passed-test good groups, and with M^ph_Λ (Lemma c5-5R-3) for the rest. §3a(7) is unchanged: (c5-5-8) holds by (2)(d), and the shift m lives on D(Λ), so the h(m_b)-sum runs over free inputs in D(Λ) (Lemma c5-5-1 with I_C ∩ D(Λ), (2)(i)). On covered terms the sharper d(n) of §3a stays.
- **No new exterior term.** The exterior inputs of links do not depend on t ((1)). So Υ^1_W − Υ^0_W carries the marks of §3a(2) only, and the exposed links enter only through the groups that are not good.
- **What the attached term covers.** The target set of d^Λ is E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ)); it contains J, since J ⊆ E(B_ξ) (Cor. c5-5-10). The de-younged bonds I∖D(Λ) are of two kinds:
  - collar-retreat bonds, within 102ℓ of x_e for a bond e found bad (Lemma c5-3-1(c)), 103ℓ with 𝒬⁺ (§3c(4⁗)(c));
  - round-0 bonds, within 100ℓ of CRE's unfilled bad region (Lemma c5-3-0(b)).
  So Cρ̂e^{−c(d^Λ(n)−1)/(LM)} is attached to the large-field region, to the bad collar tests and to CRE's bad region. That is the case D2(d).

**(5) Count check.** Let N_exp be the number of coarse segments whose stencils meet D(Λ)ᶜ. The bound is an upper bound, in the normalised history average 𝔼 := 𝒵₀^{−1}∫𝒪[Σ_lab 𝗐^lab_ξ ·]dV:
  - A stencil bond z of segment j with z ∉ D(Λ) = D^fin has no endpoint in Λ₁^fin, so z ∈ E(B^fin_ξ) (V4 Section c5-3-4(a)). It lies within c_st = 2L+2 of a contour bond b of the segment (§3b(2)). So N_exp ≤ Σ_{b∈Γ_C}Σ_{z: d(b,z)≤c_st}1{z ∈ E(B^fin_ξ)}, and at most 4(2c_st+1)⁴ bonds z lie within c_st of a given b.
  - With the enlarged retreat 𝒬⁺ (§3c(4⁗)(c)), Λ₁^{(0)}∖Λ₁^fin lies within 103ℓ of the x_e of the bad bonds, so the mark inequality (c5-3-mark-inequality) holds with 103ℓ+1 in place of 102ℓ+1. Its averages are p^{(0)}_z ≤ q and Σ_{e near z}p^ϑ_e ≤ N⁺_ret q_ϑ, with N⁺_ret = 4(209ℓ)⁴ (§3c(4⁗)(c)). So 𝔼1{z ∈ E(B^fin_ξ)} ≤ q + N⁺_ret q_ϑ for every bond z.
  - With |Γ_C| ≤ C_Ln_C (the proof of (c3-7-3), which (c5-3-coverage-average) repeats): 𝔼[N_exp] ≤ 4(2c_st+1)⁴C_Ln_C(q + N⁺_ret q_ϑ), with V4's C_L, q and q_ϑ. No lower bound on p_z is used.
- E^∂_{D(Λ)}: at most Cτ²N_exp + Cτ²Σ_{other j} e^{−c''d_j}. It is carried as local terms, not bounded. Per exposed segment it is O(τ²), one term of (regional-square-error) at d_j = 0. A bound of e^{E^∂} costs e^{Cτ²Σ_j e^{−c''d_j}}, with e^{−c''d_j} = 1 at the N_exp exposed segments; the later steps pay it per cube (WILSON_IT1_D26_CLOSURE_2026_10_24.md §7.4; map #MD2.0.5.27).
- ν_n: Cρ̂ for the traversals within O(LM) of E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ)), decaying away from them. These are carried and never exponentiated.
- No factor exponential in n_C enters (1): the identity is exact, and q° is bounded by Kotecký–Preiss.

## 3e. D1.2b: Analyticity in the Background

**(0) Target, in Bałaban's Form.**
- Every localised term of 𝐁_k has "an extension to an analytic function on the space Ũ_j^c(X, α̃₀, α̃₁)" (CRE p.261, after (2.41)); the regular terms are analytic on U_j^c(X, α_{0,j}, α_{1,j}) (p.259). The pending variable A is not complexified ((2.41)–(2.42)).
- The complex direction is 𝐔 = U′U, with U G-valued and regular ((2.34), (2.38)), and U′ = exp iξA′, A′ 𝔤^c-valued: "L^nξ|A′|, (L^nξ)²|∇_U^ξA′| < (1−β(1−2^{−(j−n)}))α_{1,n}" ((2.39), p.261), with α_{1,j} = g_jC₁(log g_j^{−2})^{q₁} ((2.28), p.259).
- Units: ξ = L^{−j} is the spacing of the fine lattice T_ξ (p.256 l.59). At j = n = 1 the factor in (2.39) is 1. In V4's dimensionless fine-lattice units the complex phase per fine bond is a′ := ξA′, and (2.39) reads h_ℂ(a′(b)) < α_{1,1}/L, with fine differences < α_{1,1}/L². Put ϱ₁ := α_{1,1}/L (the map's ϱ_{an,1}, #MD2.0.3.53-7), ‖a′‖_∞ := max_b h_ℂ(a′(b)), with h_ℂ(x+iy)² := h(x)² + h(y)² the Hermitian extension of h (Lemma c5-5-7z), and ‖a′‖_X := max_{b∈X} h_ℂ(a′(b)) for a set X of bonds.
- D1(iii)′ drops CRE's condition on differences, so its domain contains CRE's at j = n = 1 (map #MD2.0.3.53-1, #MD2.2.6.137-1).
  - **D1(iii)′.** Fix a labelled term, a real U₁ in the support of its indicators, and a 𝔤_ℂ-valued a′ on the fine bonds of Ω₁ with ‖a′‖_∞ < ϱ₁.
    - *Where a′ acts.* 𝐔 := e^{ia′}U₁ on every fine bond of Ω₁, including the bonds of J(Λ) and the de-younged bonds. The variables on these bonds stay real: the young variable on D(Λ), the exterior variable on J(Λ) and the pending A on Ω₁ ∩ Λ₁ᶜ. V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ is unchanged.
    - The term is expanded at the real centre m̂₀ := m̂(U₁) of §3j(1), the shift of the real base point (D26 closure 6.4 *Shift*, *The centre*); the background dependence of the shift at 𝐔 is carried by the tilt of (3.12). Then the background-dependent factors (Υ¹_W, the words U_j and the leading word f_ξ(m̂₀)) extend to analytic functions of a′, equal to the real ones at a′ = 0.
    - They are covariant under G-valued gauge transformations, with a′ transformed by the adjoint.
    - D1(ii) holds there in the per-traversal form of §3a(8), with three changes:
      - δ*_n(U₁) is replaced by δ*^c_n(U₁) + a*_n(a′);
      - the rate (31/8)η of §3a(6) becomes (61/16)η, in δ*^c_n, in the large-field and wrapping terms and in c := 61η/(16 max(C_box, C));
      - each group's majorant carries the factor e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} of (3.8).
    - Here δ*^c_n(U) := Σ_{k≥1}(k+1)³e^{−(61/16)ηk}Δ_n(C_boxLM(k+1)), a*_n(a′) := Σ_{k≥1}(k+1)e^{−(61/16)ηk}A_n(C_boxLM(k+1)) and A_n(R) := max{h_ℂ(a′(b)) : b within distance R of n}. The exterior marks of §3a(7) keep their rate η/(C_FLM) and gain the tilt marks 𝔪^δ_Y of (3.12).
  - The indicators are functions of the real data and stay outside, as factors.
  - q°, E^∂_{D(Λ)}, φ and e_X are numbers on each labelled term (§3b(5)), so nothing is asked of them.
- a*_n has the size D2's Cauchy estimates need: at U₁ = id and a′ ≠ 0 the background is not flat, a*_n is of order ‖a′‖_∞, and the bound on the ϱ₁-polydisc, divided by ϱ₁, bounds the first derivative in the fluctuation direction.

**(1) Complex Symmetric Matrices.** Let H = R + iS be a complex symmetric n×n matrix, with R, S real symmetric (the entrywise real and imaginary parts), R ≥ γ′ > 0 as a quadratic form, and ‖H‖ ≤ m. Write ⟨·,·⟩ for the Hermitian product on ℂⁿ.
- (a) *Numerical range.* For x ∈ ℂⁿ, ⟨x,Rx⟩ and ⟨x,Sx⟩ are real, so Re⟨x,Hx⟩ = ⟨x,Rx⟩ ≥ γ′‖x‖². This passes to every principal restriction H_E = ι_E^*Hι_E, with the same γ′ and m: Re⟨x,H_Ex⟩ = Re⟨ι_Ex,Hι_Ex⟩. This replaces min–max and interlacing.
- (b) *Inverse.* γ′‖x‖² ≤ Re⟨x,Hx⟩ ≤ ‖x‖‖Hx‖, so H is invertible with ‖H^{−1}‖ ≤ 1/γ′, and γ′ ≤ m.
- (c) *Neumann series.* With m′ := m²/γ′,

  ‖(1−H/m′)x‖² = ‖x‖² − (2/m′)Re⟨x,Hx⟩ + ‖Hx‖²/m′² ≤ (1 − 2γ′²/m² + γ′²/m²)‖x‖²,

  so ‖1−H/m′‖ ≤ q := (1−γ′²/m²)^{1/2} < 1 and H^{−1} = m′^{−1}Σ_{k≥0}(1−H/m′)^k. If H has range R_H, the k-th term vanishes at (b,c) for k < d(b,c)/R_H, so ‖H^{−1}(b,c)‖ ≤ (γ′/m²)q^{d(b,c)/R_H}/(1−q). This is the real argument with constants depending on γ′ and m only.
- (d) *Determinant.* det H = det R·det(1+iK), with K := R^{−1/2}SR^{−1/2} real symmetric and ‖K‖ ≤ ‖S‖/γ′. With eigenvalues λ_k of K, |det(1+iK)| = Π_k(1+λ_k²)^{1/2}, so

  1 ≤ |det H|/det R ≤ exp(n‖S‖²/(2γ′²)).

- (e) *Gaussian integrals.* For real x, |e^{−⟨x,Hx⟩/2}| = e^{−⟨x,Rx⟩/2}. The set {R > 0} is convex and det H ≠ 0 on it by (b), so det(H)^{1/2} has a continuous branch there, positive at S = 0. Both sides of ∫_{ℝⁿ}e^{−⟨x,Hx⟩/2}dx = (2π)^{n/2}det(H)^{−1/2} are analytic in the entries and agree at S = 0, so they agree. For the normalised expectation E_H[F] := (2π)^{−n/2}det(H)^{1/2}∫F e^{−⟨x,Hx⟩/2}dx,

  |E_H[F]| ≤ (|det H|/det R)^{1/2}E_R[|F|] ≤ e^{n‖S‖²/(4γ′²)}E_R[|F|].

  The exponent of the cost is linear in the dimension n. So it is harmless only on local carriers, never on I ((3.4)).
- (f) *Perturbation from a real background.* Let H_𝐔 be complex symmetric, and H_U real with γ ≤ H_U ≤ m at a real U. If ‖H_𝐔 − H_U‖ ≤ s, then ‖R − H_U‖ ≤ s and ‖S‖ ≤ s, since entrywise real and imaginary parts do not increase the operator norm (Re M = (M + M̄)/2 and ‖M̄‖ = ‖M‖). So (a)–(e) hold with γ′ = γ − s and m + s in place of m.
  - *Symmetry.* The compressed Hessian H_𝐔 at 𝐔 = e^{ia′}U₁ is complex symmetric: H_𝐔ᵀ − H_𝐔 is analytic in a′ on the connected polydisc ‖a′‖_∞ < ϱ₁ ((2)(c)) and vanishes at real a′, where e^{ia′}U₁ is a real background and H a real symmetric compression; a power series that vanishes at the real points of a polydisc vanishes identically.
  - *Notation.* In §3e, R := Re H. V4's Hessian range (the R of (fixed-kernel-inputs)) is written R_V.

**(2) Reduction.**
- (a) *Analyticity follows from the bounds.* On a fixed labelled term the factors are built from four things:
  - local maps analytic in the background: the entries of H, the maps of Lemma c2-uniform-chart and the primitives of Lemma c2-primitive-bounds on the link neighbourhood of (2)(c), and along the coefficient path the finite analytic entries of §c4-10-1 on its joint domain;
  - integrals over real fluctuation variables, with cutoff domains in the fluctuation variables only ("The protected cutoff domain is fixed throughout", §c4-10-3; every cutoff is checked in (3.7));
  - finite or convergent cluster sums;
  - logarithms defined as convergent cluster series or on matrix balls of radius < 1/2 (the constraint-Jacobian logarithm of §c4-10-1).
  An integral of an analytic integrand that is locally uniformly dominated is analytic (differentiation under the integral). A locally uniformly convergent series of analytic functions is analytic (Weierstrass). So analyticity on the ϱ₁-polydisc follows from the majorants of (3), taken uniformly on it. The same argument gives analyticity on a complex neighbourhood of each path background of (2)(d).
- (b) *Covariance.* For G-valued u, (e^{ia′}U₁)^u = e^{i Ad_{u}a′}U₁^u at the initial site of each bond. The formula of each factor is covariant identically in 𝐔, so the extension is covariant with a′ ↦ Ad_u a′, and h_ℂ(Ad_u a′(b)) = h_ℂ(a′(b)).
- (c) *Local charts.* V4 evaluates every local map at U₁ in a site gauge u in which the stencil phases satisfy ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2 (Lemma c5-4-0(c) and the applicability paragraph after Lemma c2-uniform-chart). In that gauge 𝐔^u(b) = e^{i Ad_{u(b_-)}a′(b)}U₁^u(b).
  - On 𝔤, ‖x‖_op ≤ √N h(x), since h(x)² = N^{−1}Tr x². So ‖X‖_op ≤ √(2N)h_ℂ(X) on 𝔤_ℂ, and ‖e^{iX} − id‖_op ≤ ‖X‖_ope^{‖X‖_op} ≤ 2√(2N)h_ℂ(X) once √(2N)h_ℂ(X) ≤ log 2.
  - Put r_e := ρ₀/(4√(2N)), with ρ₀ ≤ 1 the width of repair K′ ((3.6)). For ‖a′‖_∞ < r_e every link of 𝐔^u lies within ρ₀/2 of the real link U₁^u(b), so the local maps are evaluated in K′'s link neighbourhood 𝒱_{ρ₀}, where they are analytic with K′'s constants. The ϱ₁-polydisc lies inside once ϱ₁ ≤ r_e/2, a g₀-threshold; the margin is used for the Cauchy estimates of (3.1).
- (d) *The path argument.* Let W be a good group passing V4's phase test C_tg diam(Q_W)²δ_{Q_W}(U₁) ≤ τ_b/2 ((c4-10-17)), and let g_W be the real rooted tree gauge of U₁ on Q_W (Lemma c4-10-5), so that U₁^{g_W} = e^{iB_W} on Q_W with ‖B_W‖_∞ ≤ τ_b/2. Then on Q_W, 𝐔^{g_W} = e^{i Ad a′}e^{iB_W}.
  - *The complex exponent.* §c4-10-1 chooses τ_b so that ‖Z‖ < 2τ_b keeps every link within 1/2 of id. For 0 ≤ t < 4, ‖tB_W‖_∞ < 2τ_b, so every eigenvalue λ of the Hermitian B_W(b) has |e^{itλ} − 1| < 1/2 for all t ∈ [0, 4). By continuity from t = 0, |tλ| < 2 arcsin(1/4) for these t, so ‖B_W(b)‖_op ≤ arcsin(1/4)/2 < 1/4.
    - With ‖Ad a′(b)‖_op ≤ √(2N)ϱ₁ ≤ 1/4, the Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff series log(e^Xe^Y) = X + Y + R(X,Y) converges (‖X‖_op + ‖Y‖_op < log 2), and R(X,0) = R(0,Y) = 0 give ‖R(X,Y)‖_op ≤ C_B‖X‖_op‖Y‖_op on ‖X‖_op + ‖Y‖_op ≤ 1/2, with C_B the sup of ‖D_XD_YR‖ there, a constant of N.
  - Put B^c_W(b) := (1/i)log(e^{i Ad a′(b)}e^{iB_W(b)}) on Q_W and B^c_W = 0 off Q_W. Since h_ℂ(X) ≤ ‖X‖_op, ‖B^c_W − B_W‖_∞ ≤ C_BCH‖a′‖_{Q_W} with C_BCH := 1 + C_B√(2N)/4. So ‖B^c_W‖_∞ ≤ τ_b once C_BCHϱ₁ ≤ τ_b/2, and the direction ω^c_W := B^c_W has ‖ω^c_W‖_∞ ≤ C_tg diam(Q_W)²δ_{Q_W}(U₁) + C_BCH‖a′‖_{Q_W}.
  - The path is U^c_{W,t} := e^{itB^c_W}, 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, inside §c4-10-1's joint domain, where the Cauchy estimates hold at complex Z with ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b. At t = 1 the coefficient is the extended one at 𝐔 ((3.10)). At t = 0 it is the flat reference, as in V4.
  - t ↦ c_W(U^c_{W,t}; h) is C¹ with derivative 𝒟_{ω^c_W}c_W, because c_W is analytic along the path, by (a).
  - The real gauge is used on purpose. A complex tree gauge would multiply complex links along tree paths of length up to diam(Q_W) ≤ C_boxLM𝔰(W), with growth e^{C diam·ϱ₁}, which is not bounded uniformly in 𝔰. The real gauge preserves ‖a′‖.
  - So the good-group bound of §3a(3) holds at 𝐔 once §3a(3)'s derivative bound holds along the complex paths, in the form |𝒟_ωc_W(U^c_{W,t})| ≤ 2C_χe^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}‖ω‖_∞(n(W) + |ℋ(W)|)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) ((3.11)). By (c4-9-2) the part C_BCH‖a′‖_{Q_W} of ‖ω^c_W‖_∞ adds at most 2C_χC_BCHLM𝔰(W)‖a′‖_{Q_W}e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W), since n(W) + |ℋ(W)| ≤ LM𝔰(W) ((c4-6-1)); this gives Cρa*_n, by the summation of §3a(6) at the rate 61/16 ((3.8) *Absorption*).
- (e) *What is left.* By (a)–(d), D1(iii)′ follows from one statement:
  - (★) Under repairs K and K′ ((3.0), (3.6)), V4's majorants for the coefficient and its directional derivatives hold at the backgrounds 𝐔 of (c) and along the complex paths of (d), at the real centre m̂₀ with the tilt of (3.12), with three kinds of extra factor:
    - per group, e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} from the words and closed contractions ((3.8)), and a factor 2 on the derivative bounds from the complex direction ((3.7));
    - per Gaussian component, e^{C₄s|J_Z|} with |J_Z| ≤ 4(LM+1)⁴N₁(Z), from the determinant ratio of (1)(d) and the complex T^c ((3.4));
    - per host, the tilt part of (3.12), which keeps the host bound ω̃_t ≤ ω_*/2 and adds the marks 𝔪^δ_Y.
  - The majorants in question are M(W) and (c4-12-4), Lemma c4-9-1, their D(Λ) forms (the coefficient-path paragraph of the proof of Prop. c5-6-def), and the exterior bound (c5-5-8).
  - The factors are not of the form 1 + O(ϱ₁) uniformly in the group, since 𝔰(W) is unbounded. Since ϱ₁ ≍ g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{q₁} → 0, they are absorbed by three g₀-thresholds: C′ϱ₁ ≤ η/8 (the pin weight, (3.8)); s ≤ (16C₄(LM+1)⁴)^{−1} (the Gaussian budget under K, (3.4)); and the tilt threshold of (3.12).

**(3) V4's Inputs at Complex Backgrounds.** Throughout, H is the I-compression (or the D(Λ)-compression, Lemma c5-4-0(a)) at 𝐔, H_U the same at the real U₁ (or along V4's real path), and s := ‖H − H_U‖ ≤ C_Hϱ₁ ((3.1)). Write R := Re H and S := Im H (entrywise). γ = γ_L/2 and h_+ = m_L, as in (fixed-kernel-inputs).

- **(3.0) Margins: Repair K.** The Gaussian payment has no margin as V4 states it: C_d(α_G + δ_M²)|J_Z| ≤ C_d(α_G + δ_M²)·4(LM+1)⁴N₁(Z) ≤ N₁(Z), from 4C_dδ_M²(LM+1)⁴ ≤ 1/2 ((c-standing-conditions)) and α_G ≤ (8C_d(LM+1)⁴)^{−1} (proof of Lemma gaussian-payment, after (fixed-kernel-trace-payment)), can hold with equality. Repair K runs V4's chain in V4's order (γ, h_+, R_V; κ₁; n_*, R_*, C_w, A_*, C_d; M₁, M₂, M; α_G; then g₀; Section app:thresholds) with these changes:
  - γ_c := γ/2 in place of γ, and h_c := h_+ + γ/2 in place of h_+, throughout the chain; in particular s_H = 2h_c, q_H = 1 − γ_c/s_H, and n_*, β, C_w and δ_M are computed from these. V4's lemmas of Section sec:fixed-first-kernel hold with these parameters, since γ_c ≤ H ≤ h_c.
  - In (c-standing-conditions), 4C_d^Kδ_M²(LM+1)⁴ ≤ 1/4 in place of 4C_dδ_M²(LM+1)⁴ ≤ 1/2. It implies h_c²δ_M²/γ_c ≤ 1/16, in place of h_+²δ_M²/γ ≤ 1/8, since C_d^K ≥ 2d_Gh_c²/γ_c gives h_c²δ_M²/γ_c ≤ 1/(32d_G).
  - α_G ≤ min(γ_c/2, γ_c²/(16A_*²), (8C_d^K(LM+1)⁴)^{−1}).
  - Here A_* := h_c(γ_c^{−1/2}+1), and C_d^K := d_G max(1/γ_c + 2A_*²/γ_c², 2h_c²/γ_c): (fixed-kernel-trace-payment)'s C_d at (γ_c, h_c, A_*), with its 2/3 replaced by 1 and its δ-coefficient doubled ((3.4)).

  Every change is a choice made before the coupling, so the order and the coupling-independence of V4's constants are kept, every later choice being re-made from the changed earlier ones. No later choice bounds α_G from below or M from above ((3.11)). CRE's conditions on M are lower bounds or arithmetic: "M be large" (p.245), M ≥ 8LM₂, odd powers of L, M/M₁ integral (Section app:thresholds), so K's smaller α_G and larger M violate none of them. Then g₀ is reduced so that s ≤ s_*, with s_* fixed by the chain ((3.4)). V4's real statements hold under K, since every changed condition is monotone in the direction used.
- **(3.1) Hessian bounds.** By (1)(f), γ − s ≤ R ≤ h_+ + s and ‖S‖ ≤ s, with V4's range R_V.
  - *The bound s ≤ C_Hϱ₁.* Each entry H(b,c) is the second derivative at the zero fluctuation of the plaquette, gauge and row terms of the regional sum (the paragraph before Lemma hessian-gauge-locality), which are built from the maps of Lemma c2-uniform-chart; it depends on the links within R_V of b_- ((fixed-kernel-inputs)).
    - As a function of a′ on the n_e bonds of its stencil it is analytic on the polydisc ‖a′‖_∞ < r_H := min(r_e, r_iv), and bounded there by a constant C_ent: by K′ for the plaquette and row terms ((2)(c), (3.6)), and for the gauge terms by the bound C_𝒢 of (3.6) with Cauchy's estimate for the second q-derivative at q = 0.
  - Cauchy's estimate along the complex lines z ↦ a′ + zv, h_ℂ(v(b)) = 1 on one bond and v = 0 elsewhere, |z| < r_H/2, bounds each first derivative by 2C_ent/r_H at points with ‖a′‖_∞ ≤ r_H/2. So each entry moves by at most C_e‖a′‖_∞, C_e := 2n_eC_ent/r_H, and Schur's test with at most V₁ = 4(2R_V+3)⁴ nonzero blocks per row and column gives s ≤ V₁C_eϱ₁ at 𝐔, for ϱ₁ ≤ r_H/2.
  - Along the complex paths of (2)(d), the compressions at e^{itB^c_W} and at V4's e^{itB_W} differ by at most c_Ht‖B^c_W − B_W‖_∞ ≤ c_HC_BCHϱ₁: integrate 𝒟H along the segment between the exponents, inside ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b, where V4's Schur bound c_H holds at complex Z ((3.5)). Put C_H := max(V₁C_e, c_HC_BCH).
  - For s ≤ γ/4, R ≥ γ − s ≥ γ_c and R ≤ h_+ + s ≤ h_c (s ≤ γ/4 is put into s_*). The min–max step for the principal restriction (Lemma c5-4-0(a)) is replaced by (1)(a).
- **(3.2) Walk series (Lemma walk-series) and tail ((fixed-kernel-rooted-tail)).**
  - *Real-only step:* "has operator norm at most q_H^n" (the paragraph before (fixed-kernel-grouping)) is spectral.
  - *Complex.* 𝖠_H = (1 − R/s_H) − iS/s_H, with s_H = 2h_c and q_H = 1 − γ_c/s_H under K. For s ≤ γ/4, 0 ≤ 1 − R/s_H ≤ 1 − (γ − s)/s_H, so ‖𝖠_H‖ ≤ 1 − (γ − 2s)/s_H ≤ q_H, since γ − 2s ≥ γ_c. So every block of 𝖠_H^n has operator norm at most q_H^n, and block supports and stencils do not change. Hence the bound and the holomorphy in σ of Lemma walk-series, and (fixed-kernel-rooted-tail), hold at 𝐔 with V4's constants under K.
  - The binomial series still sums to a square root of 𝖦 = H^{−1}, since (Σ_j a_jx^j)² = (1−x)^{−1} as power series and ‖𝖠_H‖ < 1. It is symmetric, and ‖𝖦^{1/2}‖ ≤ s_H^{−1/2}(1 − ‖𝖠_H‖)^{−1/2} ≤ (γ − 2s)^{−1/2}.
- **(3.3) Conditioning (Lemma conditioning).**
  - *Real-only step:* the proof is probabilistic ("conditional law").
  - *Complex.* The convex set {R > 0, ‖𝖠_H‖ < 1} is too large, since (3.4) covers only s ≤ s_*. Put
    - 𝒦 := {H real symmetric : γ ≤ H ≤ h_+};
    - 𝒩 := {H complex symmetric : dist_op(H, 𝒦) < s_*}.
  - 𝒩 is convex (the Minkowski sum of the convex 𝒦 and an open ball), hence connected, and open. It contains the nonempty set of real symmetric H with dist(H, 𝒦) < s_*, which is open in the real symmetric matrices. It contains H_𝐔, since H_{U₁} ∈ 𝒦 and ‖H_𝐔 − H_{U₁}‖ ≤ s < s_*. Every H ∈ 𝒩 lies within s < s_* of some H′ ∈ 𝒦, so (1)(f), (3.2) and (3.4) apply to it.
  - Both sides of (c-conditioning) are analytic in H on 𝒩. The left side is a normalised complex Gaussian ((1)(e)); the right side converges locally uniformly by (3.4).
  - They agree on the real points of 𝒩. An analytic function on a connected open set that vanishes on a nonempty open set of real points vanishes identically (its power series vanishes there). So they agree on 𝒩.
  - V4's sentence after (fixed-kernel-density), "C_{E₀} is real, positive and independent of σ; its determinant is one fixed positive number on the whole polydisc", becomes: at 𝐔, C_{E₀} is complex symmetric, independent of σ, with Re C_{E₀}^{−1} = R_E ≥ γ − s, and det C_{E₀} is one fixed nonzero number ((1)(b)).
  - The sentence is not cited elsewhere in V4, and det C_{E₀} recurs only in the locality paragraph of §c4-10-2, as the local normalisation. So only its σ-independence and its nonvanishing are used.
- **(3.4) Gaussian payment (Lemma gaussian-payment, proved in Section app:fixed-kernel-payment), recomputed.** Write H_E = R_E + iS_E for ι_{E₀}^*Hι_{E₀}, C := H_E^{−1} = C′ + iC″, and γ′ := γ − s ≥ γ_c. By (1)(f), (a) and (b), R_E ≥ γ′, ‖S_E‖ ≤ s and ‖C‖ ≤ 1/γ′.
  - *(i).* The tail bound of (3.2) and ‖H‖ ≤ h_+ + s ≤ h_c give ‖Γ(σ) − Γ(1)‖ ≤ h_cδ_M. With the binomial square root of C (the series of (3.2) for H_E, to which (1)(a) applies), ‖C^{1/2}‖ ≤ (γ−2s)^{−1/2} and ‖C^{1/2}(Γ(σ)−Γ(1))‖ ≤ h_c(γ−2s)^{−1/2}δ_M.
  - V4 uses only the first bound of (i): the norm paragraph of the proof takes it for ‖Γ₁‖ and ‖Γ₂‖. The second bound occurs only in the statement and in the tail paragraph that derives it. So no consumer receives the non-Hermitian square root C^{1/2}.
  - *Real-only step:* "Γ_{E₀}(1) is real, so Γ₂ = Im(Γ_{E₀}(σ) − Γ_{E₀}(1))" (the reserve-identity paragraph of the proof). At 𝐔, Γ(1) = ι_{E₀}^*HΠ_{I∖E₀}𝖦^{1/2} is complex.
    - On the segment H_t := H_U + t(H − H_U), 0 ≤ t ≤ 1, ‖H_t − H_U‖ ≤ s, so (3.2) applies at every H_t: ‖𝖠_{H_t}‖ ≤ x := 1 − (γ−2s)/s_H, and dH_t/dt = H − H_U has norm s.
    - The binomial coefficients a_j are positive, so ‖d𝖦_t^{1/2}/dt‖ ≤ s_H^{−1/2}Σ_j ja_jx^{j−1}·s/s_H = ½(γ−2s)^{−3/2}s, since Σ_j ja_jx^{j−1} = ½(1−x)^{−3/2}.
    - With ‖𝖦_t^{1/2}‖ ≤ (γ−2s)^{−1/2}, ‖H_t‖ ≤ h_c and γ − 2s ≥ γ_c, the product rule gives ‖Γ(1) − Γ(1)|_{H_U}‖ ≤ C_Γs, C_Γ := γ_c^{−1/2} + h_cγ_c^{−3/2}/2. Γ(1)|_{H_U} is real, so ‖Γ₂‖ ≤ h_cδ_M + C_Γs.
    - ‖Γ₁‖ ≤ ‖Γ(σ)‖ ≤ ‖H‖‖𝖦^{1/2}‖ + h_cδ_M ≤ h_c((γ−2s)^{−1/2} + 1) ≤ A_*, by δ_M ≤ 1 and γ − 2s ≥ γ_c.
  - *The B-integral.* |dμ_C(B)| = (2π)^{−n/2}|det H_E|^{1/2}e^{−⟨B,R_EB⟩/2}dB. Against e^{α_G‖B‖²/2} and |e^{−⟨B,ΓX⟩}| = e^{−⟨B,Γ₁X⟩}, the B-integral is

    (|det H_E|/det R_E)^{1/2}·det(1 − α_GR_E^{−1})^{−1/2}·e^{⟨Γ₁X,(R_E−α_G)^{−1}Γ₁X⟩/2}.

  - *The X-form.* With Re⟨ΓX,CΓX⟩ = ⟨Γ₁X,C′Γ₁X⟩ − ⟨Γ₂X,C′Γ₂X⟩ − 2⟨Γ₁X,C″Γ₂X⟩ (bilinear pairings, C′ and C″ real symmetric), the X-exponent is ⟨X,T^cX⟩/2 with

    T^c := Γ₁ᵀ[(R_E−α_G)^{−1} − C′]Γ₁ + Γ₂ᵀC′Γ₂ + Γ₁ᵀC″Γ₂ + Γ₂ᵀC″Γ₁,

    real symmetric and supported on J_Z (Lemma component-blocks is a support statement). At S = 0 and s = 0 this is V4's T_{α_G} of (fixed-kernel-actual-reserve-form). T^c need not be positive semidefinite, and nothing below uses that it is.
  - *Norm.* C − R_E^{−1} = −iCS_ER_E^{−1}, so ‖C′ − R_E^{−1}‖, ‖C″‖ ≤ s/γ′² (entrywise parts do not increase the norm, (1)(f)), and ‖C′‖ ≤ 1/γ′. With ‖(R_E−α_G)^{−1} − R_E^{−1}‖ ≤ α_G/(γ′(γ′−α_G)) (resolvent identity),

    ‖T^c‖ ≤ A_*²[α_G/(γ′(γ′−α_G)) + s/γ′²] + ‖Γ₂‖²/γ′ + 2A_*‖Γ₂‖s/γ′² ≤ 2α_GA_*²/γ_c² + 2h_c²δ_M²/γ_c + C₃s.

    Under K the first two terms are at most 1/8 + 1/8, so ‖T^c‖ ≤ 1/3 for s ≤ 1/(12C₃).
  - *The constant C₃.* Put s₀ := γ/4. For s ≤ s₀ use γ′ ≥ γ_c, δ_M ≤ 1, γ′ − α_G ≥ γ′/2 (α_G ≤ γ_c/2), ‖Γ₂‖² ≤ 2h_c²δ_M² + 2C_Γ²s² and ‖Γ₂‖ ≤ h_c + C_Γs₀. The terms of ‖T^c‖ that carry a factor s then sum to at most C₃s, with

    C₃ := A_*²/γ_c² + 2A_*(h_c + C_Γs₀)/γ_c² + 2C_Γ²s₀/γ_c.
  - *Determinants.* (1)(d) gives ½log(|det H_E|/det R_E) ≤ d_G|E₀|s²/(4γ′²). The eigenvalues of α_GR_E^{−1} lie in [0, α_G/γ′] ⊆ [0, 1/2], and −½log(1−t) ≤ t there, so log det_{E₀}(1−α_GR_E^{−1})^{−1/2} ≤ α_Gd_G|E₀|/γ′. The eigenvalues of T^c lie in [−1/3, 1/3], where −½log(1−t) ≤ |t|, so log det_{J_Z}(1−T^c)^{−1/2} ≤ d_G|J_Z|‖T^c‖.
    - With E₀ ⊆ J_Z the total is at most d_G|J_Z|(α_G/γ_c + 2α_GA_*²/γ_c² + 2h_c²δ_M²/γ_c) + C₄s|J_Z| ≤ (C_d^K(α_G + δ_M²) + C₄s)|J_Z|, with C_d^K of (3.0) and C₄ := d_G(C₃ + s₀/(4γ_c²)): the determinant ratio contributes at most d_G|J_Z|s·s₀/(4γ_c²), and the s-part of ‖T^c‖ at most d_G|J_Z|C₃s.
    - V4's C_d of (fixed-kernel-trace-payment) carries the factor 2/3 of −½log(1−t) ≤ (2/3)t on [0, 1/4]. Here the eigenvalues have either sign and reach 1/3, so the factor is 1, and the δ-term is 2h_c²δ_M²/γ_c in place of h_+²δ_M²/γ. This is why K chooses α_G and M with C_d^K.
  - *Result.* With |J_Z| ≤ 4(LM+1)⁴N₁(Z) (the paragraph after (fixed-kernel-trace-payment)) and K, the total is at most (1/2 + 1/4)N₁(Z) + 4C₄s(LM+1)⁴N₁(Z) ≤ N₁(Z) for s ≤ (16C₄(LM+1)⁴)^{−1}. So (ii) holds at 𝐔 with the same e^{N₁(Z)}, and it factors over the components as in V4, since T^c, C and Γ are block diagonal ((fixed-kernel-exact-blocks)). (iii) is pointwise in real B and unchanged. The tilt of (3.12) is paid in the host potentials, not in this budget.
  - *The threshold s_*.* s_* := min(γ/4, 1/(12C₃), (16C₄(LM+1)⁴)^{−1}). It is fixed by the chain under K, before the coupling. Then g₀ is reduced so that C_Hϱ₁ ≤ s_*.
- **(3.5) The complex-Gaussian moment payment (§c4-10-3), recomputed.**
  - The computation of (3.4) per component gives: |G_σ|e^{α_G‖B‖²/2} is (|det H_E|/det R_E)^{1/2} times the positive real Gaussian of (c4-10-12) with R_E and T^c in place of H_E and T_α, in the variables y := B + (R_E−α_G)^{−1}Γ₁X.
  - With R_E − α_G ≥ γ′/2, 1 − T^c ≥ 2/3 as a form, and ‖(R_E−α_G)^{−1}Γ₁‖ ≤ 2A_*/γ′, the real form ⟨y,(R_E−α_G)y⟩ + ⟨X,(1−T^c)X⟩ is at least min(γ′/2, 2/3)(‖y‖² + ‖X‖²), and ‖B‖² + ‖X‖² ≤ 2(1+2A_*/γ′)²(‖y‖² + ‖X‖²). So it is at least c_G^K(‖B‖² + ‖X‖²), with c_G^K := min(γ_c/2, 2/3)/(2(1+2A_*/γ_c)²): (c4-10-13) with γ_c and 2/3 in place of γ and 3/4.
  - c_G^K replaces c_G at its two uses, (c4-10-14) and the dominated-convergence paragraph of §c4-11-3. The mass is at most e^{N₁(Z)} by (3.4), so (c4-10-14) holds at 𝐔 with c_G^K.
  - The derivative terms Tr(C_E𝒟_ωH_E)/2, ⟨B,(𝒟_ωH_E)B⟩/2, ⟨B,(𝒟_ωΓ)X⟩ and 𝒟_ω⟨ΓX,C_EΓX⟩/2 are bounded as in V4, using |Tr(AB)| ≤ (dim)‖A‖‖B‖, ‖C_E‖ ≤ 1/γ′, 𝒟_ωC_E = −C_E(𝒟_ωH_E)C_E, and V4's Schur bound c_H on 𝒟_ωH/‖ω‖_∞ (§c4-10-2). c_H is a Cauchy bound from the joint balls ‖Z‖ < 2τ_b of §c4-10-1, so it holds at complex Z with ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b, as (c4-10-3) does (the paragraph *Contour Rows in the Joint Domain*), hence along the complex paths of (2)(d).
  - The block bound V_jjq_H^{j−1}c_H/s_H behind (c4-10-6) uses only the block norms of 𝖠_H^l, which are at most q_H^l by (3.2). So (c4-10-6) holds along the complex paths with V4's constants under K; this is the walk-derivative input of §c4-10-2 used in (c4-12-1) and Lemma c4-9-2.
- **(3.6) Charts and Primitives (Lemmas c2-uniform-chart, c2-fibre-smallness and c2-primitive-bounds; §c4-10-1; Lemma c5-4-0(c)).**
  - *Repair K′.* Lemma c2-uniform-chart is stated for real backgrounds with a site gauge in which ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2 on the stencil. Its proof gets uniform constants from two facts: the maps h_U(c), 𝖢_U and 𝒩_{U,c} are compositions of exponentials, relative block averages, the fibre inverse and the chart logarithm, "all analytic for phases ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L and A near zero", and "the set of phase configurations with ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2 on 𝓔_c is compact".
    - In link variables the set 𝒦_L := {(e^{iφ(b)})_b : ‖φ‖_∞ ≤ τ_L/2} is compact, and these maps, the Haar and determinant logarithms of (iii) and the primitives of Lemma c2-primitive-bounds are analytic in the links, jointly with A on the balls of the lemma, on an open set containing 𝒦_L. So there is ρ₀ ∈ (0, 1], depending on L and the group only, such that they are analytic on 𝒱_{ρ₀}, the set of complex link families within operator distance ρ₀ of 𝒦_L bond by bond, and bounded on its closure.
    - K′: after τ_L choose ρ₀; define ϱ_*, H_*, K_* and C_C by V4's proofs, with every supremum over 𝒦_L replaced by the supremum over the closure of 𝒱_{ρ₀} (C_C still increased to at least max(1, (4L³(L−1))^{1/2})); then choose ϑ_* by V4's conditions, which involve ϱ_*, H_* and K_* only; then C^a_k and C_p in the same way, C^a_k also at least the bound of *(iv) at 𝐔* below (V4 chooses C^a_k "separately after (iv)", proof of Lemma c2-uniform-chart).
      - Then every later constant by V4's formulas: ρ_d = 2K_*ϑ_*², C_D = 4K_*, C^D_k = e^kk!2K_*ϑ_*²(2/ϑ_*)^k, Q_* = C^a_0, C_Π = e^{√2C_πQ_*}, C_link = max(C_π, 4m_*C_ΠC_C/ϑ_*), C₀ = N_hC_pe^{κ(B_h+4)} and C^∂₀ = N_h max(C_p, C^∂_p)e^{κ(B_h+4)}.
    - Then Lemma c2-uniform-chart(i)–(iii) and Lemma c2-primitive-bounds hold at every background in 𝒱_{ρ₀}: the proof of (ii) is a contraction on complex balls, with ‖DΦ‖ ≤ 2K_*H_*ϑ < 1/2, and the proof of (iii) uses Cauchy estimates and the chain rule; both use the background only through these suprema and the compactness of the closure of 𝒱_{ρ₀}. By (2)(c), 𝐔 lies in 𝒱_{ρ₀} on every stencil once ϱ₁ ≤ r_e/2.
  - *K′ in V4's order.* V4 fixes ϑ_*, ρ_d, C_C, C_D, C^a_k, C^D_k and C_p after τ_L, n_𝓔, R_*, R_H and R_0 and before γ, h_+, R (Section app:thresholds); ρ₀ depends on L and the group only and is chosen after τ_L.
    - The constants before ρ₀ do not involve the changed ones. The K′ constants are suprema over a set containing 𝒦_L, so they dominate V4's, and V4 uses them as majorants and in the lower bound on C_C.
