    - V4 uses ϑ_* only under upper bounds: 2ϑ_* < ϱ_*, 2ϑ_* ≤ ϱ_G, ϑ_* ≤ τ_L/2 and 4K_*H_*ϑ_* < 1 (Lemma c2-uniform-chart) and the reductions in its proof; ϑ_* + H_*ρ_d < 2ϑ_* (Section sec:source-phase-primitives); 2θ₀ ≤ ϑ_*/2 (§c4-11-5); and the g₀-thresholds that place arguments in the balls of radius ϑ_*/2 and ϑ_*/4 (items 3 and 6 of Section app:thresholds, the conditions g₀C_C(2+4r) < ϑ_*/2 of the flat and regional expansions, and (c4-10-row-domain)). Every other occurrence defines a later constant from it (ρ_d, C^D_k, C_link). None bounds ϑ_* from below.
    - So ϑ_*, chosen after ϱ_*, H_*, K_* and C_C under these upper bounds, is admissible: V4's statements hold with the K′ constants and with the g₀-thresholds re-evaluated, and the order and the coupling-independence of the chain are kept.
  - *Real-only steps of (i)–(iii), not used at 𝐔:* "real on real phases", the positivity of the determinant in (iii), and the real logarithms. At 𝐔 the logarithms are the analytic ones of (iii): the Haar logarithm on the complex neighbourhood of the chart ball, and the determinant logarithm on the disc of radius 1/2 about 1.
  - *(iv) at 𝐔: the BCH reduction.* For x ∈ 𝔤_ℂ with h_ℂ(x) ≤ ϑ_*, the eigenvalues of x have modulus at most ‖x‖_op ≤ √(2N)ϑ_* ≤ π/√2, since ϑ_* ≤ ϱ_G/2 = π/(2√N). So the principal logarithm inverts the exponential near e^{ix}, and the eigenvalues of ad(ix) have modulus at most √2π < 2π, so exp is a local biholomorphism of 𝔤_ℂ near ix.
    - Hence F(x,y) := −i Log(e^{ix}e^{iy}) is analytic, with values in 𝔤_ℂ, on {h_ℂ(x) < ϑ_*, h_ℂ(y) < 2r_a} for some r_a > 0 depending on the group and ϑ_* (compactness of the closed ball), with F(x,0) = x, F(0,y) = y and e^{iF(x,y)} = e^{ix}e^{iy}. F − x − y vanishes on both axes, so h_ℂ(F(x,y) − x − y) ≤ C_ℓh_ℂ(x)h_ℂ(y) on {h_ℂ(x) ≤ ϑ_*/2, h_ℂ(y) ≤ r_a}, with C_ℓ the supremum of ‖D_xD_yF‖ there.
    - On 𝓔_y, in the original gauge, put q̃(b) := F(q(b), a′(b)), so that e^{iq}𝐔 = e^{iq̃}U₁. Define the relative mean at 𝐔 by M^𝐔_x(q) := M_x(q̃)M_x(a′)^{−1}, with M_x V4's analytic relative mean at the real U₁ (Lemma c2-uniform-chart(iv)); at real a′ this is 𝒰_{e^{iq}𝐔}(y,x)𝒰_𝐔(y,x)^{−1}. M_x(a′) is invertible, with inverse e^{iX(0)}e^{−iX(a′)} in the notation of V4's proof.
    - Let ‖q|_{𝓔_y}‖ < ϑ_*/2 and ‖a′‖_∞ < r_iv := min(r_a, 1/(4C_ℓ), ϑ_*/(8C_C)). Then ‖a′|_{𝓔_y}‖ ≤ C_C‖a′‖_∞ ≤ ϑ_*/8 (C_C ≥ |𝓔_y|^{1/2}, the applicability paragraph after Lemma c2-uniform-chart) and ‖q̃|_{𝓔_y}‖ ≤ (1 + C_ℓ‖a′‖_∞)‖q|_{𝓔_y}‖ + ‖a′|_{𝓔_y}‖ ≤ (3/4)ϑ_*. So q̃ and a′ lie in V4's closed ball of radius ϑ_*, where M_x and M_x^{−1} are analytic and bounded uniformly in U₁.
    - The gauge term at 𝐔, 𝒢_{y,𝐔}(q) := Σ_x(1 − ½tr_N(M^𝐔_x(q) + M^𝐔_x(q)^{−1})), is V4's at real a′, since ½tr_N(M + M^{−1}) = Re tr_N M for unitary M. It is analytic in (q, a′) there and bounded by a constant C_𝒢 of L, the group and ϑ_*. Cauchy's estimates and polarisation, as in the proof of (iii), bound its q-derivatives of order k ≤ 5 on ‖q|_{𝓔_y}‖ ≤ ϑ_*/4 by e^kk!(4/ϑ_*)^kC_𝒢, and K′'s C^a_k is taken at least this. The callers' inputs lie in that ball: ‖g₀B|_{𝓔_y}‖ ≤ 2C_Cg₀(1+2r) ≤ ϑ_*/4 on 𝒰_y (threshold item 3).
    - 𝒢_{y,𝐔}(0) = 0 and D_q𝒢_{y,𝐔}(0) = 0 algebraically: F(0, a′) = a′ gives M^𝐔_x(0) = id, and the derivative of ½tr_N(M + M^{−1}) at M = id is ½tr_N(Ṁ − Ṁ) = 0. This replaces V4's "each summand is nonnegative on real q", which fails at complex 𝐔. V4's other real-only steps in (iv), "for unitaries ‖U₁⋯U_n − id‖_op ≤ Σ_j‖U_j − id‖_op" and "For real q, M_x(q) is unitary", enter only through M_x at the real U₁.
  - *§c4-10-1 at 𝐔.* In the stencil gauge of a row (the paragraph *Contour Rows in the Joint Domain*, (i)), U₁ = e^{iZ} with ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b/2, so ‖Z(b)‖_op < 1/4 as in (2)(d), and 𝐔 = e^{iZ′} with ‖Z′ − Z‖ ≤ C_BCHϱ₁ by the BCH bound of (2)(d). So ‖Z′‖ ≤ τ_b once C_BCHϱ₁ ≤ τ_b/2, and (c4-10-3), (c4-10-4), the primitive bounds of §c4-10-1 and the row-domain conclusions (i)–(iii) hold at 𝐔, and along the complex paths of (2)(d), with V4's constants, since V4 proves them at complex Z with ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b.
  - *Potentials.* (c5-4-4) is a bound on |𝐕|; it holds at 𝐔 with ω_t = C₀g₀(1+2r)³ under K′, and with ω̃_t once the tilt of (3.12) is added.
- **(3.7) Selected Integrals, Majorants and Vacuum Clusters (Lemmas c5-4-1, c5-4-2, Corollary c5-4-3, Lemmas c5-5-2, c5-5-3 and c5-5R-1–c5-5R-3; §c4-4, §c4-7).** Along the coefficient path, V4 records that the proofs of these results "use the background only through" Lemma c5-4-0(a) (the Hessian bounds and range), (c) (the row charts and primitive bounds) and (d) (the hosts) (the paragraph *Charts and primitives* of the proof of Prop. c5-6-def). For t ∈ [0,1] at U₁ the proofs use, in addition, the shift, through the cutoffs, boxes, reserves and the constant of (c5-4-2).
  - *Inputs at 𝐔.* (a) is (3.1)–(3.5), (c) is (3.6), (d) is field-independent geometry with the potential bound of (3.6) and the tilt of (3.12), and the shift is the real centre m̂₀ ((3.12)). The further steps of these proofs are supplied at 𝐔 below.
  - *Normalisers.* Lemma c5-4-0(c)'s "the exponent of K^Λ_{ξ;t} is real and finite on the open box and K^Λ_{ξ;t} > 0 there, I^Λ_t > 0", Ξ^{f,t}_Λ > 0 (Lemma c5-4-1), the ratio identity of Corollary c5-4-3 and the division by 𝔫_ΛΞ^{f,t}_Λ in Lemma c5-5-2(d) are not used at 𝐔. The extension of (3.10) takes q^t_Λ(𝒜) and w^t_Λ(W) as the cluster series of Corollary c5-4-3 and Lemma c5-5-3(a), and never divides. The constant of (c5-4-2) at 𝐔 is the nonzero constant of (3.12), which cancels.
  - *Cutoffs.* "χ^t_b ≤ 1" and the bound "for b ∈ 𝖯 and real B" (proof of Lemma c5-4-2), the values in {0,1} (property (i) in the proof of Lemma c5-4-1) and the marked differences of Lemma c5-5R-2(b) concern real indicators of the real fluctuation. At 𝐔 the cutoffs are V4's at the real centre m̂₀, so these hold as stated, with the bound |m̂₀_b|_σ ≤ r/4 of (3.12) in place of (c5-4-1).
  - *Densities.* Every modulus of a Gaussian density is paid by (3.4), and the continuity in σ is (3.2).
  - *Parity.* The evenness of the source-free kernel under (B, X) ↦ (−B, −X) (§c4-4-1, Lemma c4-7-2, Lemma c5-5R-2(a)): "the determinant, the quadratic cross term, the compensator, the real cutoffs χ_b, 1−χ_b and the spatial Cauchy integrations are even". This is algebraic and holds for complex H, the complex Gaussian μ_{C_{E₀}} being even. Parity is used at t = 0 and at the reference data, where the cutoffs are even.
  - *Potentials.* The positive host comparison ("After taking absolute values inside the real Mayer t-integral", Lemma c4-7-1; Lemma c5-5-2(c)) uses |e^{ζ𝐕} − 1| ≤ e^{|ζ𝐕|} − 1, which holds for complex 𝐕, the Mayer parameter t_Y ∈ [0,1] being real.
    - It needs |𝐕| ≤ ω(g₀)e^{−κd₀(Y)} and |𝒟_ω𝐕| ≤ ω(g₀)e^{−κd₀(Y)}‖ω‖_∞ (§c4-7-1) at 𝐔 and along the complex paths, which (3.6) gives with K′'s C^∂₀, and |W̃_t| ≤ A_Y for the t-family, which the tilt threshold of (3.12) gives. The Cauchy parameter ζ (§c4-4-1, Lemma c5-5R-2(c), Step 3 of the proof of Lemma c5-5R-3) uses the same bounds, with R_Υ = ω_*/(2ω̃_t) ≥ 1 at 𝐔.
  - *Words.* "All untouched transports are unitary matrices inside the same closed contraction" (§c4-4-2), the fixed unitary N_t of the proof of Lemma c5-5R-3, and the undifferentiated unitary links in the proofs of Lemmas c5-5-7z(b) and c5-5R-1(b): at 𝐔 these carry the factor e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} of (3.8).
  - *Inverse links.* Lemma c5-5-7z(b): "at real points its real-direction derivatives are the adjoints of those of π(L_{n_i})" uses π(L)^{−1} = π(L)^*. At 𝐔, π(L_n)^{−1} = π(U₁(r_n))^{−1}e^{−iπ(a′(r_n))}Π_n^{−1} is bounded directly: its norm by (3.8), and its derivatives by the proof of Lemma c5-5-7z(a), since Π_n^{−1} = e^{−iπ(Φ_c)} is analytic with the same bound C_Π on the complex chart ball.
  - *Real directions.* "take a real background direction ω" (§c4-4-3). For a complex direction ω^c, 𝒟_{ω^c} = 𝒟_{Re ω^c} + i𝒟_{Im ω^c}, since the coefficient is analytic, so the derivative bounds hold with a factor 2.
  - *Positive sums.* The pins and summations of Lemma c4-6-2 and Lemma c5-5-8(a) are over field-independent geometry with positive majorants, and are unchanged.
  - *Cluster series.* Kotecký–Preiss for the vacuum clusters and for the reference group gas uses the activity bounds in absolute value, so the series converge absolutely at 𝐔 and are analytic by (2)(a). The reference data have U° = id (Lemma c5-5-3(b)), so a^Λ_W, S°_Λ, q°_Λ and θ°(W) do not depend on a′.
  - *Conditional estimate.* Lemma c5-4-4 (Helffer–Sjöstrand, which uses real convexity) is not used at 𝐔. The defect term uses 𝓑^Λ_ξ[f_ξ] = F^Λ(1) − F^Λ(0) only through the expansion (c5-5-5) and the bounds of Props. c5-5-9 and c5-5R-4 (proof of Prop. c5-6-def), that is through (3.9).
- **(3.8) Words and Characters.**
  - *Real-only steps:* "| f_ξ| ≤ 1" (proof of Prop. c5-6-def(a)), and the order-zero link factor of Lemma c5-5-7z(a): "for k = 0 the factor is unitary, of norm 1".
  - *Logarithmic norm.* Let X = x + iy with x, y ∈ 𝔤, and V ∈ G. Since π(x) and π(y) are Hermitian, the Hermitian parts of iπ(X) and −iπ(X) are −π(y) and π(y). So ‖e^{±iπ(X)}‖_op ≤ e^{‖π(y)‖_op} ≤ e^{C_πh(y)}, with C_π of Lemma c5-5-7z, and ‖π(e^{iX}V)‖_op ≤ e^{C_πh(y)}.
  - *Links.* At 𝐔 a traversal link is L_n = e^{iq_𝐔(x)(r_n)}𝐔(r_n), the formula of Lemma c5-5-7z(a) at the background 𝐔, with x := Ψ_ξ(a+A_e) on F_{r_n}. The input x is real and independent of the background: the row of r_n lies within 2R_c + 2L of I (Lemma c5-5-7z), Ψ_ξ is the identity off S_1^*, and d(I, S_1^*) ≥ LMR_1 − 2 > 2R_c + 2L ((c-collar-distance); Section branch-data).
  - *The imaginary part of the phase.* Work in the stencil gauge of the row c of r_n (the paragraph *Contour Rows in the Joint Domain* of §c4-10-1). There U₁ = e^{iZ} and 𝐔 = e^{iZ′} on 𝓔_c, with ‖Z′ − Z‖ ≤ C_BCH‖a′‖_{𝓔_c} ((3.6), *§c4-10-1 at 𝐔*), so Z_t := Z + t(Z′ − Z) has ‖Z_t‖ ≤ τ_b for 0 ≤ t ≤ 1. The phase is gauge covariant with Ad-invariant norms (same paragraph), and Ad_u commutes with taking imaginary parts for u ∈ G.
    - q_Z(x) is real. The traversal inputs have h(x_b) ≤ C_inp(1+r) ((iii) of that paragraph), so g₀‖𝖢_{Z_t}x‖ < θ₀/2 by the fourth condition of (c4-10-row-domain), and (c4-10-4) holds at Z_t ((3.6)).
    - Integrating D_Zq along the segment gives h_ℂ(q_{Z′}(x)(r_n) − q_Z(x)(r_n)) ≤ C_qg₀(1+r)‖a′‖_{𝓔_c}, with C_q := C·C_BCH and C the constant of (c4-10-4). So h(Im q_𝐔(x)(r_n)) ≤ ‖a′‖_{𝓔_c} once C_qg₀(1+r) ≤ 1, a g₀-threshold.
  - *Link norms.* Write 𝓔(r_n) := 𝓔_c, which contains r_n. With 𝐔(r_n) = e^{ia′(r_n)}U₁(r_n), the logarithmic norm bounds ‖π(L_n)‖ and ‖π(L_n)^{−1}‖ = ‖π(U₁(r_n))^{−1}e^{−iπ(a′(r_n))}e^{−iπ(q_𝐔(x)(r_n))}‖ by exp(C_U‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)}), with C_U := 2C_π.
  - *Link derivatives.* The proof of Lemma c5-5-7z(a) writes π(L_n) = Π_n(g₀𝖢_Ua)π(U₁(r_n)) and differentiates Π_n alone, by Cauchy's estimate on the complex chart ball, where ‖Π_n‖ ≤ C_Π. At 𝐔 the same proof runs with π(𝐔(r_n)) in place of π(U₁(r_n)) and K′'s constants ((3.6)), so the bound (C_linkg₀)^k gains the factor ‖π(𝐔(r_n))‖ ≤ e^{C_U‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)}}.
  - *Words.* An ordered product of links and inverse links in which each traversal occurs at most once has norm at most exp(C_UΣ_n‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)}), the sum over its traversals. So the words U_j = π(P_{W_j}G_j) of Lemma c5-5-7(b) have ‖U_j(𝐔)‖ ≤ exp(C_UΣ_{n∈U_j}‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)}), and |f_ξ(m̂₀)(𝐔)| ≤ exp(C_UΣ_{n∈ℐ_C}‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)}) ≤ e^{C_Un_Cϱ₁}. χ is normalised, |χ(X)| ≤ ‖π(X)‖_op, so no factor dim π enters. Both bounds are 1 at a′ = 0.
    - The factor grows like e^{C_Un_Cϱ₁} with the number n_C of traversals. So D1(iii)′ states it in this product form, and D2 may not use a uniform bound of f_ξ over the polydisc ((6)).
    - The words U_j of (c5-5-7), the gaps G_j included, carry this product-form factor over their own traversals. It is not charged to the group majorants.
  - *Groups.* In a group's coefficient c_W the transports form its closed character χ(T_WP_W^{−1}h) and sub-words of it with fixed insertions: "All untouched transports are unitary matrices inside the same closed contraction" (§c4-4-2), and Lemma c5-5-7z(b). T_W and P_W are products over the traversals of ℋ(W) (Lemma c5-5-7), so these words have at most 2|ℋ(W)| link factors. The s-derivative of the source slot in Lemma c5-5-7(d), tr(π(T_W)N′_s) with N′_s = −π(P^s_W)^{−1}(∂_sπ(P^s_W))π(P^s_W)^{−1}π(h), has at most 4|ℋ(W)|. Also |ℋ(W)| ≤ LM𝔰(W), since 𝔰(W) = n(W) + |ℋ(W)|/(LM) ((c4-6-1)).
    - So at 𝐔 each of these words has norm at most e^{4C_U|ℋ(W)|ϱ₁} ≤ e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}, with C′ := 4C_ULM, in place of 1. By the link derivatives above, the derivative bounds built from them (Lemmas c4-4-3, c4-9-1, c5-5-7z(b) and c5-5-7(d)) gain the same factor.
  - *Absorption.* At 𝐔 a group's majorant carries e^{θ°(W)}e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}. With θ°(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 = η𝔰(W)/16 (proof of Prop. c5-6-def(a), step (i): ν = η and σ = 𝔰) and the g₀-threshold C′ϱ₁ ≤ η/8, this is at most e^{3η𝔰(W)/16}. The pin weight e^{4η𝔰} of §3a(6) then leaves the rate 61/16:
    - for good groups, with k = ⌊𝔰⌋, 𝔰³e^{3η𝔰/16} ≤ (k+1)³e^{3η/16}e^{−(61/16)ηk}e^{4η𝔰}, and 𝔰e^{3η𝔰/16} ≤ (k+1)e^{3η/16}e^{−(61/16)ηk}e^{4η𝔰} for the extra term of (2)(d);
    - for the other groups, 𝔰 ≥ s_n gives e^{3η𝔰/16} ≤ e^{−(61/16)ηs_n}e^{4η𝔰}.
    This is the rate of δ*^c_n, a*_n and c in (0). §3a(7) keeps its rate, since σe^{3ησ/16} ≤ η^{−1}e^{(19/16)ησ} ≤ η^{−1}e^{4ησ−ησ}.
- **(3.9) The Exterior Part (Lemmas c5-5-4–c5-5-8; Props. c5-5-9 and c5-5R-4).** At 𝐔 the defect term is expanded at the centre m̂₀ with the tilted host potentials W̃_t of (3.12). The proof of Prop. c5-5-9 divides (c5-5-5) by S°_Λ, which does not depend on a′ and is nonzero ((3.7) *Cluster series*), and bounds the shift term, the background-word part and the marked part. Put a′ := 0 off Ω₁, c_n := C_U‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)} and E_C(a′) := exp(Σ_{n∈ℐ_C}c_n) ≤ e^{C_Un_Cϱ₁}, the product-form factor of (3.8).
  - *Algebraic steps.* Burnside (Lemma c5-5-7(a)) concerns End(V_π) only. The factorisation (b) is multilinearity of the trace and Fubini; π extends holomorphically to the complexified group, so π(P^t_W)^{−1} exists at 𝐔. The telescoping identities in the proofs of Lemma c5-5-7(c), (d) and Lemma c5-5-6(a) are identities. So these hold at 𝐔 as stated.
  - *Words.* V4's unitarity steps are ‖U^t_j‖ = 1, ‖N_t‖ = ‖N_s‖ = 1 and "the links are unitary and the word is their ordered product, so the difference is at most the sum of the link differences" (proofs of Lemma c5-5-7(c), (d)). At 𝐔, telescoping gives ‖Π_iX_i − Π_iY_i‖ ≤ e^{Σ_ic_i}Σ_i‖X_i − Y_i‖ when max(‖X_i‖, ‖Y_i‖) ≤ e^{c_i}. The link norms are at most e^{c_n} ((3.8) *Link norms*), and ‖π(L_n(m̂₀)) − π(L_n(0))‖ ≤ e^{c_n}C_linkg₀Σ_{b∈𝓕_{r_n}}h(m̂₀_b) ((3.8) *Link derivatives*, along the segment sm̂₀, which lies in the box of radius r/4 by (3.12)).
    - The intervals and gaps partition the traversals of C, so (c5-5-7) holds at 𝐔 with the factor E_C(a′) on its right side.
    - The words of a group carry e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} ((3.8) *Groups*), so (c5-5-8) and Lemma c5-5-7(e) hold at 𝐔 with this factor on their right sides. Write 𝔟̄_W(𝐔) := e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}(M_Λ(W) + |a^Λ_W|).
  - *Shift term (Lemma c5-5-4).* f_ξ(m̂₀) − f_ξ(0) = ∫_0^1Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m̂₀_b)∂_{e_b,b}f_ξ(sm̂₀)ds, e_b := m̂₀_b/h(m̂₀_b), holds at 𝐔. In place of the real bound (c3-2-3), ∂_{e,b}f_ξ is a sum over the at most N_tr traversals with input b of the normalised trace of the contour word with that link differentiated, so |∂_{e,b}f_ξ| ≤ N_trC_linkg₀E_C(a′) by (3.8) and |χ(X)| ≤ ‖π(X)‖_op. So Lemma c5-5-4 holds at 𝐔 with C_resp,χ replaced by (C′_χ/2)E_C(a′).
  - *Marks (Lemmas c5-5-5, c5-5-6).* The proof of Lemma c5-5-6(a) takes moduli. At 𝐔 the modulus of the density is (|det H_E|/det R_E)^{1/2}e^{−½Re⟨ΓX,CΓX⟩}e^{−⟨B,Γ₁X⟩}dμ_{R_E^{−1}}(B)dμ₀(X) ((3.4) *The B-integral*): a real centred Gaussian in B with precision R_E and the real tilt v := Γ₁X. Lemma c5-5-5 is a statement about such a Gaussian. Its proof needs (R_E)_bb − α ≥ γ − s − α_G ≥ γ/2 and (R_E)_bb ≤ h_+ + s ≤ h_c ((3.1); α_G ≤ γ_c/2 under K), so it holds with V4's ϖ_b, computed with h_c under K, and S_b at m̂₀. The remaining B- and X-integrals are paid by e^{N₁(Z)} ((3.4) *Result*).
    - The host factors at 𝐔 are the W̃_t = Θ^t_{E(Y)}P̃_t of (3.12), with |P̃_t| ≤ ω̄̃ on the box. With real t_Y the undifferenced Mayer factors satisfy |M^t_Y| ≤ |W̃_t|e^{|W̃_t|} ≤ ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}, by ω̃_t ≤ ω_*/2. In a host mark without exterior input, ∂_tP̃_t = Σ_𝔭D𝔭(B + tm̂₀)[m̂₀|_{E(Y)}] − L_Y(δ^𝐔, B): the first term gives V4's second 𝔪_Y with h(m̂₀_b) and ω̄̃, the second the tilt mark 𝔪^δ_Y of (3.12). The cutoff part and the dummy comparison use (c5-5-T)(iii) through P̃₀ = 𝐏₀ only, where the tilt vanishes.
    - So Lemma c5-5-6 holds at 𝐔 with 𝔪_b at m̂₀, 𝔪_Y with ω̄̃ and h(m̂₀_b), and 𝔐_Λ(Z) enlarged by Σ_{Y⊆Z}𝔪^δ_Y.
  - *Pins (Lemmas c5-5-8 and c5-5R-3).* e^{θ°(W)}e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} ≤ e^{3ν𝔰(W)/16} ≤ e^{2νσ(W)} ((3.8) *Absorption*; σ = 𝔰, ν = η), and 𝔟̄_W(𝐔) ≤ 2e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}M_Λ(W) (Lemma c5-5-3(d)). So the proof of Lemma c5-5-8 gives (a) and (b) at 𝐔 with 𝔟̄_W(𝐔) and the same ε̄_t, and likewise Lemma c5-5R-3's pins with 𝔟̄^ph_W(𝐔) := e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}·2M^ph_Λ(W) and ε̄_ph at 𝐔 ((3.12) *Order*).
  - *Result.* The proofs of Props. c5-5-9 and c5-5R-4 run at 𝐔 and give (c5-5-9) and (c5-5-9R) with the factor E_C(a′) on their right sides, at the rates and constants of §3j(5) at m̂, with C_resp,χ replaced by C′_χ/2 in C₁ and the tilt marks of (3.12) added to C₂. §3a(7) holds at 𝐔 with h(m̂₀_b) and with 𝔪^δ_Y among the marks 𝔪̂_Δ, at its rate ((3.8) *Absorption*).
- **(3.10) The Extension and Its Identification.**
  - *Definition.* For every group, c_W(𝐔; h) is V4's coefficient formula at the background 𝐔, with the definitions V4 gives for a generic noncritical background on the coefficient path: the energy primitive with its constant, linear and quadratic Taylor terms subtracted, and the Haar and constraint-Jacobian primitives by their analytic row formulas ("These definitions specify an analytic coefficient extension", the paragraph after (c4-9-1)).
    - Its other ingredients are the selected integrals with H_𝐔, the charts of (3.6), the cluster series of (3.7), and on the t-family the centre m̂₀ and the tilt of (3.12). The regional data I, Ω₁, D(Λ), the cutoffs and the labels are held fixed, as on V4's path ("exactly the same I, Ω₁, W, cutoffs and constants", §c4-11-3).
    - Υ¹_W(𝐔) is built from these coefficients by Lemma c5-5-7's formula, its norm controlled uniformly in h ∈ G by Burnside (Lemma c5-5-7(a)). By (2)(a), (3.1)–(3.9) and (3.12) it is analytic on the ϱ₁-polydisc.
    - At a′ = 0 it is V4's coefficient at U₁: "at the physical endpoint each compensated primitive agrees with its Taylor-subtracted formula by locality, criticality being used in the primitive-sum identity (native-source-primitive-sum) for the physical exponent" (same paragraph). No criticality is used at 𝐔.
  - *Covariance.* The proof of (c4-11-5) (§c4-11-2) uses the intertwinings of the row formulas with R_g, the uniqueness of the small row solution, the invariance of the plaquette actions and the Haar density, the transformation of the Taylor coefficients "even at noncritical backgrounds", and the change of variables B ↦ R_gB, X ↦ R_gX. For G-valued g, with 𝐔 ↦ 𝐔^g and a′ ↦ Ad_g a′ ((2)(b)), each step holds at 𝐔.
    - The intertwinings are identities of the formulas, and the small row solution is unique in the complex ball of (3.6). R_g is real orthogonal, so it pushes the complex Gaussian μ_{C_{E₀}} forward, C_{E₀} transforming by R_g. The cutoff norms are Ad-invariant ((marginal-cutoff-invariance)).
    - m̂₀ and δ^𝐔 transform by R_g, since P, K, ℓ_Λ and H_𝐔 do and λ is a constant ((3.12)), and L_Y is Ad-invariant. So (c4-11-5) holds at 𝐔.
  - *Path endpoint.* For a good group passing the phase test, the formula at 𝐔 equals the formula at e^{iB^c_W}, the path endpoint of (2)(d), by V4's two steps for (c4-11-6). First, (c4-11-5) with the real rooted gauge g_W, g_W(o) = id, gives the formula at 𝐔^{g_W}.
    - Second, coefficient locality on Q_W (§c4-10-2: every surviving walk and stencil lies in its declared domains, and the seed inverse is the inverse on the seed; §c4-11-4: every consulted background bond has both endpoints in Q_W) gives the formula at the background equal to 𝐔^{g_W} on Q_W and id elsewhere, which is e^{iB^c_W}. Both steps are properties of the formula at every background, so the identity holds at 𝐔.
  - *Other groups.* For the other groups, and when the phase test fails, only the direct formula is used, with the endpoint majorant |c_W(𝐔; h)| ≤ e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) ((3.11)).
- **(3.11) The Remaining Uses (§c4-10-4, Lemma c4-9-2, §c4-12; the Order of K; Uncovered Terms).**
  - *§c4-10-4.* "Only the source f_W(a;h) = χ(T_W(a)P_W^{−1}h), or the original full closed Wilson character, is used (a normalised character, so |f| ≤ 1); untouched factors are unitary in its representation." At 𝐔 the word bounds of (3.8) replace this: (c4-10-8)–(c4-10-10) hold with the factor e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} for f_W and E_C(a′) ((3.9)) for the full character. Their derivative constants (c4-10-4) hold at complex Z with ‖Z‖ ≤ τ_b ((3.6)).
  - *§c4-12-1.* (c4-12-1) is built from Lemmas c4-7-1 and c4-7-2 ((3.7) *Parity*, *Potentials*), (c4-10-9) and (c4-10-10) (above), (c4-10-6) and (c4-10-14) ((3.5)), the vacuum-cluster derivative (c4-7-8) ((3.7) *Cluster series*, *Real directions*) and the product rule (c4-8-10), an identity.
    - So at 𝐔 and along the complex paths, (c4-12-1) holds with C_χe^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} in place of C_χ, and (c4-12-2) and its D(Λ) form (proof of Prop. c5-6-def, paragraph *Parity and the source*) give |c_W| ≤ e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) and |𝒟_ωc_W| ≤ 2C_χe^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}‖ω‖_∞(n(W) + |ℋ(W)|)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W). The factor 2 is the complex direction ((3.7) *Real directions*).
  - *§c4-12-2.* It uses δ_{Q_W}(U₁) ≤ Cε₁ and the phase test, both at the real U₁; the path integral (c4-9-2) with (c4-12-2) along it, which at 𝐔 is (2)(d); the endpoint identities (c4-11-6), which at 𝐔 are (3.10); and the pins of Lemmas c4-8-2 and c4-8-3, over positive majorants ((3.7) *Positive sums*). The weight e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} is paid by (3.8) *Absorption*.
  - *Lemma c4-9-2.* Its proof uses positive majorants, Kotecký–Preiss for the reference activities |a_W| ≤ M(W), which do not depend on a′, the walk-derivative series of §c4-10-2 ((3.5)) and "the uniform Gaussian coercivity (Section c4-10-3)" ((3.5), with c_G^K). So termwise differentiation and t-integration hold along the complex paths.
  - *The order of K.* After its choice α_G enters only through upper bounds (Lemma gaussian-payment, Lemma c5-5-5, (c4-10-13)), through factors e^{(α_G/2)h(B_b)²} paid by Lemma gaussian-payment(ii), and through a_bad = c_nα_Gr²/4 in conditions on g₀ alone (items 4(i), (ii) and (vi) of Section app:thresholds, (c5-4-Tp), (c5-5R-T)), which hold for small g₀ at every fixed α_G > 0 since r = A₁(log g₀^{−2})^{p₀} → ∞.
    - V4's conditions on M are lower bounds or arithmetic: M₁ < M₂ < M odd powers of L, M/M₁ integral, M ≥ 8LM₂, (fixed-kernel-collar-size), CRE's "M be large" (p.245), and the δ_M conditions, which hold for large M. So no later choice bounds α_G from below or M from above, as (3.0) uses.
  - *Uncovered terms.* On the uncovered terms of §3d, the preamble of Lemma c5-5-7z is replaced by §3d(1)(d): the row input a + Ψ_ξ(A_e) lies in 𝒰_c, with ‖A‖ ≤ ϑ_*/4. a′ acts on the background only ((0)), so the row input stays real and background-independent, and (3.8)'s link bounds hold there with the same proof.

- **(3.12) The Shift at 𝐔.**
  - *The centre.* At 𝐔 the term is expanded at a = B + tm̂₀, with the real centre m̂₀ := m̂(U₁) of §3j(1), built at the real U₁ and the term's real exterior data (D26 closure 6.4 *The centre*). The translation is exact for every real centre with |m̂₀_b|_σ ≤ r/4, and |m̂₀_b|_σ ≤ r/4 holds by §3j(1). So the cutoffs χ^t_b(B) = 𝟏{|B_b + tm̂₀_b|_σ < r}, the boxes, the reserves, the shells S_b and the bond marks are V4's at the real centre (§3j(4)), and none depends on a′.
  - *The completed square.* Write H := H_{𝐔,DD}, symmetric by (1)(f), and ℓ_𝐔 := H_{𝐔,DJ}A^{Λ,Ψ}, with bilinear pairings. Then

    −½⟨a,Ha⟩ − t⟨a,ℓ_𝐔⟩ = −½⟨B,HB⟩ − t⟨B,δ^𝐔⟩ − t²(½⟨m̂₀,Hm̂₀⟩ + ⟨m̂₀,ℓ_𝐔⟩),   δ^𝐔 := Hm̂₀ + ℓ_𝐔 = δ̂ + δℓ,

    with δ̂ := H_{U₁,DD}m̂₀ + ℓ_{U₁} the real tilt of §3j(3) and δℓ := (H_𝐔 − H_{U₁})_{DD}m̂₀ + (H_𝐔 − H_{U₁})_{DJ}A^{Λ,Ψ}, which vanishes at a′ = 0.
  - *The constant.* e^{−t²(½⟨m̂₀,Hm̂₀⟩ + ⟨m̂₀,ℓ_𝐔⟩)} is a nonzero number, the same in the numerator and the denominator of every ratio (c5-4-3) and in Ξ^{f,t}_Λ. V4 never estimates its counterpart e^{½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} in (c5-4-2), and the extension of (3.10) uses normalised quantities only through cluster series ((3.7) *Normalisers*). So it cancels.
  - *The size of δℓ.* An entry H(b,b′) vanishes for d(b,b′) > ρ_L at every background, its entry stencil being empty (Lemma hessian-gauge-locality), and the entries of H_𝐔 − H_{U₁} have norm at most C_eϱ₁ ((3.1)). With h(m̂₀(b′)) ≤ 2C_mϑrΣ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b′,e)} (§3j(5)) and J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ), h(A^{Λ,Ψ}_e) ≤ |A^{Λ,Ψ}_e|_σ < ϑr on J_col(Λ) (Lemma c5-3-1(b)), which contains every e ∈ J(Λ) within ρ_L of D(Λ), d(b′,e) ≥ d(b,e) − ρ_L and at most 4(2ρ_L+1)⁴ bonds b′ with d(b,b′) ≤ ρ_L (proof of Lemma c5-5-1),

    h_ℂ(δℓ(b)) ≤ C_δϱ₁ϑrΣ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)},   C_δ := C_ee^{μ_xρ_L}(8(2ρ_L+1)⁴C_m + 1).

  - *The tilt in the host potentials.* Each b ∈ D(Λ) has its probe host Y_b ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ), with b ∈ E(Y_b) (Lemma c5-4-0(d), (c-probe-host)). With the probe L_Y(λ,B) := Σ_{b:Y_b=Y}⟨λ_b,B_b⟩ of V4 Section c5-5 (before Lemma c5-5-2), put P̃_t(Y,B) := 𝐏_t(Y,B) − tL_Y(δ^𝐔,B) and W̃_t(Y,B) := Θ^t_{E(Y)}(B)P̃_t(Y,B), with V4's 𝐏_t(Y,B) = Σ_{𝔭 near, Y_𝔭=Y}𝔭(B + tm̂₀ + tA^{Λ,Ψ}) at 𝐔 (proof of Lemma c5-5-6(a)). On the support of Θ^t every box factor is 1, so Σ_YW̃_t = Σ_Y𝐖_t − t⟨B,δ^𝐔⟩ there and the expansion is exact. The Gaussian stays centred with the complex H, as (3.4) needs. At real backgrounds this is §3j(4)'s tilt.
  - *Bounds.* W̃_t(Y,·) depends on B|_{E(Y)} only and vanishes off the translated local box, where |B_b|_σ < r + |m̂₀_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4; so V4's properties (i)–(iii) of the proof of Lemma c5-4-1 hold, as V4 records for 𝐖_t + L_Y before Lemma c5-5-2. For real x, |⟨x,y⟩| ≤ h(x)h_ℂ(y) ≤ |x|_σh_ℂ(y) (h ≤ |·|_σ, proof of Lemma c5-5-7z(b)), and at most C_M bonds b have Y_b = Y ((c5-5R-T)).
    - So the complex part of the tilt is at most τ̄ := (5/4)C_MC_δN_lat(μ_x)ϱ₁ϑr² and its real part at most τ̂_N (§3j(4)). Hence |W̃_t(Y,B)| ≤ ω̃_te^{−κd₀(Y)} and |P̃_t| ≤ ω̄̃ on the box, with ω̃_t := ω_t + e^{κs_p}(τ̄ + τ̂_N) and ω̄̃ := ω̄ + e^{κs_p}(τ̄ + τ̂_N), since d₀(Y) < s_p = 5⁴ for every host (§3j(4) *Host bound*; a probe host may carry the tilt alone).
  - *Tilt threshold.* Items 4(ii) and (iii) of Section app:thresholds ((c5-5-T)(ii), (iii); (c5-5R-T)(ii)) are required with ω̃_t and ω̄̃ in place of ω_t and ω̄ at t > 0. They are g₀-thresholds: ω_t, ω̄ and τ̂_N tend to 0 with g₀ (§3j(6)), and τ̄ ≤ Cϱ₁r² with ϱ₁r² ≍ g₁(log g₁^{−2})^{q₁}A₁²(log g₀^{−2})^{2p₀} → 0. Then Lemmas c5-4-2 and c4-7-1 and §c5-5, §c5-5R apply at 𝐔 with W̃_t ((3.7), (3.9)). The second branch of (iii) enters only through P̃₀ = 𝐏₀ and is unchanged.
  - *Tilt marks.* On the support of Θ^t the t-derivative of the tilt part is −L_Y(δ^𝐔,B), of modulus at most (5/4)rΣ_{b:Y_b=Y}h_ℂ(δ^𝐔(b)). In a host mark without exterior input it adds, as for V4's second 𝔪_Y,

    𝔪^δ_Y := (5/4)r(1+ω̄̃)e^{ω̄̃}e^{κs_p}e^{1/8}ω_*^{−1}Σ_{b:Y_b=Y}h_ℂ(δ^𝐔(b)) ≤ Ĉ^δ_bd rΣ_{b:Y_b=Y}h_ℂ(δ^𝐔(b)),

    with §3j(4)'s Ĉ^δ_bd = (5/2)e^{1+κs_p+1/8}/ω_*, by ω̄̃ ≤ 1. With h_ℂ(δ^𝐔(b)) ≤ h(δ̂(b)) + h_ℂ(δℓ(b)), §3j(5)'s bound on δ̂ and J₁(Λ) ⊆ J_col(Λ), 𝔪^δ_Y ≤ Ĉ^δ_bd(C_she^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}g₀ + C_δϱ₁)ϑr²Σ_{b:Y_b=Y}Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}. This is the form of V4's host marks without exterior input at rate μ_x, small in the coupling, and the tilt marks enter Prop. c5-5-9 as those marks do ((3.9)). At a′ = 0 they are §3j(4)'s tilt marks.
  - *Order.* At 𝐔, ρ_ph is V4's with ω̃_t in place of ω_t: the class-(β) factor of Step 3 of the proof of Lemma c5-5R-3 is 2ω̃_t/ω_*, with R_Υ := ω_*/(2ω̃_t) in Lemma c5-5R-2(c). Since g₁ ≤ (1+β₀)g₀ (CRE (2.6), p.255) and x(log x^{−2})^{q₁} increases for small x, ϱ₁ ≤ C₁(1+β₀)g₀(log g₀^{−2})^{q₁}/L. With r = A₁(log g₀^{−2})^{p₀} and r²/(1+2r)³ ≤ 1/(8r), ρ_tτ̄ ≤ Cg₀²(1+2r)⁴(log g₀^{−2})^{q₁−p₀}, and ρ_tτ̂_N ≤ Cg₀²(1+2r)⁴ (§3j(6)). So ρ_ph ≤ C^𝐔_phg₀²(1+2r)⁴(1 + (log g₀^{−2})^{(q₁−p₀)₊}) at 𝐔, and ε̄_ph = 2K_χC_pinρ_ph with it.

**(4) Result.** Assume repairs K and K′ ((3.0), (3.6)), and let g₀ satisfy, besides V4's thresholds with the constants of K and K′:
- C_Hϱ₁ ≤ s_* ((3.4));
- ϱ₁ ≤ r_H/2, with r_H = min(r_e, r_iv) ((2)(c), (3.1), (3.6));
- C_BCHϱ₁ ≤ τ_b/2 ((2)(d), (3.6));
- C′ϱ₁ ≤ η/8 and C_qg₀(1+r) ≤ 1 ((3.8));
- the tilt threshold of (3.12).

Each is a condition on g₀ alone: the constants are fixed before the coupling, and ϱ₁ ≤ C₁(1+β₀)g₀(log g₀^{−2})^{q₁}/L ((3.12) *Order*), g₀(1+r)³, ϱ₁r² and τ̂_N tend to 0 with g₀. Then D1(iii)′ holds on every labelled term, covered or not (§3d(2); (3.11) *Uncovered terms*): for real U₁ in the support of the term's indicators and ‖a′‖_∞ < ϱ₁,
- Υ¹_W, the words U_j and f_ξ(m̂₀) are analytic in a′ and G-covariant ((3.10));
- D1(ii) holds in the per-traversal form of §3a(8), with the curvature term C_χρ[δ*^c_n(U₁) + a*_n(a′)], the large-field and wrapping terms at the rate (61/16)η ((0), (3.8)), and the exterior marks of §3a(7) at their rate, including 𝔪^δ_Y ((3.9)); the root weight max(ρ, ρ_ph) has ρ_ph as in (3.12) *Order*;
- ‖U_j(𝐔)‖ ≤ exp(Σ_{n∈U_j}c_n) and |f_ξ(m̂₀)(𝐔)| ≤ E_C(a′) ((3.8), (3.9));
- |c_W(𝐔;h)| ≤ e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) and |𝒟_ωc_W| ≤ 2C_χe^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁}‖ω‖_∞(n(W) + |ℋ(W)|)M^{(ρ)}_Λ(W) on the polydisc and along the complex paths, in complex directions ((3.11));
- (c5-5-9) and (c5-5-9R) hold with the factor E_C(a′) on their right sides ((3.9)).

The constants are V4's under K and K′ and those defined in (1)–(3), independent of n, the torus and the labelled term.

**(5) Count Check.**
- A_n(R) ≤ ‖a′‖_∞ < ϱ₁ and Σ_{k≥1}(k+1)e^{−(61/16)ηk} < ∞, so a*_n(a′) ≤ C_ηϱ₁. With δ*^c_n ≤ C_ηε₁ (§3a(6) *Count check*, at the rate 61/16), the curvature term is at most C_χρC_η(ε₁ + ϱ₁) on the polydisc; ε₁ and ϱ₁ are both g₁ times a power of log g₁^{−2}.
- The other costs at 𝐔 are e^{C′𝔰(W)ϱ₁} ≤ e^{η𝔰(W)/8} per group, e^{C₄s|J_Z|} ≤ e^{N₁(Z)/4} per component, and e^{κs_p}τ̄ per host inside ω̃_t ≤ ω_*/2. None is a volume factor: the global Hessian enters only through the local det C_{E₀} ((3.3), (3.4)).
- *Tilt marks per traversal.* Each bond has one probe host, so by (3.12) the tilt marks sum to at most Ĉ^δ_bd(C_she^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}g₀ + C_δϱ₁)ϑr²Σ_{b∈D(Λ)}Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}, against the weights e^{−ηdist(Δ,n)/(C_FLM)} of §3a(7). As in the proof of Prop. c5-5-9 (ν = η), passing from the assigned cube to the host costs C_dist, and from the host to a bond b ∈ E(Y) costs e^{νR_st/(C_FLM)}; no factor N_host enters, since each bond has one probe host.
  - The splitting of (c5-5-2) with X = {n} and μ = η/(C_FLM) bounds the sum by C(g₀ + ϱ₁)ϑr²N_lat(μ_x/2)Σ_{e∈J_col(Λ)}e^{−min(μ_x/2, η/(C_FLM))dist(e,n)}: (g₀ + ϱ₁)r² times the collar decay.

**(6) Words on the Polydisc.** At 𝐔 the words U_j and f_ξ(m̂₀) have norm up to E_C(a′) ≤ e^{C_Un_Cϱ₁} ((4)), which grows with the perimeter. So no estimate takes their supremum over the whole ϱ₁-polydisc; their Cauchy estimates are local, as follows.
- *(a) Cauchy estimates in a bounded set of variables.* Let F be f_ξ(m̂₀) or a word U_j, analytic in a′ on {‖a′‖_∞ < ϱ₁} with |F(a′)| ≤ exp(C_UΣ_{n∈C_F}‖a′‖_{𝓔(r_n)}) ((3.8) *Words*; C_F the traversals of C for f_ξ and of U_j for U_j; operator norm for words). Fix an h-orthonormal basis (T_k) of 𝔤, so that h_ℂ(Σ_kz_kT_k) ≤ √d_G max_k|z_k|. Fix a′₀ with ‖a′₀‖_∞ < ϱ₁, a set S of fine bonds and 0 < r < ϱ₁ − ‖a′₀‖_∞, and put D_S := {a′₀ + z : z(b) = Σ_kz_k(b)T_k, z = 0 off S, |z_k(b)| ≤ r/√d_G}.
  - On D_S, ‖a′₀ + z‖_∞ < ϱ₁ and ‖a′₀ + z‖_{𝓔(r_n)} ≤ ‖a′₀‖_{𝓔(r_n)} + r𝟏{𝓔(r_n) ∩ S ≠ ∅}. So sup_{D_S}|F| ≤ exp(C_UΣ_{n∈C_F}‖a′₀‖_{𝓔(r_n)})e^{C_Urν_S}, with ν_S := #{n ∈ C_F : 𝓔(r_n) ∩ S ≠ ∅}.
  - Cauchy's estimate in the |S|d_G variables z_k(b) gives |∂^αF(a′₀)| ≤ α!(√d_G/r)^{|α|}sup_{D_S}|F|. At a real base point, a′₀ = 0, the first factor is 1, so derivatives in the variables of S cost α!(√d_G/r)^{|α|}e^{C_Urν_S}.
  - ν_S ≤ N_C|S|, with N_C := max_b#{n ∈ ℐ_C : b ∈ 𝓔(r_n)}. A bond b lies in 𝓔_c for boundedly many rows c (the definition of N_tr in Lemma c5-5-7z), and the traversals whose row is c have distinct bonds r_n ∈ 𝓔_c, since the square traverses each link once, so there are at most n_𝓔 of them (Lemma c2-fibre-smallness). So N_C depends on L only, not on n_C.
- *(b) Fluctuation integrals.* Inside a Gaussian fluctuation integral the loop is evaluated at real fluctuation fields; the complex parameters (the forest interpolation parameters and §3i's box parameters s) go into the density, as in V4 ("No hard cutoff or Wilson function is differentiated", the paragraph after (marginal-forest-identity)) and in §3i(5). So the loop's bounds there are those of the real fluctuation: |f_ξ| ≤ 1 and ‖U^t_j‖ = 1 at real backgrounds (proof of Lemma c5-5-7(c)), and (a) at 𝐔.
- *(c) Use in D2.* Every Cauchy estimate that D2 applies to a term containing the loop is taken on a polydisc D_S in the variables of a set S charged to that term (its group, its polymer, or the sites it differentiates). |S| is paid by the term's own weight, and (a) gives the factor e^{C_UrN_C|S|}, never e^{C_Un_Cϱ₁}.

## 3f. Proofs for §3c(4‴)–(4⁗): The Bad-Cube Factor and the Construction Check

**(0) Source facts.**
- *p₀ and 𝔯.* CRE p.246 l.14–15: "ε₀ = g₀p₀(g₀), p₀(g₀) = A₀(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}, p₀ ≥ 5r and A₀ is a sufficiently large constant", where CRE's r is the exponent 𝔯 of R_j ≥ (log g_j⁻²)^𝔯 (CRE (2.5)). LFR II (1.88), p.387, imposes 2(1+β₀)⁻¹p₀(g_{j+1}) ≥ O(1)3(100M)^dR_{j+1}^{d+2} "for p₀ large"; this forces p₀ > (d+2)𝔯 = 6𝔯, which is stronger than p₀ ≥ 5𝔯.
- *The large-field factors.* LFR II p.380 l.31–47 gives, for the large-field functions "introduced in the j^th step" (volumes for the L^{−j}-scale, l.36):
  - χ_j^c(P_j) yields exp(−γ₀(2g_j²)⁻¹(B₃⁻¹ε_j)²(LM₂R_j)^{−d}|P_j|), "using Theorem 1 [15] and (71) [16]";
  - χ_j′^c(Q_j) yields exp(−½γ₀g_j⁻²(2δ_j)²(LM₂R_j)^{−d}|Q_j|), "by expanding the Wilson action locally in the approximate fluctuation field, and using the positivity bound for the resulting quadratic form";
  - χ^{(j−1)c}(R_j) yields exp(−γ₀g_j⁻²δ_j²(LM₂R_j)^{−d}|R_j|), "by the same positivity bound for the quadratic form";
  - [15] is VAR and [16] is UV3 (LFR II p.392). UV3 (71), p.273, gets the factor exp(−¼p²(g_j)) of its curvature sets from a lower bound of the action on the four j-blocks around a large plaquette.
- *Volume and linear size.* p.380 l.25–29: every large-field domain Z_j contributes O(1)log g_j⁻²|Z_j| ≤ O(1)log g_j⁻²(MR_j)^dd_j′(Z_j), with d_j′ the linear size in MR_j-cubes. Since d′ can be 0, the note uses |Z_j| = (MR_j)^d#cubes(Z_j) and #cubes ≤ 3·2^{d−1}(d′+1) (D26 §1 *Counting cubes*).
- *(1.76).* p.381 l.11–13: Z_j ⊂ Z′_{j−1}^{∼10} ∪ ⋃_i(Z_j^{(i)})^{∼2}, where the Z_j^{(i)} are the components of the MR_j-cube cover of P_j ∪ Q_j ∪ R_j (p.380 l.47–49). For j = 1, p.385 l.15–16 states it per component Z of Z₁: Z ⊂ ⋃_i(Z₁^{(i)})^{∼2}. The transcription cites (1.76) on pp.383, 385 and 386 only.

**(1) Positivity and Local Gaps.**
- *Notation.* H := H_ξ, the operator on 𝔤^{Ω₁*} of V4 Section c5-0, for V4's bondwise inner product, whose norm on 𝔤 is h; |z| is the norm of z ∈ 𝔤^{Ω₁*}. d(b,b′) is the ℓ^∞ distance of the initial sites in fine units (V4 Lemma c5-1-1). γ := γ_L/2, ρ := ρ_L = 4L, μ := μ_H = γ_L/(8Lm_L), and ℓ := LMR₁. The cutoff norm |·|_σ, in which V4 reads the norms of CRE's tests (V4 Lemma boundary-bijection), satisfies h ≤ |·|_σ ≤ c_hh and ‖·‖_op ≤ c_h|·|_σ, c_h = √N (V4 Section c5-0; proof of V4 Lemma boundary-bijection). ϑ ≤ 1/16 is V4's (Remark c5-1-3).
- (D-a) *H ≥ 0 on 𝔤^{Ω₁*}.* The critical point U₁ of (1.12) "is a minimum of (1.12), with a strictly positive second order differential" (CRE p.248, Theorem 1 [15]). By V4 (eq:regular-covariance-hessian) with U = U₁, ⟨B,HB⟩ = D²(S+𝒢)(0)[a,a] − DS(0)[h_UD²𝒩_U(0)[a,a]] with a := 𝖢_UB, and H is CRE's C*Δ₁C of (1.15).
  - This is the second derivative at B = 0 of g₀⁻²(S+𝒢)(q_U(B)) along V4's chart B ↦ e^{iq_U(B)}U of the constraint set of (1.12). Indeed q_U(B) = A − h_UD̃_U(A) with A = g₀𝖢_UB (proof of V4 Lemma collar-coverage (iv)), D̃_U(0) = 0 and DD̃_U(0) = 0 (V4 Lemma row-locality), and the quadratic term of D̃_U is ½D²𝒩_U(0) (V4, after (eq:regular-covariance-hessian)). So Dq_U(0)B = g₀a and D²q_U(0)[B,B] = −g₀²h_UD²𝒩_U(0)[a,a]; and D𝒢(0) = 0, since 𝒢 ≥ 0 and 𝒢(0) = 0.
  - At a constrained critical point the second derivative along a parametrisation is the intrinsic second differential, so H ≥ 0. Only H ≥ 0 is used.
- (D-b) *Local gap near I.* Let P ⊂ Ω₁* have all its bonds within ℓ/2 of I. Then every row b ∈ P has Σ_{b′∈Ω₁*}‖H_{bb′}‖ ≤ m_L, and every P′ ⊆ P has (γ_L/2)id ≤ H_{P′P′} ≤ m_L id and ‖H_{P′P′}⁻¹(b,b′)‖ ≤ γ⁻¹e^{−μd(b,b′)}.
  - The proof is V4's proof of Lemma regular-bounds with I replaced by P. That proof uses the position of I three times: the patches 𝔓_w meeting I lie within s_𝔓 + 1 of I, where Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation is applied; the entry stencils lie within 3L of b₋ (Lemma c2-entry-range), hence in Ω₁, as Λ₁ ⊆ Ω₁^{∼−2} (proof of V4 Lemma collar-coverage (i)); and the rectangle 𝔓_{z(b)} of each row b lies within s_𝔓 + 1 of b. With P in place of I, all these bonds lie within ℓ/2 + s_𝔓 + 2 of I.
  - Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation uses the position of its bond only through Lemma collar-coverage (i): the L²M₂R₁-cube containing the initial site must lie within LMR₁ − 1 of Λ₁. Here it lies within ℓ/2 + s_𝔓 + 3 + L²M₂R₁ ≤ 5ℓ/8 + s_𝔓 + 3 of Λ₁ (L²M₂R₁ ≤ ℓ/8, V4 Lemma c5-2-4), which is < ℓ − 1 once ℓ > (8/3)(s_𝔓 + 4), a g₀-threshold.
  - The flat gap (eq:covariance-uniform-gap) holds on the whole free space, and Lemma c2-regional-entry on all of Ω₁*×Ω₁*. The bounds pass to principal restrictions, and the decay is V4 Lemma c5-1-1, whose proof uses only the entry range (I1) and the two-sided bound.
- (D-c) *Localisation lemma.* Let W ⊂ P ⊂ Ω₁* with every bond of Ω₁*∖P at distance > R from W, and ∂P := {b ∈ Ω₁*∖P : d(b,P) ≤ ρ}. Suppose H ≥ 0, H has entry range ρ (V4 (I1)), γ ≤ H_PP ≤ m_L, the rows of P ∪ ∂P have row sums ≤ m_L, and ‖H_PP⁻¹(b,b′)‖ ≤ γ⁻¹e^{−μd(b,b′)}. Then, for every z ∈ 𝔤^{Ω₁*},
  ⟨z,Hz⟩ ≥ (γ/2)|z_W|² − γσ(R−ρ)²m_L²|z_{∂P}|², with σ(T) := γ⁻¹Σ_{t≥T}32(2t+1)³e^{−μt}.
  - Put u := −H_PP⁻¹H_{P,Pᶜ}z_{Pᶜ} and w := z_P − u. Completing the square, ⟨z,Hz⟩ = ⟨w,H_PPw⟩ + ⟨z_{Pᶜ},Sz_{Pᶜ}⟩ with S := H_{PᶜPᶜ} − H_{PᶜP}H_PP⁻¹H_{PPᶜ}.
  - S ≥ 0, since ⟨v,Sv⟩ is ⟨·,H·⟩ at (−H_PP⁻¹H_{PPᶜ}v, v). So ⟨z,Hz⟩ ≥ γ|w_W|² ≥ γ(½|z_W|² − |u_W|²).
  - f := H_{P,Pᶜ}z_{Pᶜ} depends on z_{∂P} only, and Schur's test gives |f| ≤ m_L|z_{∂P}|. It is supported on bonds of P within ρ of ∂P, at distance ≥ R − ρ from W.
  - At most 32(2t+1)³ bonds have initial site at ℓ^∞ distance t from a given one. So the block (H_PP⁻¹)_{W,supp f} has row and column sums ≤ σ(R−ρ), and |u_W| ≤ σ(R−ρ)m_L|z_{∂P}|. ∎
- (D-d) *Choice of R.* N(R) := 4(2R+2ρ+1)⁴ bounds the number of bonds within R + ρ of a bond. R_ϑ is the least integer R > ρ with σ(R−ρ)²m_L²(2π+1)²N(R) ≤ ϑ²/(4c_h²); it depends on L and the group only, since σ decays exponentially.
- (B-q) *The comparison on every cube of Ω₁.* Let □′ ⊂ Ω₁ be an L²M₂R₁-cube.
  - (a) |U₁(b)U_{1,□′}(b)⁻¹ − 1|_σ < 2δ₀ for every b ∈ (□′^{∼2})*. By V4 (B1)(iii), U₁ lies in the domain of integration of (1.11), on which the factor χ₀′(□′) of χ₀′(Ω₁) is nonzero (CRE (1.8), p.247; (1.11), p.248), as in the proof of V4 Lemma boundary-bijection.
  - (b) |U₁(e)U_{1,□′}(e)⁻¹ − 1|_σ < O(L²)ε₀ on every bond e of □′^{∼2}, the designated bonds included (V4 (B1)(ii)–(iii)). The O(L²) does not depend on g₀ (V4 (B1), (H4)).
  - (a) excludes the designated bonds, which plaquettes may contain; so (D-b⁺) uses (b), and (D1⁺) *Boundary data* uses (a).
- (D-b⁺) *Local gap near R₁.* Let P ⊂ Ω₁* be such that the initial site of every bond within s_𝔓 + 2 of P lies in an L²M₂R₁-cube □′ with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω₁. Put ε′ := ½ε₁L⁻² + 2O(L²)ε₀ and μ̂′ := 6c_Gc_hs_𝔓ε′, and let μ̂′ < τ_L/2 and max(1,c_L)μ̂′ < γ_L/4. Then the conclusions of (D-b) hold for P.
  - The proof is V4's proof of Lemma regular-bounds with I replaced by P, as in (D-b). It uses the cubes only through Lemma c2-plaquette-deviation, on the patches 𝔓_w and on the rectangles 𝔓_{z(b)} of the rows, whose bonds lie within s_𝔓 + 2 of P.
  - That lemma is redone for a plaquette p containing a bond b whose initial site lies in a cube □′ with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω₁; then p ⊂ □′^∼. As □′ ⊂ Ω₁, the test χ₁(Ω₁) gives |U_{1,□′}(∂p) − 1|_σ < ε₁L⁻², as in V4's proof of the lemma. By (B-q)(b), ‖U₁(e) − U_{1,□′}(e)‖_op ≤ c_hO(L²)ε₀ on the bonds of □′^{∼2}. Telescoping over the four bonds of p, ‖U₁(∂p) − 1‖_op ≤ c_hε₁L⁻² + 4c_hO(L²)ε₀ = 2c_hε′.
  - Lemma c2-local-gauge with ε′ in place of ε₁ (V4's remark after its proof) then gives phases h(A_b) ≤ μ̂′. Its hypothesis 6c_hs_𝔓ε′ ≤ r_G follows from μ̂′ < τ_L/2 and τ_L ≤ 2c_Gr_G (eq:c2-tau-final). The proof of Lemma regular-bounds uses μ̂ only through the two conditions of (eq:convex-background-thresholds), which μ̂′ satisfies. Both are g₀-thresholds, since ε′ → 0 as g₀ → 0.
  - The entries are local on all of Ω₁* (Lemma c2-regional-entry), and the flat gap holds on the whole free space.
- (B-q) and (D-b⁺) give the comparison, at size O(L²)ε₀, and the near-flat gauge on every cube □′ with □′^{∼4} ⊂ Ω₁, including the cubes of Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1} with this property. V4 Remark c5-2-2(ii) denies both on Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1}, so it overstates for these cubes; its conclusion, that no lower bound of the form on 𝔤^J is used, stands, and §3f uses none.

**(2) The ϑδ₀ Bad-Cube Factor (§3c(4‴)).**
- *R1: the statement.* Let 𝒲 be the set of cubes of the partition of CRE (1.18) (side L²M₂R₁ in fine units) that contain the initial site of a bad bond of the label, and ℛ the set of cubes of R₁. Λ = Λ^fin, with the enlarged retreat 𝒬⁺.
  - Let g₀ be below the thresholds ℓ > (8/3)(s_𝔓 + 4) ((D-b)), R_ϑ + 2ρ ≤ ℓ/2 ((D1)), R_ϑ + ρ + s_𝔓 + 5 ≤ L²M₂R₁ and 4θ₁ ≤ 1 ((D1⁺)), μ̂′ < τ_L/2 and max(1,c_L)μ̂′ < γ_L/4 ((D-b⁺)), δ₀ ≤ δ_G (V4 Lemma boundary-bijection), (1 + 2log(1+β₀)/log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀} ≤ 2 ((D2) *The constants*), and ½c₄ϑ²A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{p₀} ≥ 2 ((D2) *Per component*).
  - (i) *Pointwise.* On the support of the bad tests, of χ^{(0)c}(R₁), and of the cutoffs (1.19) and box factors on the bonds ∂P_X, every component form satisfies Q_X(x) ≥ 2c_bϑ²r²|W_X|. Here W_X holds one witness bond for each bad cube of X and at least |ℛ_X|/(2·5^d) witness bonds for its R₁ cubes, so |W_X| ≥ |𝒲_X| + |ℛ_X|/(2·5^d) ((D1), (D1⁺)).
  - (ii) *Per component, as the j = 1 factor of (1.79).* Let k ≥ N + 1 be a step at which T′_k acts on a component X_k of the step-k region Z, let 𝒳 be a component of Z₁ = Λᶜ in X_k, and let T₁′(𝒳) be the j = 1 part of T′_k(X_k) on 𝒳 ((D2) *Setting*). Then T₁′(𝒳,(U,0))(label tests) ≤ exp{O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′(𝒳)+1)}·sup Π_G exp(−½c₄ϑ²a(d₁′(G)+1) − 2p₀(g₁)).
  - In (ii), G runs over the components in 𝒳 of the MR₁-cube cover of P₁ ∪ Q₁ ∪ R₁ ∪ W₁ (Minor 4), and c₄ and m_A are those of #T12.19-9-1. The sup runs over the domain data P₁, Q₁, R₁, S₁ in 𝒳, as in (1.79); their count is in the first factor ((D2)(j)), and p.385's step removes the dependence. This is the j = 1 factor of (1.79) for T′_k(X_k), with the cover of P₁ ∪ Q₁ ∪ R₁ ∪ W₁ in place of p.380's cover of P₁ ∪ Q₁ ∪ R₁; its consumers are (1.82) and the κ induction of pp.384–386.
  - *Notation.* c_b := γ_L/(16c_h²). p₀(g) := (log g⁻²)^{p₀}, LFR II's convention, inferred from p.381's display, not printed as a definition: its ½B₃⁻²A₀² is (B₃⁻¹ε_j/g_j)²/2 with ε_j/g_j = A₀(log g_j⁻²)^{p₀}. CRE (2.4) writes p₀(g) for A₀(log g⁻²)^{p₀}; the factor A₀ changes no conclusion. a := A₁²p₀²(g₁) = A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀}. X ranges over the components of Ω₁ ∩ Λᶜ in 𝒳, the blocks of (2.21). Bad bonds in one cube count once.
- (D0) *Setting.* x denotes the T₁^Λ variables on J(Λ) = Ω₁*∖D(Λ), in the variables of (1.15). Ψ_ξ is the identity off S₁*, and d(I,S₁*) ≥ ℓ − 2 (V4 (eq:c-collar-distance)). Q_Λ(x) := min_y⟨(y,x),H(y,x)⟩, with y on D(Λ). (2.21) keeps the component blocks (§3c(4″)): the form of a component X is Q_X(x) := Q_Λ(x⁰), with x⁰ = x on X and 0 elsewhere.
- (D1) *The bad witnesses.* Fix a labelled term with Λ = Λ^fin. For each □′ ∈ 𝒲 choose one bad bond e(□′) with initial site in □′; distinct cubes give distinct bonds. For a component X let W_X be the chosen bonds in X, 𝒲_X their cubes, and P_X the bonds of Ω₁* within R_ϑ of W_X.
  - *Placement.* A bad bond e is a collar bond of some round region Λ^{(k)}, so d(e,I) ≤ d(e,D(Λ^{(k)})) ≤ ρ, as D(Λ^{(k)}) ⊂ I; with 𝒬⁺, d(e,Λ) ≥ ℓ (§3c(4⁗)(c)(α)). So P_X ∪ ∂P_X lies within R_ϑ + 2ρ ≤ ℓ/2 of I, at distance ≥ ℓ − 1 − R_ϑ − ρ > 0 from D(Λ) and ≥ ℓ − 2 − R_ϑ − 2ρ > 0 from S₁*. (D-b) applies to P_X ∪ ∂P_X.
  - *Boxes.* Every b ∈ ∂P_X has |x(b)|_σ < r. For b ∈ J this is the argument of V4 Lemma collar-coverage (i) and (iii) with 3R_c replaced by R_ϑ + 2ρ: the cube of b₋ lies within R_ϑ + 2ρ + 1 + L²M₂R₁ < ℓ − 1 of Λ₁, so b ∈ 𝒢_ξ∖S₁*, and 1{|x(b)|_σ < r} is a factor of (1.25) (V4 Lemma collar-coverage (ii)). For b ∈ I∖D(Λ) it is the retained box (V4 Lemma c5-2-5).
  - *The bound.* Apply (D-c), with W := W_X and P := P_X, to z* := (y*(x⁰), x⁰), the minimiser defining Q_X(x). Since (P_X ∪ ∂P_X) ∩ D(Λ) = ∅, z*_{W_X} = x_{W_X}, and z*_{∂P_X} is x on ∂P_X ∩ X and 0 elsewhere. On the support of the bad tests and of the boxes on ∂P_X, h(x(e)) ≥ |x(e)|_σ/c_h ≥ ϑr/c_h for e ∈ W_X, and |z*_{∂P_X}|² ≤ r²N(R_ϑ)|W_X|, as h ≤ |·|_σ and |∂P_X| ≤ N(R_ϑ)|W_X|. By (D-d), Q_X(x) ≥ (γ/2)|x_{W_X}|² − γϑ²r²|W_X|/(4c_h²) ≥ (γ/4)|x_{W_X}|² ≥ 2c_bϑ²r²|W_X|.
- (D1⁺) *The R₁ witnesses.*
  - *Choice.* For each cube □′ of R₁, χ^{(0)c}(R₁) gives a bond of (□′^{∼2})* with |A(b)|_σ ≥ r (CRE (1.18)); choose one, e(□′), and add it to W_X for the component X of Ω₁ ∩ Λᶜ that contains it. A bond lies in (□′^{∼2})* for at most 2·5^d cubes □′, so the R₁ cubes of X give at least |ℛ_X|/(2·5^d) distinct bonds.
  - *Placement.* R₁′ ⊃ R₁ is at distance ≥ ℓ from Λ₁ ⊃ Λ (V4 Lemma collar-coverage (i)), so e(□′) is at distance ≥ ℓ − 2L²M₂R₁ − 2 ≥ 3ℓ/4 − 2 from D(Λ), and it differs from the bad witnesses, which lie within ρ of I. As R_ϑ + 2ρ ≤ ℓ/2, (P_X ∪ ∂P_X) ∩ D(Λ) = ∅.
  - *(D-b⁺) applies.* A bond within s_𝔓 + 2 of the part of P_X ∪ ∂P_X within R_ϑ + ρ of e(□′) has its initial site within 2L²M₂R₁ + R_ϑ + ρ + s_𝔓 + 3 of □′ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−1}. Its cube □″ then has □″^{∼4} within 7L²M₂R₁ + R_ϑ + ρ + s_𝔓 + 3 ≤ 8L²M₂R₁ − 2 ≤ LMR₁ − 2 of Ω₁^{∼−1}, by R_ϑ + ρ + s_𝔓 + 5 ≤ L²M₂R₁ and M ≥ 8LM₂ (V4 (eq:c-standing-conditions)). Points within LMR₁ − 1 of Ω₁^{∼−1} = ((Ω₁ᶜ)^∼)ᶜ lie in Ω₁, so □″^{∼4} ⊂ Ω₁.
  - Near the bad witnesses the cube □″ of such a bond lies within 5ℓ/8 + s_𝔓 + 3 < ℓ − 1 of Λ₁, as in (D-b), hence in Ω₁^{∼−1} (V4 Lemma collar-coverage (i)), and □″^{∼4} ⊂ Ω₁ as 4L²M₂R₁ ≤ ℓ/2. So the hypothesis of (D-b⁺) holds on all of P_X ∪ ∂P_X, and (D-b⁺) is applied to that set.
  - *Variables.* Ψ_ξ differs from the identity only on S₁* (CRE (1.22); V4 Section c5-0). On S₁* the T-variable is A′ of (1.22), and A = Ψ_ξ(A′). By V4 Lemma boundary-bijection and its proof, e^{ig₀A(b)} = e^{ig₀A′(b)}W_b with |W_b − 1|_σ ≤ |𝐇_{1,Ax}(b)|_σ, and its (a), with |Z_b − 1|_σ < 2δ₀, gives |e^{ig₀A(b)} − 1|_σ < 4δ₀ on the support.
  - By V4 Lemma axial-boundary-field and the norm facts, |𝐇_{1,Ax}(b)|_σ = L|log(U₁(b)U₁′(b)⁻¹)|_σ ≤ (π/2)LC_Hε₁e^{−c_HLMR₁} ≤ θ₁δ₀, with C_H, c_H the constants of that lemma (not those of §3e(3)) and θ₁ := (π/2)(1+β₀)(A₀/A₁)LC_He^{−c_HLMR₁}, by ε₁ ≤ (1+β₀)ε₀ (CRE (2.8), n = 1, m = 0) and δ₀ = (A₁/A₀)ε₀. θ₁ → 0 as g₀ → 0, since C_H and c_H are fixed before g₀ and R₁ → ∞.
  - So |e^{ig₀A′(b)} − 1|_σ < 5δ₀, as θ₁ ≤ 1. g₀A(b) and g₀A′(b) lie in the chart ball (V4 Lemma boundary-bijection (a) and the domain 𝒲_b), where ‖·‖_op < π, so both have |·|_σ ≤ (π/2)·5δ₀ < 12δ₀, and there |X − X′|_σ ≤ 2|e^{iX} − e^{iX′}|_σ (norm facts). Hence |A − A′|_σ ≤ 2g₀⁻¹|W_b − 1|_σ ≤ 2θ₁r ≤ r/2, by 4θ₁ ≤ 1. The bounds below hold for the form's argument, A or A′.
  - *Boundary data near R₁.* Let b ∈ ∂P_X lie within R_ϑ + ρ of e(□′), and let □″ be the cube of b₋, so □″^{∼4} ⊂ Ω₁. As b ∈ Ω₁* is not designated and has an endpoint in □″, b ∈ (□″^{∼2})*.
    - If □″ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−1}∖R₁, then b ∈ 𝒢_ξ, and the first factor of (1.25) gives |A(b)|_σ < r (V4 Lemma collar-coverage (ii)).
    - Otherwise □″ ⊂ S₁ = (Ω₁∖Ω₁^{∼−1}) ∪ R₁ (CRE (1.20)), and the factor χ₀′(S₁) of (1.25) holds (proofs of V4 Lemma collar-coverage (ii) and (iv)): |e^{ig₀A(b)}Y − 1|_σ < 2δ₀ with Y := U₁(b)U_{1,□″}(b)⁻¹, since U(b) = e^{ig₀A(b)}U₁(b) on the free bond b (V4 Lemma row-locality). By (B-q)(a), |Y − 1|_σ < 2δ₀, so |e^{ig₀A(b)} − 1|_σ < 4δ₀.
    - g₀A(b) lies in the chart ball, by the chart-class condition of V4 (eq:c-free-variable-domain), as in V4 Lemma boundary-bijection (a). So |g₀A(b)|_σ ≤ (π/2)|e^{ig₀A(b)} − 1|_σ < 2πδ₀, that is |A(b)|_σ < 2πr.
    - With |A − A′|_σ ≤ r/2 on S₁*, the form's argument is at most r_∂ := (2π + 1)r on ∂P_X, and at least r − r/2 ≥ ϑr on the R₁ witnesses (ϑ ≤ 1/16).
  - *Conclusion.* Apply (D-c) as in (D1), with W_X holding the bad and the R₁ witnesses and P_X the bonds of Ω₁* within R_ϑ of W_X. Then z*_{W_X} = x_{W_X}, |z*_{∂P_X}|² ≤ r_∂²N(R_ϑ)|W_X|, and by (D-d), Q_X(x) ≥ (γ/4)|x_{W_X}|² ≥ 2c_bϑ²r²|W_X|. The support conditions are the bad tests, χ^{(0)c}(R₁), and the cutoffs (1.19) and box factors on ∂P_X, all factors of the integrand. (D1) is the case ℛ_X = ∅.
- (D2) *The labelled integral, factor by factor.*
  - *Setting.* Fix a labelled term with Λ = Λ^fin. T′_k acts on a component X_k of the step-k region Z only at steps k ≥ N + 1 ((l)), and the step-1 large-field functions reach it inside T_h(Z_h ∩ X_k), h = k − N, which LFR II §1 leaves unchanged (p.356 l.16–18) and which is a factor of (1.71) (pp.378–379).
  - T_h(Z_h ∩ X_k) contains T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} at Λ: LFR I (1.71), the domains Ω_hᶜ, Ω″_{h+1}, Z″_{h+2}, the preliminary-integration data and T′_k(X_k) are formed at Λ as printed (RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.2-17-2); T₁^{Λ₁,Λ} is untouched by steps 2, …, h and by the 𝐑-operations on other components (#RP.3-1-5); and if the basic step acted on the component containing 𝒳 at a step k′ < k, the j = 1 factor was spent there, once (#RP.3-3, #RP.3-3-1).
  - Fix such k and X_k, and a component 𝒳 of Z₁ = Λᶜ in X_k. T₁′(𝒳) is the j = 1 part of T′_k(X_k) on 𝒳: the integral over the step-1 variables in 𝒳 at fixed values of all other variables of (1.71). The bound below is uniform in them; as the integrand is ≥ 0, it enters the bound of T′_k(X_k) as a factor, as p.380 extracts its factors per configuration.
  - Its measure is the A-integrals over the step-1 T-variables on J(Λ) ∩ 𝒳, the V₀-integrals with δ(V̄₀V⁻¹), and the sums over the step-1 domain data in 𝒳, Σ_{S₁} included (§3c(4⁗)(d)). Its integrand is the label tests times the j = 1 factors that p.380 l.25–47 extracts:
    - the step-1 forms at Λ, e^{−½Σ_XQ_X}, with χ^{(0)c}(R₁), the cutoffs (1.19) and the box factors on ∂P_X, carried in T_h(Z_h ∩ X_k);
    - the step-k Wilson term (1.48) (pp.366, 368) on the plaquettes near P₁ ∪ Q₁, with the step-1 tests χ₁^c(P₁), χ₀′^c(Q₁) of ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) (1.11) and their gauge-fixing factors;
    - the step-1 parts of V(X^∼), of the vacuum counterterms and of the normalisation constants ((b)), and the z^{(0)} rows off Λ ((k)).
  - By (1.73), |T′_k(X_k,(U,J))F| ≤ sup e^{|σ|}·T′_k(X_k,(U,0))|F|, and the density is positive at J = 0 (p.379 l.38; at Λ by #RP.2-10-2). So it suffices to bound T₁′(𝒳,(U,0)) applied to the label tests, with an integrand ≥ 0. The B-integral of (1.71), over the step-k field V′ = exp ig_kB (p.360 l.42–44), is part of T′_k(X_k), is bounded as printed ((h)), and is not a j = 1 factor.
  - *Check of pp.379–383 at Λ.* The steps of the proof of (1.79), for the factors j = 1 inside T′_k(X_k), k ≥ N + 1, and what each uses of the configuration:
    - (a) p.379 l.28–p.380 l.24, (1.73)–(1.75): reduction to J = 0, by positivity of the density at the real configuration. Per configuration.
    - (b) p.380 l.25–29: V(X^∼) by (1.69), the vacuum counterterms and the normalisation constants, in modulus. Their step-1 parts are CRE's step-1 terms at Λ (𝐄^{(1)}(Λ), V^{(0)}, the 𝐁-terms), made by CRE's construction at Λ ((3) S1). p.377 l.3–4 bounds the corresponding sum "similar to the bound (2.48) [III] for the sum of the boundary terms"; [III] is CRE (LFR II p.392).
      - CRE (2.47)–(2.48), p.264, print |𝐁^{(j,n)}| ≤ B₁2^{−(j−n)}|Γ_n| for j > n, the same with |Γ_n∖Λ_k⁰| for j = n, and |(2.40)| ≤ 2B₁(Σ_{n=1}^{k−1}|Γ_n| + |Γ_k∖Λ_k⁰|) ≤ 2B₁Σ_{n=1}^k|Γ_n|, with Γ_n = Ω_n^{(n)}∖Ω_{n+1}^{(n)} for 1 ≤ n ≤ k−1 and Γ_k = Ω_k^{(k)} (p.255, (2.2)).
      - At step 1 the cost is §3c(4⁗)(a)'s |𝐁₁| ≤ 2B₁|Ω₁^{(1)}∖Λ⁰| at Λ, in points of T^{(1)} (p.263). Since Λ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−2}, each MR₁-cube of the layer removed from Λ to form Λ⁰ is adjacent to a cube of Z₁ ∩ Ω₁, which has at most 3^d − 1 neighbours; so on 𝒳 the cost is at most 2B₁·3^d|Z₁ ∩ Ω₁ ∩ 𝒳|, absorbed in this item's factor exp(O(1)log g₁⁻²|Z₁ ∩ 𝒳|).
    - (c) p.380 l.31–42: the P₁- and Q₁-factors ((0)), printed for the large-field functions "introduced in the j^th step" inside T′_k, k ≥ N + 1, and used here in that scope at j = 1. Their source term is the step-k Wilson term (1.48), pp.366, 368. P₁ and Q₁ lie in Ω₁ᶜ, at distance ≥ 2LMR₁ from Ω₁ (CRE p.248 l.9–10), and their characteristic functions sit in ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) of (1.11) (p.248 l.24–25).
      - *P₁.* [16] = UV3 (70)–(71), p.273 l.36–56: for a large plaquette p′ the action on the four j-blocks at the corners of p′ is ≥ ½g_j⁻²|V_j(∂p′) − 1|² − O(1)g_jp³(g_j) ≥ ¼p²(g_j), pointwise, for g_j small. Theorem 1 [15] (VAR p.279) gives the minimal configurations U_{1,□′}, their uniqueness and their regularity. At step 1 the test χ₁^c(P₁) of (1.4) (p.246) is on U_{1,□′}(V,∂p) for p ⊂ □′^∼, □′ ⊂ P₁, and U_{1,□′} depends on V on □′^{∼4} only (p.246 l.46).
      - *Q₁.* The expansion is in the approximate fluctuation field UU_{1,□′}⁻¹, which p.247 l.34–37 defines on □′^{∼2}. The test χ₀′^c(Q₁) of (1.8) is on (□′^{∼2})*, □′ ⊂ Q₁, and U_{1,□′} again depends on V on □′^{∼4}.
      - *Locality.* □′^{∼4} lies within 4L²M₂R₁ ≤ LMR₁/2 of □′ (M ≥ 8LM₂, V4 (eq:c-standing-conditions)), so for □′ ⊂ P₁ ∪ Q₁ it lies at distance ≥ LMR₁ from Ω₁, in Ω₁ᶜ. The rates are p.380's displays, quoted, not traced through VAR and UV3.
      - The tests χ₁^c(P₁), χ₀′^c(Q₁) and their variables V₀, V are the same at Λ as at Λ₁: (P) changes only the conditional domain inside Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−2}, and through it the forms and 𝐄 on Ω₁. The step-k term (1.48) on these plaquettes involves Λ only through its domains Ω_hᶜ, Ω″_{h+1} and Z″_{h+2} (pp.364–368), which are formed at Λ as printed (#RP.2-17-2). p.380's displays are bounds per configuration on the support of the tests, so they hold at the labelled configuration.
    - (d) p.380 l.43–47: the R₁-factor, "by the same positivity bound for the quadratic form". R₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−1} lies in the T-region, so at Λ this step concerns the (2.21) forms at Λ, in T_h(Z_h ∩ X_k). It is derived at Λ in (D1⁺), jointly with the bad cubes.
    - (e) p.380 l.47–p.381 l.9: the combination per component; redone below for the cover with W₁.
    - (f) p.381 l.11–13, (1.76): a property of LFR II's domains. It is used in a bound only on p.385 l.16 and p.386 l.6; p.383 l.45 restricts the sup of (1.79) to the domains that occur. At Λ the modified (1.76) of Minor 4 enters on p.385.
    - (g) p.381 l.15–p.383 l.29: the preparatory factors Π′, from characteristic functions ≤ 1. Not used.
    - (h) p.383 l.31–32: the group integrals ≤ 1 (normalised Haar measure under δ(V̄₀V⁻¹)). Per configuration. The line's second half, the B-form bounded below by 0 with the logarithmic constants put into the measure, concerns the step-k B-integral of T′_k(X_k), bounded as printed.
    - (i) p.383 l.33–34: the A-integrals, "using the positivity properties of the quadratic forms". At Λ this is derived in *The bound*, together with the (1.15) Jacobian.
    - (j) p.383 l.34–35: the sums over admissible sequences, exp O(1)(MR₁)^{−d}|Z₁|. Counting, per configuration. The label sum is R2.
    - (k) The z^{(0)} rows off Λ (§3c(2)(a)) are a factor of the labelled integrand that CRE's does not have, bounded by exp(2Cd|Z₁∩Ω₁∩𝒳|).
    - (l) *(1.71) and T′.* T′_k(X_k) is defined at step k "for a component of Z", as the composition of the integration of (1.70) and the T_h-operation of (1.100) [IV], localised in X (p.378 l.26–29; (1.71), p.378 l.30–p.379 l.8), with h = k − N. By LFR I's condition (ii) the last N one-step operations contain no large-field functions (LFR I p.178 l.11–14), and N ≥ (ν/log L)log log g_k⁻² (p.363 l.40–42).
      - (1.71) states no range of k. LFR I p.177 (ii) admits only components in which "in the preceding N renormalization steps no new large field regions were created", so the basic step acts only at k ≥ N + 1, and the step-1 regions are created before the preceding N steps (RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.2-2, #RP.2-2-2).
      - The step-1 data enter pp.356–379 through T_h(Z_h ∩ X_k); the volume bound (1.69); the sums over the domain data in (1.71), whose geometry enters on p.385 through (1.76); the size bounds over Γ″_j in (1.47); Ω_h in χ_{h,1/2}; and the Wilson term (1.37)/(1.48) (pp.366, 368). Every other domain on these pages is the step-k region Z or LFR's own Λ ⊂ Z, "a rectangular parallelepiped contained in a cube of the size 100M" (p.357 l.44, p.358 l.9), not the note's Λ.
      - (P) changes only the step-1 Λ-data, which enter only through T_h(Z_h ∩ X_k) and the domain-data sums. Ω″_j, Z″_{h+1}, Z″_{h+2}, Ω_k and 𝐁₀ are data of steps h+1, …, k.
  - *The bound.* On the support of the integrand:
    - χ_lab·χ^{(0)c}(R₁)·(the cutoffs (1.19) and box factors on ∂P_X)·e^{−½Σ_XQ_X} ≤ e^{−c_bϑ²r²|W|}, with W := ⋃_XW_X, by (D1⁺). This is the only use of the forms; the rest of the integrand is ≤ 1 and is integrated over |A| ≤ C_𝐠/g₀ below.
    - For each term of ζ(Ω₁ᶜ), (c) gives exp(−γ₀(2g₁²)⁻¹(B₃⁻¹ε₁)²) = e^{−½γ₀B₃⁻²A₀²𝔭²} per cube of P₁ and exp(−½γ₀g₁⁻²(2δ₁)²) = e^{−2γ₀A₁²𝔭²} per cube of Q₁, with 𝔭 := (log g₁⁻²)^{p₀}, ε₁/g₁ = A₀𝔭 and δ₁/g₁ = A₁𝔭; (LM₂R₁)^{−d}|P₁| and (LM₂R₁)^{−d}|Q₁| are the numbers of cubes. Both exponents are ≥ 4γ₀m_Aa per cube, as ½B₃⁻²A₀²𝔭² = ½B₃⁻²A₀²A₁⁻²a and 2A₁²𝔭² = 2a.
    - The P₀, P₁, Q₁ and S₁ sums are decompositions of unity, with one nonzero term at each point; their terms are bounded one at a time and counted in (j). The gauge-fixing exponentials of ζ(Ω₁ᶜ) are ≤ 1. Their normalisations 1/z of (1.5), σ₀ and the (1.24) Jacobian contribute at most O(log g₀⁻²) per site to the exponent.
    - For 1/z: by Haar invariance z = ∫dv χ(|v − 1| < ε₀)exp[−g₀⁻²(1 − Re tr v)] ((1.5), p.247; tr normalised, so Re tr v ≤ 1; with the unnormalised trace, N − Re tr v), so z ≤ 1. Restricting to v = e^{iX} with |X| < g₀ ≤ ε₀, where |e^{iX} − 1| ≤ |X| and 1 − Re tr v ≤ c_tr|X|², gives z ≥ e^{−c_tr}Haar{|X| < g₀} ≥ cg₀^{d(𝐠)}, with c and c_tr depending on G only.
    - So log(1/z) = O(log g₀⁻²), and log g₀⁻² ≤ log g₁⁻² + 2log(1+β₀) by CRE (2.6) (g₁ ≤ (1+β₀)g₀). These go into (b)'s factor exp(O(1)log g₁⁻²|Z₁∩𝒳|).
    - The rest of (1.48): its Wilson action has a G-valued field, "hence the action is positive" (p.368 l.37–39), so its exponential is ≤ 1; its O(1) terms are small (p.368 l.32, l.37), a factor ≤ e absorbed in the first factor of the bound; the remaining characteristic functions are ≤ 1.
    - (b): exp(O(1)log g₁⁻²|Z₁∩𝒳|); (k): exp(2Cd|Z₁∩Ω₁∩𝒳|); Σ_{S₁}: at most 2^{#(L²M₂R₁-cubes of Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ∩𝒳)} terms.
    - The A-integral: each T-variable is A(b) = g₀⁻¹A′(b) with A′ = (1/i)log U′ in the principal chart (CRE (1.13), p.248), so |A(b)| ≤ C_𝐠/g₀. With the (1.15) Jacobian g₀^{d(𝐠)} per T-bond (p.249's scaling) and Q_X ≥ 0, the A-integral is ≤ C_𝐠^{d(𝐠)·#(T-bonds)}, and #(T-bonds) ≤ O(1)|Z₁∩Ω₁∩𝒳|, the O(1) depending on d, d(𝐠) and L.
  - *Together.* T₁′(𝒳,(U,0))(label tests) ≤ exp{O(1)log g₁⁻²|𝒳|}·sup_{P₁,Q₁,R₁,S₁ in 𝒳}Π_{cubes of P₁∪Q₁ in 𝒳}e^{−4γ₀m_Aa}·e^{−c_bϑ²r²|W|}, the sup over the domain data as in (1.79). Since |𝒳| = (MR₁)^d#cubes(𝒳) in points of T^{(1)}, #cubes(𝒳) ≤ 3·2^{d−1}(d₁′(𝒳)+1) (D26 §1 *Counting cubes*) and log g₁⁻² ≤ (log g₁⁻²)^𝔯 ≤ R₁ (CRE (2.5)), exp{O(1)log g₁⁻²|𝒳|} ≤ exp{O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′(𝒳)+1)}.
  - *The constants.* r = δ₀/g₀ = A₁(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀} (V4 Section c5-0; δ₀ = (A₁/A₀)ε₀). By CRE (2.6), g₀ ≤ (1+β₀)g₁, so log g₁⁻² ≤ log g₀⁻² + 2log(1+β₀), and (log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀} ≤ 2(log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀} under the threshold (1 + 2log(1+β₀)/log g₀⁻²)^{2p₀} ≤ 2. So r² ≥ ½a, and with |W| ≥ |𝒲| + |ℛ|/(2·5^d), c_bϑ²r²|W| ≥ ½c_bϑ²a|𝒲| + (c_bϑ²a/(4·5^d))|ℛ|.
  - *Splitting the R₁ witnesses among the G.* Two closed MR₁-cubes may meet only in an edge or a corner, so R₁ cubes with a common witness need not lie in one component G. A witness e(□′) has an endpoint in □′^{∼2}, so every R₁ cube it serves meets the box of side 4L²M₂R₁ + 2 < ℓ centred at its initial site, and so does the MR₁-cube containing it. A box of side < ℓ meets at most 2^d MR₁-cubes, so the served cubes lie in at most 2^d components G.
    - Each such G receives the share 2^{−d}c_bϑ²r² of the witness's exponent c_bϑ²r², and the shares of one witness add up to at most its exponent. A bad witness has its initial site in its own cube, so it stays in its own G.
    - With r² ≥ ½a and at least |ℛ_G|/(2·5^d) witnesses serving the R₁ cubes of G, each G receives at least ½c_bϑ²a|𝒲_G| + (c_bϑ²a/(4·10^d))|ℛ_G|. This holds for wall-components and for ∗-components alike.
  - *Per component.* Let G run over the components in 𝒳 of the MR₁-cube cover of P₁ ∪ Q₁ ∪ R₁ ∪ W₁ (Minor 4). The generating sets are unions of LM₂R₁-cubes, and the three kinds are disjoint: P₁ ∪ Q₁ ⊂ Ω₁ᶜ, R₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−1}, and the bad cubes lie in Ω₁^{∼−1}∖R₁′ (V4 Lemma c5-2-3(c)). Each LM₂R₁-cube lies in exactly one MR₁-cube, since LM₂ divides M (V4 (eq:c-standing-conditions)), so #MR₁-cubes(G) ≤ #gen(G), the number of generating cubes in G.
    - *Linear size.* d₁′ is the length of a shortest tree contained in the set and meeting all its MR₁-cubes, in units of the cube side (D26 §1 *Counting cubes*). For a ∗-connected union of n cubes, join the centres of intersecting cubes through a common point along a spanning tree of the intersection graph; the union of these segments lies in the set, is connected, meets every cube, and has length ≤ √d(n − 1). So d₁′(G) + 1 ≤ 2#MR₁-cubes(G) (d = 4).
    - RG1 p.257 l.15–19 measures the trees in the continuous space and calls trees formed by cube edges an equivalent definition; the note uses the continuous form, and the edge form changes only constants depending on d.
    - The exponent available for G is γ₀m_Aa·#(cubes of P₁∪Q₁ in G) + ½c_bϑ²a|𝒲_G| + (c_bϑ²a/(4·10^d))|ℛ_G| ≥ 2c₄ϑ²a·#gen(G) ≥ c₄ϑ²a(d₁′(G) + 1), since ϑ ≤ 1, 2c₄ ≤ γ₀m_A and 2c₄ ≤ c_b/(4·10^d) ≤ ½c_b.
    - By the threshold ½c₄ϑ²A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{p₀} ≥ 2, ½c₄ϑ²a ≥ 2(log g₁⁻²)^{p₀} = 2p₀(g₁). So the factor of G is at most exp(−½c₄ϑ²a(d₁′(G) + 1) − 2p₀(g₁)). This is R1 (ii).
- *R2: label entropy.*
  - *Injectivity.* At fixed outer Λ₁ the label of a term is its sequence (B^{(0)}, B^{(1)}, …) of bad sets (V4 Lemma c5-3-3), and each collar bond is tested once, in the first round whose collar J_col(Λ^{(k)}) contains it (V4 Section c5-3-1). So the label is determined by 𝔘 := ⋃_kB^{(k)}: Λ^{(0)} = Fill(Λ₁) is label-free (V4 Lemma c5-3-0(a)); B^{(k)} is the set of bonds of 𝔘 in J_col(Λ^{(k)}) and in no earlier collar; and Λ^{(k+1)} is determined by Λ^{(k)} and B^{(k)} (§3c(4⁗)(c)).
  - *Where 𝔘 lies.* A bond e found bad in round k lies in J_col(Λ^{(k)}), so x_e is within ρ_L + 1 of Λ^{(k)} ⊂ Λ^{(0)} (proof of V4 Lemma c5-3-1(a)), and both endpoints of e lie in 𝒬⁺(e) ⊂ Λᶜ (§3c(4⁗)(c)). So 𝔘 ⊂ S, the set of bonds with both endpoints in Λᶜ and initial site within ρ_L + 1 of Λ^{(0)}, which depends on Λ₁ and Λ only.
  - *Count.* A bond with both endpoints in Λᶜ lies in one ∗-component Z of Λᶜ, and each site is the initial site of d bonds. The ℓ-cubes are the MR₁-cubes of T^{(1)}, so |S∩Z| ≤ dℓ^d#cubes(Z) ≤ 3d2^{d−1}L^dM^dR₁^d(d₁′(Z)+1) (D26 §1 *Counting cubes*).
  - *The bound.* The terms with Λ^fin = Λ number at most 2^{|S|} = Π_Z2^{|S∩Z|} ≤ Π_Z exp(M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′(Z)+1)) once R₁ ≥ 3d2^{d−1}L^d, since log 2 < 1. This is a cost of the form of the first factor of R1 (ii), paid with it in (1.82) (Minor 4).
- *Minor 4: the bad cubes as a fourth generator, and the modified (1.76).*
  - *Generators.* W₁ := ⋃𝒲. The components G of the MR₁-cube cover of P₁ ∪ Q₁ ∪ R₁ ∪ W₁ take the place of p.380's Z₁^{(i)}, and R1 (ii) gives the factor of each G. Each G lies in one component of Λᶜ:
    - the cover cubes of P₁ ∪ Q₁ lie in Ω₁ᶜ, a union of ℓ-cubes (CRE (1.10)), and Λ ⊂ Λ₁ ⊂ Ω₁^{∼−2};
    - the cover cubes of R₁ form R₁′ (p.251), and R₁′ ⊂ Λ₁ᶜ by (1.20);
    - the ℓ-cube that contains a bad cube contains its x_e, so it meets the ball defining 𝒬⁺(e) and lies in 𝒬⁺(e) ⊂ Λᶜ (§3c(4⁗)(c)).
  - *Geometry.* Let p be a point of a component Z of Z₁ = Λᶜ.
    - If p ∈ 𝔅₀ = (Λ^{(0)})ᶜ, then p lies within 100ℓ of a point q of CRE's unfilled region 𝒰 := Q₁′^{∼4} ∪ (B(P₁¹)ᶜ)^{∼5} ∪ R₁′^{∼}, in the same component of 𝔅₀ (V4 Lemma c5-3-0(b) and its proof). 𝒰 is the complement of Ω₁^{∼−2} ∩ (R₁′^∼)ᶜ (CRE (1.10), (1.20)), and p.251's filling only removes cubes, so 𝒰 ⊂ Λ₁ᶜ, CRE's Z₁ (p.255 l.25).
    - Let Z′ be the component of Λ₁ᶜ that contains q. By p.385 l.15–16 (Bałaban's j = 1 form of (1.76), accepted), Z′ ⊂ ⋃_i(Z₁^{(i)})^{∼2} over the regions Z₁^{(i)} that determine Z′; these lie in Z′, as on p.386 l.3–5 ("joined together into the one component"), inferred, not printed for j = 1. So q ∈ (Z₁^{(i)})^{∼2} with Z₁^{(i)} ⊂ Z′ ⊂ Z. Z₁^{(i)} lies in some G, which lies in Z, and p ∈ G^{∼102}.
    - If p ∈ Λ^{(0)}∖Λ, then p lies within 100ℓ of a point of 𝒬⁺(e), in the same component of Λᶜ, for a bond e found bad (proof of V4 Lemma c5-3-1(c), with 𝒬⁺). 𝒬⁺(e) is face-connected, contains x_e, and lies within 3ℓ of x_e (§3c(4⁗)(c)). x_e lies in a bad cube, so in some G, which lies in Z, and p ∈ G^{∼103}.
    - Hence Z ⊂ ⋃_{G⊂Z}G^{∼107}, the modified (1.76) per component. In particular each Z contains a G.
  - *p.385's step.* Z is ∗-connected and Z ⊂ ⋃_{G⊂Z}G^{∼107}, so d₁′(Z)+1 ≤ 2d(434)^dΣ_{G⊂Z}(d₁′(G)+1) (D26 *The fundamental factor at j = 1*). Hence Π_{G⊂Z}exp(−½c₄ϑ²a(d₁′(G)+1)) ≤ exp(−¼c₄d^{−1}(434)^{−d}ϑ²a(d₁′(Z)+1)), and (1.82) holds with ¼c₄d^{−1}(434)^{−d}ϑ² in place of ¼γ₀(14)^{−d} and with d₁′(Z)+1 in its volume term. The costs of R2 and of the first factor of R1 (ii) are of that form, O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′(Z)+1).
  - *The j = 1 condition.* (1.80) at j = 1 then needs K₁ ≥ 8B₁, with K₁ and B₁ = O(1)(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R₁^{d+2} of #T12.19-9-1 (D26 *Ledger at j = 1*; this B₁ is not CRE's of (D2)(b)). K₁ is of order (log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀}, and B₁ is of order (log g₁⁻²)^{(d+2)𝔯} = (log g₁⁻²)^{6𝔯}, since R₁ ≤ L(log g₁⁻²)^𝔯 (CRE (2.5)). As p₀ > 6𝔯 ((0)), 2p₀ > 6𝔯, so K₁ ≥ 8B₁ for small g₁; c₄, ϑ, L and M are fixed before the coupling.
  - *Where (1.76) enters.* It is used in a bound only on p.385 l.16 and p.386 l.6 ((D2)(f)). On p.386, (1.84) relates a component of Z_{j+1}, j ≥ 1, to the components of Z_j and the new regions of step j + 1, which steps 2, 3, … form as printed from Z₁ = Λᶜ (RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.3-1-1), with no further retreat (map #MD2.2.3.24-3). So the modified form enters only on p.385. The sup of R1 (ii) runs over the domain data at the labelled configuration; pp.380–383 do not use (1.76), and (D2) bounds each labelled configuration directly.
  - *The fill.* The round-0 fill and the iterated Fill of the rounds (V4 Section c5-3-1) add only points of Λᶜ, which *Geometry* covers; their volume costs are in the first factor of R1 (ii).
- *Minor 5.* By V4 Lemma collar-coverage (i) and (ii), I ⊆ 𝒢_ξ and 𝒢_ξ∖I = 𝒢_ξ ∩ J, so the labelled cutoff box(D(Λ))·box(I∖D(Λ))·Π_{b∈𝒢_ξ∩J}1{|A(b)| < r} equals Π_{b∈𝒢_ξ}1{|A(b)| < r}. As Λ ⊆ Λ₁ ⊆ R₁ᶜ := Ω₁^{∼−1}∖R₁ and both are unions of (1.18) cubes, this is χ^{(0)}(Λ)χ^{(0)}(R₁ᶜ∩Λᶜ), by idempotence of indicators (the identity for Λ₁ after V4 (eq:c-good-region)).
  - A bond of (□′^{∼2})*, □′ ⊂ Λ, outside D(Λ) has its initial site in a cube □″ ⊂ Λᶜ within 3L²M₂R₁ + 1 < ℓ − 1 of Λ₁, so □″ ⊂ R₁ᶜ (V4 Lemma collar-coverage (i)), and the bond is read by χ^{(0)}(R₁ᶜ∩Λᶜ). Hence the labelled cutoff is box(D(Λ))·χ^{(0)}(R₁ᶜ∩Λᶜ), the step-1 form at Λ of the first factor of CRE (3.21).

**(3) The k = 1 Construction Check (§3c(4⁗)).**
- *S1: (α) in sublevel-set form.* On Λ^∼∖Λ the characteristic functions of the T-integrand are sublevel indicators {|A(b)| < t} with t ≤ r, located as in §3c(4⁗)(c)(α): a cube test by its index cube, a single-bond test by its bond.
  - *Why 𝒬⁺.* A bad test is a superlevel indicator, but none of the occurrences below depends on the sign of an extra factor. 𝒬⁺ is needed for the placement in (D1): P_X must miss D(Λ), and 𝒬⁺ gives d(e,Λ) ≥ ℓ for every bad bond e.
  - *The other factors.* ι_ξ = 1 on the support (§3c(2)(e)), so it is not a factor. The terminal-round good tests lie in Λ^∼∖Λ (V4 Lemma c5-3-1(b)); the earlier-round good tests and the box factors on I∖D(Λ) lie in Λᶜ, possibly outside Λ^∼. All of these factors take values in [0,1].
  - *The monotonicity principle.* Every step-1 consumer of T₁'s characteristic functions is an identity of functions, an upper bound of an integral with a nonnegative integrand, or a supremum over the support:
    - no step-1 operation integrates T₁, since CRE's inductive assumption leaves the operations corresponding to T₁ undefined beyond their general properties (p.254 l.36–39);
    - at k = 0 the kernel of (1.29) (p.254) is a nonnegative measure: δ, ζ and the characteristic functions are nonnegative, and the fields are real, so the quadratic forms in the exponent are real;
    - the conditional cutoff is box(D(Λ)) (V4 Lemma c5-3-3(iv));
    - §3c(4⁗)(a), R1 and (1.79) are upper bounds of such integrals.
    - A pointwise identity survives multiplication by any factor, and an upper bound of a nonnegative integrand, or a supremum over the support, survives multiplication by a factor in [0,1]. So the collar tests and the box factors break no step-1 use.
  - *LFR I and II.* LFR I (1.71) and the operation T′_k(X) of LFR II (1.71) are formed at Λ as printed (RC_PAYMENT_PASS #RP.2-17-2), with the box factors, the collar tests and the bad tests inside T₁^{Λ₁,Λ}, in the slot of p.255's χ(Ω₁∩Λᶜ,S₁) (#RP.2-10-2). They use that integrand only through its positivity at real configurations and its gauge invariance (#RP.2-10-2, #RP.2-12-1, #RP.2-12-2), and p.177's class selects the terms for Λ₁ and Λ alike (#RP.2-1-1).
  - *Occurrences in the transcriptions.*
    - *CRE p.253 (1.26) (k = 0) and p.269 l.16–18 (general k):* "their product is χ^{(k)}(Λ_{k+1})", with (3.21) on Ω∩Λᶜ; p.251 has "On the set Λ₁^∼ we have only the small fluctuation field characteristic functions from (1.18)". This is an identity of functions, which splits the characteristic functions into the cutoff of the conditional integral on Λ and the T-part. Under (P) the cutoff is box(D(Λ)) (§3c(4″); V4 Lemma c5-3-3(iv)), and the T-part is the first factor of (3.21) at Λ times the collar tests (Minor 5). No estimate is drawn from it at step 1.
    - *CRE p.251 l.22–24:* the background U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V) is defined using Λ₁^∼∖Λ₁. This is a definition at the original Λ₁, which V4 keeps (Section c5-3-1, the convention after Lemma c5-3-0) and (P) does not change. Since Λ ⊂ Λ₁, a function of V on Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ is a function of V on Ω₁∩Λᶜ, which is the locality in V that p.251 asks for; step 2's determining set is #RP.3-1-1. This is item (δ) of §3c(4⁗)(c).
    - *LFR I p.178* defines the step-j functions: (1.8) for □′ ⊂ (Ω_{j+1}∖Λ_{j+1}^∼)∩Z and (1.9) for □′ ⊂ (Λ_{j+1}^∼∖Λ_{j+1})∩Z, and ζχ "is equal to the product of the following seven groups" (1.3)–(1.9) under its condition (ii). Under (P) the product has an eighth group, the collar tests.
    - *LFR I's uses of (1.9).* On p.179 the functions (1.5)–(1.9) "are not gauge invariant with respect to gauge transformations of the variables V_j" (l.2–3), which motivates the gauge fixing; on p.183, (1.28) completes them to Z∩Ω by a decomposition of unity; on p.187 l.36–39 they are in the list of the remaining functions. (1.29) is stated on p.183 and proved on pp.183–187; p.185 proves its (1.8) and (1.7) parts with (1.38) on □′^{∼2} for □′ ⊂ Ω_{j+1}^∼∖Λ_{j+1}^∼, which under (P) are S₁-cubes of the original Λ₁, outside Λ₁^∼ ⊃ Λ^∼. The proof uses the restrictions only as upper bounds on |A_j|.
    - *The good collar tests in LFR I.* They are sublevel indicators of the same variables A_j(b), at threshold ϑg_j⁻¹δ_j. (1.28) and (1.29) are identities, which hold with the extra factor on both sides, and p.187's list gains them. Under the gauge transformations that LFR needs at h = 1 they are invariant, as A ↦ R(u)A and |·|_σ is Ad-invariant (#RP.2-12-1, #RP.2-12-2).
    - *CRE (2.41)* uses Z_j^∼ for the 𝐁-localisation (p.261), with Z_j = Λ_jᶜ (p.255 l.25), so Z₁ = Λᶜ under (P). This is a definition from Λ, not a use of (α).
  - So (α), in sublevel-set form, holds at step 1, and the later steps accept the refined integrand.
- *M8: the format of 𝐁, by the Neumann series.*
  - By V4 (I3), γ ≤ H_DD ≤ h_+ on D = D(Λ), a principal restriction of I. So C^{(0)}(Λ) = h_+⁻¹Σ_{n≥0}K^n, with K := 1 − H_DD/h_+ and 0 ≤ K ≤ 1 − γ/h_+.
  - By V4 (I1), (K^n)_{bb′} = 0 unless d(b,b′) ≤ nρ_L. It depends on D(Λ) only within nρ_L of b, and on U₁ only within nρ_L + 3L of b, since the stencil of an entry (b,b′) lies within 3L of b₋ (V4 Lemma c2-entry-range).
  - *The off-diagonal blocks.* For a row b ∈ D, V4 (I2) gives Σ_e‖H(b,e)‖ ≤ m_L, so every entry has norm ≤ m_L. By V4 (I1), H(b,e) ≠ 0 only if |z(b)−z(e)|₁ ≤ 2, so a column e meets at most 164L⁴ rows. Schur's test gives ‖H_{DJ(Λ)}‖ ≤ (m_L·164L⁴m_L)^{1/2} ≤ 13L²m_L =: C_{DJ}, and ‖H_{J(Λ)D}‖ = ‖H_{DJ(Λ)}‖, as H is symmetric.
  - So the Schur cross term is Σ_n h_+⁻¹⟨x, H_{xD}K^nH_{Dy}y⟩. Its n-th term is a function of the fields within (n+2)ρ_L of x, has norm ≤ C_{DJ}²h_+⁻¹(1−γ/h_+)^n|x||y|, and vanishes unless (n+2)ρ_L ≥ d(x,y) ≥ ℓ/2.
  - This is a localised representation with exponential decay in the diameter. The format of 𝐁-terms is (2.41)–(2.42) (p.261), which CRE supplies at Λ (§3c(4⁗)(a), *Their 𝐁-format*). U₁'s own dependence on V is localised as in CRE: p.251 l.22 determines U₁(Ω₁∩Λ₁ᶜ,V) on Λ₁^∼∖Λ₁ "by the set 𝐁₁(Λ₁ᶜ)", and p.252 l.22 calls the result "the desired localization property".
- *M9: adjacency, per use.*
  - Fill: face adjacency of ℓ-cubes (V4 Section c5-3-1, which notes that "CRE p.251 does not specify its adjacency"; its proofs hold for any symmetric adjacency under which parallelepipeds are connected).
  - The components of (2.19) and (2.21): ∗-adjacency. This is the ℓ/2 rule of §3c(4⁗)(a) at the cube level, in the direction used: every ℓ/2-component of Ω₁∩Λᶜ lies in one ∗-component of Λᶜ.
  - p.258 leaves the adjacency of its components unspecified ("in the same component"). §3c(2)(b) uses only that CRE's (2.19) components at Λ₁ are ∗-connected, so each lies in one ∗-component of Λᶜ; it derives this for components in Bałaban's sense (common walls, RG1 p.257 l.8–10) and in the topological sense.

## 3g. D1.3 Continued: Carrier Dependence of the Reference Species and the Locality of D(Λ)

**(1) Carrier Locality of the Reference Species (V4 Lemma c5-6-2 for Two Arbitrary Carriers).**
- *Setting.* The carriers are I and D(Λ) := {b ∈ I : b has an endpoint in Λ} for regions Λ ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)} (V4 Section c5-2-3). For a carrier D and a union Z of LM-cubes put E_D(Z) := {b ∈ D : b has an endpoint in Z}. The Hessians at all carriers are principal restrictions of the one H_ξ, and the reference ones of the one H° (proof of V4 Lemma c5-6-2; Remark c5-5-11).
- *(a) Selected integrals.* Let D, D′ be carriers with E_D(Z) = E_{D′}(Z). Then the selections and split selections with final domain Z are the same at D and D′, and at t = 0 the selected integrals coincide, for the actual and the reference data: k^{f,0}_D(Z) = k^{f,0}_{D′}(Z), ν^{f,0}_{D,Z} = ν^{f,0}_{D′,Z}, k^{f,∘}_D(Z) = k^{f,∘}_{D′}(Z), ν̂^{f,∘}_{D,Z} = ν̂^{f,∘}_{D′,Z}.
  - *Proof.* V4 Lemma c5-6-2 proves this for (D, D′) = (D(Λ), I) under "every bond of I with an endpoint in Z lies in D(Λ)", which is E_{D(Λ)}(Z) = E_I(Z). Its proof uses the hypothesis only in that form. The uses, in order:
    - the young part of a primitive's free-input set lies in E_D(Y) = E_D(Z) ∩ E(Y) for its host Y ⊆ Z ("the same set for both carriers by hypothesis");
    - probe hosts Y_b ⊆ Z have b ∈ E_D(Y_b), so the 𝐃₀-hosts inside Z are the same;
    - the bad bonds 𝖯 meet Z, so 𝖯 ⊆ E_D(Z), and the condition 𝖯 ⊆ D ∖ E(Y₀) is the same at both carriers;
    - E₀ = E_D(Z₀) = E_D(Z) ∩ E(Z₀), so C_{E₀} has the same entries;
    - every bond entering S_{Z₀}(σ) and Γ_{E₀}(σ) is a carrier bond in Z: walks have D(ω) ⊂ Z, the walk blocks are entries of powers of id − H/s_H over carrier bonds in D(ω), and the rows e ∈ E₀ use b′ ⊂ int D ⊂ Z;
    - the X-integral (Γ_{E₀}(σ)X involves X on bonds of Z only) and the Dirac tensors.
  - Where V4 writes "in D(Λ) by hypothesis", the general case uses E_D(Z) = E_{D′}(Z). No step uses D(Λ) ⊆ I.
- *(b) Groups.* For a group W let F(W) be its footprint (V4 Section c4-6-2) and Ẑ(W) the union of the cubes of F(W). If E_D(Ẑ(W)) = E_{D′}(Ẑ(W)), then W is a group at both carriers, w°_D(W) = w°_{D′}(W), and a^D_W = a^{D′}_W.
  - Each root and constituent domain lies in Ẑ(W), so (a) applies to each.
  - The probe label sets 𝖫_Z are carrier bonds b with Y_b ⊆ Z, so b ∈ E_D(Z). The contact sets and hulls are then determined by the domains and the contour (V4 Lemma c5-6-2).
  - The Ursell coefficients and 1/k(W)! depend on the incompatibility graph only.
  - a_W = w°(W)∫χ(P°_W(a)) d⊗ν̂^{f,∘}(a) (V4 (eq:part1-reference-group)) integrates the reference word, whose links depend on the root variables only, against the same measures.
- *(c) Clusters and q°.* Incompatibility is footprint contact or a shared traversal (V4 Lemma c4-6-env(d)). It does not involve the carrier. For a cluster Γ put Ẑ(Γ) := ⋃_{W∈Γ}Ẑ(W). If E_D(Ẑ(Γ)) = E_{D′}(Ẑ(Γ)), then Φ°_D(Γ) = Φ°_{D′}(Γ), by (b).
  - Call Γ *marked* (by D, D′) if Ẑ(Γ) contains an endpoint of a bond of D △ D′. A cluster present at one carrier only contains a group present at one carrier only, so it is marked, by (b). Put Φ° := 0 at a carrier where Γ is not a cluster.
  - With q°_D(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅}Φ°_D(Γ)) (V4 Lemma c5-5-3(d)), for every compatible family 𝔅 of groups present at both carriers:

    log q°_D(𝔅) − log q°_{D′}(𝔅) = −Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅, Γ marked} [Φ°_D(Γ) − Φ°_{D′}(Γ)].

  - The same holds for log S° = Σ_Γ Φ°(Γ), with the sum over all marked Γ. For (D(Λ), I) this is the first line of Lemma c5-6-3's proof.
- *(d) Bounds, with the cluster weight kept.* At D(Λ) (and at I, by the closing remark of V4 Lemma c5-5-3(d)), V4 applies Kotecký–Preiss to the gas with activities |a_W|e^{3νσ(W)/2} and node cost νσ(W)/8 (proof of Lemma c5-5-3(d)). The cluster coefficient of that gas has modulus |φ^T(Γ)|∏_{W′∈Γ}|a_{W′}|e^{3νσ(W′)/2} = |Φ°(Γ)|e^{3νσ(Γ)/2}, with σ(Γ) := Σ_{W′∈Γ}σ(W′). So the rooted conclusion, before V4 drops the weight, is

  Σ_{Γ∋W} m_W(Γ)|Φ°(Γ)|e^{3νσ(Γ)/2} ≤ |a_W|e^{13νσ(W)/8}.

  - With |a_W| ≤ M_Λ(W) and the pin sum Σ_{W: Δ∈F(W)}e^{4νσ(W)}M_Λ(W) ≤ C_pinρ_t (Lemma c5-5-8(a), first display, as used in Lemma c5-6-3), for every cube Δ:

    Σ_{Γ hits Δ}|Φ°(Γ)|e^{3νσ(Γ)/2} ≤ C_pinρ_t.
