  - Hence, with 𝒩(D, D′) the cubes containing an endpoint of a bond of D △ D′, the difference in (c) is at most Σ_{Δ∈𝒩(D,D′)} Σ_{Γ hits Δ and 𝔅}(|Φ°_D(Γ)| + |Φ°_{D′}(Γ)|), and each Δ contributes ≤ 2C_pinρ_t e^{−3νσ_*(Δ,𝔅)/2}, where σ_*(Δ,𝔅) is the least σ(Γ) of a cluster hitting both. For (D(Λ), I) this is Lemma c5-6-3 with the weight kept.
- *(d′) Diameter.* σ(W) := 𝔰(W) = n(W) + |ℋ(W)|/(LM) (V4 Lemma c5-5-8, (eq:c4-6-1)), the size of V4 Lemma c4-6-env. That lemma gives F(W) ⊆ 𝔈(W), with the envelope 𝔈(W) connected under max-norm adjacency of cubes and |𝔈(W)| ≤ A_Eσ(W) ((a), (b)). Incompatible groups have footprints in contact or share a traversal n ∈ ℋ(W) ∩ ℋ(W′) ((d)), and π₁(ℋ(W)) ⊆ 𝔈(W).
  - So for a cluster Γ (connected in the incompatibility graph) the envelope union 𝔈(Γ) := ⋃_{W∈Γ}𝔈(W) is connected, with at most A_Eσ(Γ) cubes. A connected set of k cubes has fine ℓ^∞ diameter < kLM (proof of (c)). Hence Ẑ(Γ) ⊆ 𝔈(Γ) and diam 𝔈(Γ) ≤ A_E·LM·σ(Γ).
  - With (d): for every cube Δ and R ≥ 0, Σ_{Γ hits Δ, diam 𝔈(Γ) ≥ R}|Φ°(Γ)| ≤ C_pinρ_t·e^{−3νR/(2A_E LM)}.
  - In (c), a marked cluster hitting 𝔅 has 𝔈(Γ) containing a cube of 𝒩(D, D′) and a cube within index distance one of 𝔈(W) for some W ∈ 𝔅. So with d := dist(𝒩(D, D′), ⋃_{W∈𝔅}𝔈(W)) − LM, the difference in (c) is at most 2C_pinρ_t|𝒩(D, D′)|e^{−3νd/(2A_E LM)}.
- *(e) What (1) gives.* E_{D(Λ)}(Z) is a function of the ℓ-cubes of Λ that contain a site within distance 1 of Z. So every reference species of a labelled term (a^Λ_W, w°_Λ(W), Φ°_Λ(Γ), hence q°_Λ and S°_Λ) depends on the label only through Λ near its footprint. This is Remark c5-6-5's "what is local is the marking, not the domain", now for any two labels, which is the comparison D2 makes.
- *(f) Not covered by (1).*
  - *The t > 0 species* (Υ¹_W and the terms of 𝓑^Λ). Their selected integrals contain the shift m, which depends on the carrier through C^{(0)}(D) = H_DD⁻¹ (§3f(3) M8 gives its Neumann series: exponential decay, not exact locality). So (a) does not apply to them as it stands. Their carrier dependence is derived in §3j, with the local shift m̂.

**(2) Locality of D(Λ) ∩ Y.**
- D(Λ) ∩ Y = {b ∈ I ∩ Y : b has an endpoint in Λ} is a function of the ℓ-cubes of Λ containing an endpoint of a bond of Y, with Λ = Λ₁^fin.
- Λ₁^fin near Y is not a function of the tests near Y, since a retreat can propagate from round to round (V4 Section c5-3-1). What is used is the label sum in polymer form:
  - Σ_lab 1_lab(…) = Σ_Λ χ_Λ(…), χ_Λ = 1{Λ₁^fin = Λ} (§3c(4⁗)(d) *Index by Λ*);
  - at fixed outer labels (Ω₁, Λ₁) and on the range Λ ⊂ Λ₁^{(0)}, χ_Λ = ∏_X χ_{Λ,X} over the ∗-components X of Λᶜ, with χ_{Λ,X} the indicator of the local run (§3c(4⁗)(d) *Claim*). It is a function of the tests at bonds with both endpoints in X and of 𝔅₀ ∩ X (*Locality in 𝔅₀* there), and 𝔅₀ = (Fill(Λ₁))ᶜ is a function of the outer label Λ₁ (V4 Lemma c5-3-0(a));
  - on the term labelled Λ, every carrier-dependent quantity is a function of Λ near its own support: E^∂_{D(Λ)} through D(Λ) ∩ X for each of its sets X (§3b), and the reference species through Λ near their footprints ((1)(e)).
- So at fixed (Ω₁, Λ₁) the sum over Λ has the form of CRE's sum over Λ₁. The components of Λᶜ are the polymers, each with its own indicator, and every other factor depends on Λ locally. The sum over Λ₁ is CRE's own, and the later steps carry the pair (Λ₁, Λ) as in §3c(4⁗)(d) *The extra label*.
- *Size of the polymers.* Each component X of Λᶜ lies in ⋃_{G⊂X}G^{∼107} and carries the factor of its generators P₁ ∪ Q₁ ∪ R₁ ∪ W₁ (§3f(2) Minor 4 *Geometry*, R1 (ii)).

## 3i. D2, First Item: The Thin Form of E^∂

**(0) Why It Is Needed.** At step 2, e^{E^∂_{D(Λ)}} enters Bałaban's large-field polymer gas. A Kotecký–Preiss weight a(X) for a defect polymer X must be at least the mass of the large-field polymers that meet X, about ε·#(cubes of X), and at most what its activity pays. §3b's sets X = B_{m+1} have #(cubes) ≍ (m/a₂)⁴ for step-2 cubes of side a₂, and activity e^{−a₀m}. So ε(m/a₂)⁴ ≤ a₀m fails for large m, whatever ε is.
A bound of |E^∂_D| alone gives no polymer activities. What the KP route needs is a decomposition of E^∂ into thin sets with a rooted bound per step-2 cube, and (7) gives it: Σ_{X∩Q≠∅}|e_X|e^{κ_{a₂}#cubes(X)} ≤ (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ² for every step-2 cube Q of side a₂ ((7) *Rooted form*). The comparisons of (a₂/ℓ₀+2)⁴C_rτ² with κ_{a₂}, and of κ_{a₂} with the step-2 large-field mass per cube, are D26 7.4(b).

**(1) The Source of the Non-Locality.** The regional integrals are Gaussian integrals with covariance C_{D,T} = H_{D,T}⁻¹, H_{D,T} = i_{D,T}^*H_Ti_{D,T} (V4 (eq:regional-periodic-weight), the torus form of (eq:regional-precision) and (eq:regional-integrals)). The cutoff Θ_D^σ of (eq:regional-weight) and the interaction P_{D,T} are local in D, and so is the precision H_{D,T}.
So are the source factor and the restricted free-input supports Λ∩D of the paragraph after (eq:regional-integrals); Λ there is V4's free-input support, not the region Λ of this note. The Gaussian normalisation cancels in the normalised ratios. The non-locality is only in the inverse.

**(2) The Method.** Bałaban localises such inverses in RG2 (2.1)–(2.13), pp.12–14: a Mayer expansion of the action density (2.1) and the expansion χ_k = Σ_P(−1)^{|P|}χ_{k,Y₀}χ^c_{k,P} of the characteristic functions off the localisation domain (2.3); conditioning on Z₀ᶜ (2.5) and the change of variables to the standard Gaussian dμ₀(X) (2.6); random walk expansions of C^{(k)}(Z₀), (C^{(k)})^{1/2} and Δ_k with one parameter s(Δ) per cube (2.7), and the localisation Σ_ZΠ_{Δ⊂Z∖Z₀′}∫₀¹ds(Δ)∂/∂s(Δ) (2.8). The activities H(Z) are localised in Z, factorise over the components of Z (2.10) and are exponentiated (2.12).
CRE pp.276–278 repeat the scheme, citing RG2 as "Sect. 6 [I]" and "(I.7.3)–(I.7.13)", that is RG2 §1 and (2.3)–(2.13).
- Here (4) expands every principal-restriction inverse in box walks with parameters s_Δ; (5) reruns V4's cluster bounds with every covariance block replaced by its expansion at complex s; and (6) localises in s by the identity behind (2.8).
- RG2's conditioning step (2.5)–(2.6) is not needed, since V4's clusters are already Gaussian principal marginals on their carriers, with fixed bounded integrands ((5)).
- RG2 pp.12–14 state nothing about complex s, and RG2's integrand G contains dμ_{C^{(k)}(Z₀,s(Z))}, so the interpolated covariances must stay accretive. Complex s is admissible on a polydisc on which every complex covariance block is within λ/16 of the real one in operator norm and the complex entries decay exponentially, uniformly on the polydisc. (4) proves both for the full matrix when |s_Δ| ≤ R and ℓ₀ ≥ ℓ₀(R, L, G), and (5) passes the first to the blocks.

**(4) Box Walk Expansion of Principal-Restriction Inverses.**
- *Setting.* H = H_T is the flat torus Hessian of §3b, and d_T is the periodic metric of V4 (eq:marginal-covariance-input).
  - *Range.* H_T = 𝖢^*Δ₁𝖢 (V4 (eq:flat-covariance)) is the torus form of (eq:bulk-operator-definition), and the maps composed there have finite range and the same local formulas on every compatible torus (V4 Appendix app:flat-covariance, after (eq:bulk-operator-definition): "Each map has finite range"). So H_T(b,c) = 0 for d_T(b,c) > R_H, with R_H the range of H (V4, the paragraph before (eq:regional-distance)), which depends on L, G and the fixed reconstruction, not on the torus.
  - *Spectrum.* Every principal restriction H_{AA} has γ_L ≤ H_{AA} ≤ h_L, by §3b (Q3): the Rayleigh quotient applied to the gap and row-sum bound that V4 states on the compatible tori (the paragraph after (eq:marginal-covariance-input)).
  - *Decay.* The proof of V4 Lemma c5-1-1 is a Neumann series that uses only the two-sided bound and the range. With (γ_L, h_L, R_H, d_T) for (γ, h_+, ρ_L, d) it gives ‖(H_{AA})⁻¹(b,c)‖ ≤ γ_L⁻¹e^{−μ_Td_T(b,c)}, μ_T := γ_L/(h_LR_H), for every bond set A; μ_T is not V4's covariance exponent μ of (eq:marginal-covariance-input). Put σ(T) := γ_L⁻¹Σ_{t≥T}32(2t+1)³e^{−μ_Tt}; at most 32(2t+1)³ bonds lie at distance t from a bond (§3f(2) (D-c)).
- *Boxes.* Fix an integer ℓ₀ ≥ max(R_H + 1, c_supp + 2), chosen after R (*Constants*), with c_supp = R_* + R_K of §3b(2); the second bound is used only for connectivity in (6). Partition each coordinate circle into intervals with lengths in [ℓ₀, 2ℓ₀), the whole circle if its period is < ℓ₀, and let the boxes Δ be the products of these intervals.
  - Such partitions exist for every period p ≥ ℓ₀: with n := ⌊p/ℓ₀⌋ parts, nℓ₀ ≤ p ≤ n(2ℓ₀ − 1), since (n+1)ℓ₀ − 1 ≤ n(2ℓ₀ − 1) for n, ℓ₀ ≥ 1, and the parts get lengths ℓ₀ + r_i with 0 ≤ r_i ≤ ℓ₀ − 1 and Σr_i = p − nℓ₀ ≤ n(ℓ₀ − 1).
  - A bond belongs to the box of its initial site. Δ̃ := bonds within ℓ₀ of Δ, Δ̂ := bonds within R_H of Δ̃. Any bond lies in Δ̂ for at most N_nb := 7⁴ boxes (in fact ≤ 5⁴), and a box has at most N_nb boxes within ℓ₀ + R_H of it; N_nb does not depend on ℓ₀. Full-circle intervals only lower these counts; Δ ⊆ Δ̃ and the distance bound below hold by the definition of Δ̃, and the step bound below uses only the upper side bound 2ℓ₀. So no condition on the periods is needed beyond V4's compatible torus.
- *Parametrix.* 𝒢 below is the parametrix, not the gauge term 𝒢 of V4 (eq:regular-covariance-hessian) or the source 𝒢_S of (eq:regional-wilson-polynomial). Fix a bond set D′. For each box, G_Δ := (H_{Δ̃∩D′,Δ̃∩D′})⁻¹ extended by zero, χ_Δ := the projection onto 𝔤^{Δ∩D′}, 𝒢 := Σ_ΔG_Δχ_Δ, K_Δ := −1_{D′∖Δ̃}HG_Δχ_Δ.
  - For u supported in Δ̃∩D′, H_{D′D′}u equals H_{Δ̃∩D′}u on Δ̃∩D′, and 1_{D′∖Δ̃}Hu off it. Since Σ_Δχ_Δ = 1_{D′}, this gives H_{D′D′}𝒢 = 1_{D′} − K with K := Σ_ΔK_Δ.
  - K_Δ uses only entries G_Δ(y,z) with z ∈ Δ and y ∈ Δ̃ within R_H of D′∖Δ̃, so d_T(y,z) ≥ ℓ₀ − R_H. By the Schur test, ‖K_Δ‖ ≤ h_Lσ(ℓ₀ − R_H) =: ε_w.
- *Walks.* Fix R ≥ 1; (6) takes R ≥ 2. For s = (s_Δ) ∈ ℂ^{boxes} with |s_Δ| ≤ R, put K(s) := Σ_Δ s_ΔK_Δ.
  - The pieces χ_Δf are orthogonal, and K_Δχ_Δf is supported in Δ̂∖Δ̃, which meets each bond for at most N_nb boxes. So ‖K(s)‖ ≤ q := N_nb^{1/2}Rε_w. In the same way ‖𝒢‖ ≤ N_nb^{1/2}γ_L⁻¹.
  - For q < 1 put C_{D′}(s) := 𝒢(1 − K(s))⁻¹ = Σ_{n≥0}𝒢K(s)ⁿ. Expanding K(s)ⁿ gives the walks ω = (Δ₀,…,Δ_n): the term s_{Δ₁}⋯s_{Δ_n}G_{Δ₀}χ_{Δ₀}K_{Δ₁}⋯K_{Δ_n} is nonzero only if consecutive boxes lie within ℓ₀ + R_H. Its (b,c) entry needs b ∈ Δ̃₀ and c ∈ Δ_n.
  - At s = 1, C_{D′}(1) = (H_{D′D′})⁻¹ = C_{D′}, since H_{D′D′}𝒢(1−K)⁻¹ = 1.
  - *Symmetric form.* C^{sym}_{D′}(s) := ½(C_{D′}(s) + C_{D′}(s)ᵀ), with ᵀ the transpose for the real bondwise inner product. It is complex symmetric, and C^{sym}_{D′}(1) = C_{D′}.
- *Locality.* The walk term of ω depends on D′ only through D′ ∩ (Δ̂₀ ∪ … ∪ Δ̂_n), and so does its transpose.
- *Bounds.*
  - ‖C^{sym}_{D′}(s) − C_{D′}‖ ≤ ‖𝒢‖‖(1−K(s))⁻¹ − (1−K)⁻¹‖ ≤ N_nbγ_L⁻¹ε_w(R+1)(1−q)⁻² (resolvent identity).
  - *Range of a walk.* Between arbitrary bonds of consecutive boxes the displacement can reach 6ℓ₀ − 3, so the bound is chained through the intermediate bonds. For n ≥ 1, b ∈ Δ̃₀ lies within ℓ₀ + (2ℓ₀ − 1) = 3ℓ₀ − 1 of a bond x₀ ∈ Δ₀ ∩ Δ̂₁. For 1 ≤ i ≤ n, x_{i−1} ∈ Δ̂_i lies within (ℓ₀ + R_H) + (2ℓ₀ − 1) ≤ 4ℓ₀ − 2 of the next bond x_i ∈ Δ_i ∩ Δ̂_{i+1}, with x_n := c (R_H ≤ ℓ₀ − 1). So d_T(b,c) ≤ 3ℓ₀ − 1 + n(4ℓ₀ − 2) ≤ 5ℓ₀(n+1), which also holds for n = 0, and the (b,c) entry of 𝒢K(s)ⁿ vanishes unless n ≥ d_T(b,c)/(5ℓ₀) − 1. Bounding each entry by the norm of 𝒢K(s)ⁿ, ‖C^{sym}_{D′}(s)(b,c)‖ ≤ N_nb^{1/2}γ_L⁻¹(1−q)⁻¹q^{d_T(b,c)/(5ℓ₀)−1}.
- *Constants.* ε_w ≤ C(ℓ₀)³e^{−μ_Tℓ₀}. For ℓ₀ ≥ ℓ₀(R, L, G), q ≤ e^{−μ_Tℓ₀/2}, ‖C^{sym}_{D′}(s) − C_{D′}‖ ≤ λ/16 with λ = h_L⁻¹, and the entry decay rate log(1/q)/(5ℓ₀) is ≥ μ′ := μ_T/10, independent of R and ℓ₀. All of this holds for every |s_Δ| ≤ R, in particular with s_Δ = 0 off any set of boxes.

**(5) V4's Cluster Bounds at C^{sym}_{D′}(s).**
- *How the clusters see the covariance.* A label selection J has the carrier A_J of V4 (eq:marginal-selection), and its term is ∫F_JG_S dμ_{(C)_{A_J}}, a principal marginal (the paragraph after (eq:marginal-selection)) with a fixed bounded integrand (the paragraph after (eq:marginal-density-definition)). The forest formula (eq:marginal-forest-identity) differentiates the density p_{C_A(x)} of (eq:marginal-density-definition) in the pair variables x_{bc} at w^F ∈ 𝒦_A, and no hard cutoff or Wilson function is differentiated.
  The regional expansion is the same with C_{D′} and the restricted supports (the paragraph after (eq:regional-integrals); on the torus, the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). So a cluster weight φ_{D′}(Γ) is a function of the blocks (C_{D′})_{A_γ} of its components, through the densities p_M and the edge entries.
- *Replacement.* Define φ_{D′}(Γ;s) by replacing every block (C_{D′})_{A_γ} with (C^{sym}_{D′}(s))_{A_γ}. Then φ_{D′}(Γ;1) = φ_{D′}(Γ).
- *Conjugation equivariance.* H_T, principal restriction, inversion, χ_Δ, the indicators 1_{set} and the scalars s_Δ commute with constant Ad_u, and transposition preserves this since Ad_u is orthogonal. So C^{sym}_{D′}(s) commutes with constant Ad, as C_T does in V4 (the paragraph after (eq:marginal-component-functional)), and the τ^{𝟏{|S|=1}} gain of (eq:marginal-fixed-forest-bound) (the paragraph after it) and the τ² source loads of (eq:marginal-load-series) hold at complex s.
- *V4's estimate at the replaced blocks.*
  - Base point: (C_{D′})_{A}(w^F) is real, with λ ≤ · ≤ Λ ("Block restriction and convexity give λI ≤ C_A(x) ≤ ΛI throughout 𝒦_A", V4 after (eq:marginal-density-definition); C_{D′} has spectrum in [h_L⁻¹, γ_L⁻¹] = [λ, Λ] by (4) *Setting*). Since w^F is a convex combination of partition matrices, (δ)_A(w^F) is a convex combination of block-diagonal pinchings of δ_A. So with δ := C^{sym}_{D′}(s) − C_{D′}, ‖(δ)_A(w^F)‖ ≤ ‖δ‖ ≤ λ/16 ((4)).
  - Take V4's Cauchy radius (eq:marginal-edge-radius) with 16 in place of 8 and ‖C^{sym}_{D′}(s)(b,c)‖ in place of ‖C_T(b,c)‖. The forest perturbation then has norm ≤ λ/16 (the sentence after (eq:marginal-edge-radius)), and the total distance from the real base point is ≤ λ/8.
  - So every M on the Cauchy circles satisfies (eq:marginal-complex-density) verbatim, and (eq:marginal-density-majorant) holds. The determinant branch is continued from the real base point inside the convex set {M complex symmetric : Re M ≥ 7λ/8}.
  - (eq:marginal-fixed-forest-bound) holds with 16 for 8 and the edge factors ‖C^{sym}_{D′}(s)(b,c)‖ ≤ A′e^{−μ′d_T(b,c)}, A′ := N_nb^{1/2}γ_L⁻¹(1−q)⁻¹q⁻¹.
  - The degree payment (eq:marginal-degree-payment), the counting (eq:marginal-counting-weight) and (eq:marginal-rooted-tree-count), the load series (eq:marginal-load-series), the extent weights of Section extent-weights and the rooted majorant (eq:regional-square-rooted-majorant) use the edge majorants, the carrier counts, the τ-factors, s_*, q_*, R_*, the interval constant c_h of (eq:marginal-hull-extent) and the one-mark gain.
    Only the edge majorants change. So these steps hold with μ′, A′ in place of V4's μ, A of (eq:marginal-covariance-input), with constants fixed before the coupling. Write Ū(Γ) and η̄ for the resulting pinned-tree majorant and extent rate: Σ_{Γ: j∈J_Γ}Ū(Γ)e^{η̄ℓ(Γ)} ≤ Cτ².
- *Analyticity.* Each component functional is an integral of a fixed bounded function against ∂_Fp_M, with M analytic in s and the uniform majorant above. So φ_{D′}(Γ;s) is analytic on |s_Δ| < R, with |φ_{D′}(Γ;s)| ≤ Ū(Γ) there (the Ursell coefficients do not depend on the covariance).
- *Order of constants.* μ′ = μ_T/10 is fixed by L and the group. V4 fixes its extent rates from the decay exponents alone: the rooted majorant (eq:regional-square-rooted-majorant) is at η′ = μ₁/1024, with μ₁ the edge exponent left after the degree payment and at most one eighth of the covariance and comparison exponents (the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). Here η̄ := μ₁′/1024, with μ₁′ ≤ μ′/8 the corresponding exponent; the comparison exponent does not enter, since (6) replaces V4's boundary comparison. η̄ does not depend on A′.
  Then κ and R ((6)), then ℓ₀ = ℓ₀(R, L, G), then A′, K̄₂ and C̄′_* below, and the coupling. R and ℓ₀ do not enter μ′ or η̄, so the order is not circular.
- *New coupling threshold.* V4's α′ = μ₁/(1024(1+c_h)) (the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction) is taken at V4's μ₁. Put ᾱ := μ₁′/(1024(1+c_h)), and let K̄₂, C̄′_* be V4's K₂, C′_* re-run at μ′, A′ and the factor 16. g₀ is chosen last so that K̄₂^{s_*}τ ≤ 1/2, C̄′_*τ ≤ 1/4 and C̄′_*τ² ≤ ᾱ/4, V4's conditions before (eq:regional-square-rooted-majorant) at the new constants; V4's own thresholds stay in force separately. A′ depends on ℓ₀, which is fixed before the coupling.

**(6) Localisation in the Box Parameters.**
- *The identity.* Fix a source cluster Γ of 𝒞_{D′}, D′ ∈ {D, T}. On the finite torus, s ↦ φ_{D′}(Γ;s) is analytic on |s_Δ| < R ((5)). The identity behind RG2 (2.8), F(s_Δ=1) = F(s_Δ=0) + ∫₀¹∂_{s_Δ}F ds_Δ in each box parameter, multiplied out over the boxes, gives
  φ_{D′}(Γ) = Σ_Y f_{Γ,D′}(Y), f_{Γ,D′}(Y) := ∫_{[0,1]^Y}∂^Yφ_{D′}(Γ; s_Y, 0_{Yᶜ}) ds_Y,
  over the sets Y of boxes.
- *Which Y occur (admissible Y).* At (s_Y, 0) only walks with Δ₁,…,Δ_n ∈ Y contribute, and an entry (b,c) of a block of Γ needs b ∈ Δ̃₀ and c ∈ Δ_n with b, c ∈ A_Γ. So ∂_{s_Δ} vanishes unless Δ lies on such a walk. Hence f_{Γ,D′}(Y) = 0 unless every box of Y lies on a walk Δ₀, Δ₁, …, Δ_n with Δ̃₀ ∩ A_Γ ≠ ∅ (Δ₀ need not lie in Y), Δ₁, …, Δ_n ∈ Y, consecutive boxes within ℓ₀ + R_H, and Δ_n meeting A_Γ. Y is *admissible* (for Γ) when every box of Y lies on such a walk; only admissible Y occur.
- *The set.* Let B₀(Γ) be the boxes Δ₀ with Δ̃₀ ∩ A_Γ ≠ ∅. Let 𝒩(Γ) be V4's support network of Γ (Section extent-weights, the paragraph after (eq:marginal-hull-extent)): the carrier A_Γ, lattice paths for the edges of a spanning tree of F_γ ∪ Q_{J_γ} for each component γ, the attachment paths (length ≤ R_*) of the source contacts, and the fine contour bonds of the intervals h_W of the webs of Γ.
  The choices are fixed by a rule that depends only on the index of Γ: the lexicographically first spanning tree in a fixed order of the bonds, and, for each tree edge and for each contact to the nearest bond of its restricted stencil, the lexicographically first shortest lattice path. The counts in *Size* and the connectivity below hold for every such choice.
  X(Γ,Y) = X_{D′}(Γ,Y) is the union of the boxes that contain a bond of supp Γ (§3b(2)), of 𝒩(Γ), or of Δ̂ for some Δ ∈ Y ∪ B₀(Γ).
  Here A_Γ, B₀(Γ) and 𝒩(Γ) are those of Γ as a cluster at the carrier D′ of the term: the D′-restricted carrier and stencils (V4, the paragraph after (eq:regional-integrals)) and the restricted support network ("Contacts remain within R_* of the restricted support network", the proof of Prop. regional-square-extraction). The web intervals are the shortest cyclic intervals of the source positions on the contour (same proof), so they do not depend on D′. So X_{D′}(Γ,Y) depends on D′ only through D′ ∩ supp Γ.
  For a D-interior Γ (supp Γ ⊆ D, §3b(2)) the restricted objects at D are the original ones, which are those at T, so X_D(Γ,Y) = X_T(Γ,Y).
  - *Locality.* By (4) (Locality) and §3b(2), f_{Γ,D′}(Y) and the indicator Γ ∈ 𝒞_{D′} depend on D′ only through D′ ∩ (supp Γ ∪ ⋃_{Δ∈Y∪B₀(Γ)}Δ̂) ⊆ D′ ∩ X(Γ,Y).
  - *Connectivity.* Connected means ∗-connected: two boxes are ∗-adjacent when they contain sites at ℓ∞ distance ≤ 1. The boxes containing the bonds of a lattice-connected bond set form a ∗-connected family, since consecutive bonds of a lattice path have initial sites at ℓ∞ distance ≤ 1.
    - 𝒩(Γ) is lattice-connected: "In a cluster, incompatible objects meet through carriers or contour intervals, so these networks form a connected union" (V4, the paragraph after (eq:marginal-hull-extent)).
    - Each Δ̂ (the bonds within ℓ₀ + R_H of a box) is lattice-connected. For Δ₀ ∈ B₀(Γ), Δ̂₀ ⊇ Δ̃₀ meets A_Γ ⊆ 𝒩(Γ).
    - Each Δ ∈ Y lies on a walk Δ₀ ∈ B₀(Γ), Δ₁, …, Δ_m = Δ with Δ₁, …, Δ_m ∈ Y and nonzero term (*Which Y occur*). The output of K_{Δ_{i+1}} lies in Δ̂_{i+1} and meets the input box Δ_i, so Δ̂_i ∩ Δ̂_{i+1} ⊇ Δ_i ∩ Δ̂_{i+1} ≠ ∅.
    - Every label of a nonzero cluster at D′ has nonempty restricted support inside A_Γ, the D′-restricted carrier of *The set*: the primitives of P_{D′,T} meet D′ (V4 (eq:regional-periodic-weight)), cutoff labels lie in D′ (Θ_{D′} is a product over b ∈ D′, (eq:regional-weight)), and "A source mark with empty restricted stencil is zero" (the paragraph after (eq:regional-integrals)).
      With original label diameters ≤ R_* and stencil diameters ≤ 2c_st, every bond of supp Γ therefore lies within c_supp of A_Γ (§3b(2)). Since ℓ₀ ≥ c_supp + 2, its initial site is within ℓ∞ distance < ℓ₀ of the initial site of a carrier bond. Boxes have sides ≥ ℓ₀ or are full circles, so the two boxes are equal or ∗-adjacent.
    So X(Γ,Y) is ∗-connected. Without 𝒩(Γ) it need not be, since components inside a web have disjoint carriers (V4, after (eq:marginal-web-activity)) and meet only through contour intervals.
  - *Unchanged consequences.* Enlarging X keeps the locality, the cancellation below (X ⊆ D still forces supp Γ ⊆ D and every Δ̂ used ⊆ D), and "nonzero ⇒ X ∩ Dᶜ ≠ ∅".
- *Size.* A bond lies in one box.
  - supp Γ: Γ has at most q_*|A_Γ| labels, A_Γ the D′-restricted carrier (at most q_* labels meet a bond, V4, the paragraph after (eq:marginal-selection), and every label meets A_Γ by *Connectivity*), each with at most s_* bonds and at most one marked stencil (of its source traversal), of at most C(c_st) bonds (diameter ≤ 2c_st, §3b(2)). With |A_Γ| ≤ Σ_γ|A_γ| ≤ ℓ(Γ) (ℓ of V4 (eq:marginal-extent)), the boxes meeting supp Γ number at most C_sℓ(Γ), C_s = C(s_*, q_*, c_st).
  - 𝒩(Γ): at most C_netℓ(Γ) bonds, C_net = 8(1+R_*)(1+q_*+c_h) (V4, the paragraph after (eq:marginal-hull-extent)); the web intervals cost Σ_W(H_W+1) ≤ (c_h+1)ℓ(Γ), by (eq:marginal-hull-extent) and ℓ(W) ≥ 1.
  - |B₀(Γ)| ≤ N_nb|A_Γ| ≤ 2N_nbℓ(Γ), and each Δ̂ meets at most N_nb boxes.
  - So #boxes(X(Γ,Y)) ≤ N_nb|Y| + C₀ℓ(Γ), with C₀ := C_s + C_net + 2N_nb². C₀ depends on L, G and V4's fixed constants and not on ℓ₀.
- *Bound.* For s ∈ [0,1]^Y and 0 < ρ < R − 1 the closed polydisc of radius ρ around s lies in |s_Δ| < R. By Cauchy's estimate and (5), |∂^Yφ_{D′}(Γ; s_Y, 0)| ≤ ρ^{−|Y|}Ū(Γ); letting ρ ↑ R − 1, |∂^Yφ_{D′}(Γ; s_Y, 0)| ≤ (R−1)^{−|Y|}Ū(Γ). So |f_{Γ,D′}(Y)| ≤ (R−1)^{−|Y|}Ū(Γ).
- *The decomposition of E^∂.* By §3b (Q1), (Q2), E^∂_D = Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_D}φ_D(Γ) − Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_T}φ_T(Γ) = Σ_Y[Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_D}f_{Γ,D}(Y) − Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_T}f_{Γ,T}(Y)].
  - *Paired terms.* A D-interior Γ lies in both families with the same index (§3b(2)), and X_D(Γ,Y) = X_T(Γ,Y) =: X(Γ,Y). Put t_{Γ,Y} := f_{Γ,D}(Y) − f_{Γ,T}(Y), with the set X(Γ,Y). If X(Γ,Y) ⊆ D, every walk term used and every G_{Δ₀} is the same at D and at T, since D ∩ Δ̂ = Δ̂ = T ∩ Δ̂ for the boxes involved. So t_{Γ,Y} = 0, and every nonzero paired term has X(Γ,Y) ∩ Dᶜ ≠ ∅.
  - *Unpaired terms.* For Γ not D-interior, t_{Γ,Y,D} := 1{Γ∈𝒞_D}f_{Γ,D}(Y) with the set X_D(Γ,Y), and t_{Γ,Y,T} := −1{Γ∈𝒞_T}f_{Γ,T}(Y) with the set X_T(Γ,Y). Each set contains the boxes of supp Γ, and supp Γ ⊄ D, so it meets Dᶜ.
  - Each term depends on D only through D ∩ (its set): f_{Γ,D}(Y) by *Locality*; the indicator 1{Γ∈𝒞_D} (§3b(2): the labels whose original support meets D), the set X_D(Γ,Y) and whether Γ is D-interior through D ∩ supp Γ; and the boxes of supp Γ lie in the set.
  - Each term is bounded by |f_{Γ,D}(Y)| + |f_{Γ,T}(Y)| or by one of them, and its set obeys *Size* at its own carrier, so the summation below is unchanged.
- *Anchoring and summation.* Allocate (Γ,Y) to j ∈ J_Γ with weight |J_Γ|⁻¹, as in §3b(4); the set of each term meets K_j, since supp Γ does. Let 𝒳_j^{thin} be the sets so produced from the nonzero terms, paired and unpaired, so that every member meets Dᶜ, and e^{thin}_{j,X} the allocated sums.
  - *Box graph.* Boxes Δ, Δ′ are adjacent when Δ′ meets Δ̂; this is symmetric, and each box has at most N_nb neighbours. A connected set of k boxes containing a given box has a spanning tree whose depth-first closed walk has 2(k−1) steps, each to one of at most N_nb neighbours, and the walk determines the set; so there are at most N_nb^{2(k−1)} such sets (the count of V4 Lemma c2-domain-geometry(iv) for cubes).
  - *Admissible Y.* Split Y = (Y ∩ B₀) ⊔ (Y ∖ B₀). For Δ ∈ Y ∖ B₀ on a walk Δ₀ ∈ B₀, Δ₁, …, Δ_m = Δ, let i < m be the last index with Δ_i ∈ B₀; then Δ_{i+1}, …, Δ_m is a path in Y ∖ B₀ and Δ_{i+1} is adjacent to B₀. So each component of Y ∖ B₀ contains a box u of ∂B₀ := {boxes outside B₀ adjacent to B₀}, with |∂B₀| ≤ N_nb|B₀|. Assigning to each component its first such u (in a fixed order of the boxes) is injective, so for 0 < w ≤ 1/(2N_nb²)
    Σ_{Y admissible} w^{|Y|} ≤ (1+w)^{|B₀|}·Π_{u∈∂B₀}(1 + Σ_{k≥1}N_nb^{2(k−1)}w^k) ≤ (1+w)^{|B₀|}(1+2w)^{N_nb|B₀|} ≤ e^{3wN_nb|B₀|} ≤ e^{6wN_nb²ℓ(Γ)}.
  - *Choice of κ and R.* κ := η̄/(4C₀ + 4), so 2κC₀ ≤ η̄/2. Then R ≥ 2 with w := (R−1)^{−1}e^{2κN_nb} ≤ η̄/(24N_nb²) (≤ 1/(2N_nb²), as η̄ < 1). With #boxes(X(Γ,Y)) ≤ N_nb|Y| + C₀ℓ(Γ),
    Σ_{Y admissible}(R−1)^{−|Y|}e^{2κ#boxes(X(Γ,Y))} ≤ e^{2κC₀ℓ(Γ)}Σ_{Y admissible}w^{|Y|} ≤ e^{(η̄/2 + η̄/4)ℓ(Γ)} ≤ e^{η̄ℓ(Γ)}.
  - So Σ_{X∈𝒳_j^{thin}}|e^{thin}_{j,X}|e^{2κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_D, j∈J_Γ}Ū(Γ)e^{η̄ℓ(Γ)} + Σ_{Γ∈𝒞_T, j∈J_Γ}Ū(Γ)e^{η̄ℓ(Γ)} ≤ 2Cτ², by |t_{Γ,Y}| ≤ |f_{Γ,D}(Y)| + |f_{Γ,T}(Y)| and V4's pinned-tree summation at the extent rate η̄ ((5)), once at D and once at T. C absorbs the 2 below.
  - *Decay in d_j.* Here d_j = max(0, d_T(K_j,Dᶜ) − R_0) is V4's (eq:regional-square-distances), as in §3b. A ∗-chain of n boxes of side < 2ℓ₀ spans d_T-distance at most 2nℓ₀, so a ∗-connected X meeting K_j and Dᶜ has #boxes(X) ≥ d_T(K_j,Dᶜ)/(2ℓ₀) ≥ d_j/(2ℓ₀). Spending half of the weight, Σ_{X∈𝒳_j^{thin}}|e^{thin}_{j,X}|e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ e^{−κd_j/(2ℓ₀)}·Cτ² ≤ Cτ²e^{−c_thd_j}, with c_th := κ/(4ℓ₀).
  - *Near a segment (the analogue of §3b(4)).* Let X ∈ 𝒳_{j′}^{thin} with d_T(X, K_j) ≤ R_0. X is ∗-connected and meets K_{j′} and Dᶜ, so #boxes(X) ≥ d_T(K_{j′},Dᶜ)/(2ℓ₀) ≥ d_{j′}/(2ℓ₀) and #boxes(X) ≥ (dist(K_j,K_{j′}) − R_0)/(2ℓ₀). Splitting e^{−κ#boxes(X)} into two halves, e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ e^{2κ#boxes(X)}e^{c_thR_0}e^{−c_th(d_{j′} + dist(K_j,K_{j′}))}.
    - d_j ≤ d_{j′} + dist(K_j,K_{j′}) + diam K_{j′} (trivial if d_j = 0; otherwise d_T(K_j,Dᶜ) ≤ dist(K_j,K_{j′}) + diam K_{j′} + d_T(K_{j′},Dᶜ) and d_T(K_{j′},Dᶜ) − R_0 ≤ d_{j′}), and diam K_{j′} ≤ L + 2c_st (§3b(4)). Using d_{j′} ≥ 0 in the second half, e^{−c_th(d_{j′} + dist(K_j,K_{j′}))} ≤ e^{(c_th/2)(L+2c_st)}e^{−(c_th/2)d_j}e^{−(c_th/2)dist(K_j,K_{j′})}.
    - With the bound Σ_{X∈𝒳_{j′}^{thin}}|e^{thin}_{j′,X}|e^{2κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ² above,
      Σ_{j′}Σ_{X∈𝒳_{j′}^{thin}, d_T(X,K_j)≤R_0}|e^{thin}_{j′,X}|e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ²e^{c_thR_0 + (c_th/2)(L+2c_st)}e^{−(c_th/2)d_j}Σ_{j′}e^{−(c_th/2)dist(K_j,K_{j′})}.
    - The number of j′ with dist(K_j,K_{j′}) < r is at most C(1+r)⁴ (§3b(4); periodic shells have at most the infinite-lattice shell counts, the proof of V4 Prop. regional-square-extraction), so the last sum is bounded uniformly in n and the torus. The near-segment bound holds with rate c_th/2.

**(7) Result.** E^∂_D = Σ_jΣ_{X∈𝒳_j^{thin}}e^{thin}_{j,X}, where each X is a ∗-connected union of boxes of side in [ℓ₀, 2ℓ₀) (or full circles in a direction of period < ℓ₀), meets K_j and Dᶜ, and e^{thin}_{j,X} depends on D only through D ∩ X, with
- Σ_{X∈𝒳_j^{thin}}|e^{thin}_{j,X}|e^{2κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ² ((6), *Anchoring and summation*);
- Σ_{X∈𝒳_j^{thin}}|e^{thin}_{j,X}|e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ²e^{−c_thd_j}, c_th := κ/(4ℓ₀).
- *Range of D.* D is any carrier D ⊆ D₊, covered or not, with D₊ the free bonds with at least one endpoint in Λ₁ (V4, the paragraph after (eq:regional-torus-bound)). So D₊ = I = Λ₁^*, "the free bonds with an endpoint in Λ₁" (V4 Section c5-0), and the range contains I and every retreated D(Λ) ⊆ I. (5)–(6) do not use the collar. (Q1), (Q2) and the rooted majorant hold at covered carriers by §3b, and at uncovered ones by §3d(2)(g), (h). The bounds of (6) hold for every j, including the segments meeting Dᶜ, where d_j = 0; "Same index" of §3b(1) needs no coverage.
- *Rooted form.* Put e_X := Σ_je^{thin}_{j,X}. Let Δ be a box and X ∈ 𝒳_j^{thin} with Δ ⊆ X. X is ∗-connected and meets K_j, so #boxes(X) ≥ dist(Δ,K_j)/(2ℓ₀), by the span bound of (6) (*Decay in d_j*). Spending half of the weight of the first bound,
  Σ_{X⊇Δ}|e_X|e^{κ#boxes(X)} ≤ Cτ²Σ_je^{−κ·dist(Δ,K_j)/(2ℓ₀)} ≤ C_rτ².
  - The last sum: fix a bond b_Δ with initial site in Δ. If dist(Δ,K_j) < r, some bond of segment j lies within r + 2ℓ₀ + c_st of b_Δ (diam Δ < 2ℓ₀; every point of K_j is within c_st of a bond of segment j, §3b(4)). Distinct segments contain distinct contour bonds, and periodic shells are at most the infinite-lattice shells (the proof of V4 Prop. regional-square-extraction), so the number of such j is at most C(1 + r + 2ℓ₀ + c_st)⁴. Hence Σ_je^{−κ·dist(Δ,K_j)/(2ℓ₀)} ≤ Σ_{r≥0}C(2 + r + 2ℓ₀ + c_st)⁴e^{−κr/(2ℓ₀)}, and C_r depends on κ, ℓ₀, L, c_st only, uniformly in n and the torus.
  - Per step-2 cube: an interval of length a₂ meets at most a₂/ℓ₀ + 2 box intervals (sides ≥ ℓ₀, or one full circle), so at most (a₂/ℓ₀ + 2)⁴ boxes meet a cube Q of side a₂ (the boxes are products of intervals, (4)). With κ_{a₂}#(cubes of X) ≤ κ#boxes(X) (*Against (0)*), Σ_{X∩Q≠∅}|e_X|e^{κ_{a₂}#(cubes of X)} ≤ (a₂/ℓ₀ + 2)⁴C_rτ².
- κ, ℓ₀, C, C_r, c_th depend on L, G and V4's fixed constants only, and are fixed before the coupling. The bounds are uniform in n and the torus.
- *Against (0):* a box of side < 2ℓ₀ meets at most (2ℓ₀/a₂ + 2)⁴ cubes of side a₂, so for Bałaban's step-2 cubes, of side a₂, #(cubes of X) ≤ (2ℓ₀/a₂ + 2)⁴#boxes(X) and e^{−κ#boxes(X)} ≤ e^{−κ_{a₂}#(cubes of X)}, κ_{a₂} := κ(2ℓ₀/a₂ + 2)^{−4}. This needs no order between M and ℓ₀, and κ_{a₂} ≥ κ(2ℓ₀ + 2)^{−4} for a₂ ≥ 1, fixed before the coupling even if a₂ depends on g. With the *Rooted form*, the defect polymers have the per-cube rooted bound of (0).
- *Conditions*, fixed before or with the coupling: R ≥ 2 with (R−1)^{−1}e^{2κN_nb} ≤ η̄/(24N_nb²) ((6)); then ℓ₀ ≥ max(R_H + 1, c_supp + 2) and ℓ₀ ≥ ℓ₀(R, L, G) ((4)); g₀ chosen last with K̄₂^{s_*}τ ≤ 1/2, C̄′_*τ ≤ 1/4, C̄′_*τ² ≤ ᾱ/4 ((5)), V4's own thresholds in force separately.
- *Near a segment.* The near-segment bound of §3b(4) holds for the thin form with rate c_th/2 ((6)).
- *Check.* Summing over j gives |E^∂_D| ≤ Cτ²Σ_je^{−c_thd_j}, the d_j-part of V4 (eq:regional-square-error) with a smaller rate.

## 3j. D2, Second Item: Carrier Dependence of the t > 0 Species

**(0) What Is Needed.** By §3g(2), on the term labelled Λ every carrier-dependent factor must depend on Λ only near its own support; then the label sum is CRE's polymer sum. The reference species are exactly local (§3g(1)). The t > 0 species (Υ¹_W, the terms of 𝓑^Λ) and the leading word f_ξ(m) = W_χ(C;U_ξ(m)) (§2) read the carrier through the mean shift m = −H⁻¹ℓ_Λ, with H := H_{D(Λ)D(Λ)} and ℓ_Λ := H_{D(Λ)J(Λ)}A^{Λ,Ψ} (V4 Section c5-4), and m depends on all of D(Λ).
- Every other carrier dependence of the t-family is local: C_{E₀}, Γ_{E₀}(σ), hosts and probe hosts (§3g(1)(a) at t = 0; Lemma c5-4-0(d)), and at t > 0 the near primitives, which are evaluated at a = B + tm̂ and read A^{Λ,Ψ} only on their own inputs. The reference species are exactly local per group or cluster (a^D_W, w°_D(W), Φ°_D(Γ)); q°_Λ and S°_Λ are cluster sums, local in the cluster form of §3g(1)(c).
- The contour-wide factors (f_ξ(m̂) and the words U_j) depend on Λ only through D(Λ) and A^{Λ,Ψ}|_{J₁(Λ)} within R_N of C ((2)). They enter the label sum through the subset expansion of D26 6.5.
- *Weakest form.* Exact locality within a finite range R_t(g₀) that is small against the polymer weights of the label sum ((6)). §3g(2)'s factorisation is an identity that uses exact locality; a decaying dependence would need an extra decoupling expansion. The cutoffs χ^t_b are indicators in B + tm, which rules out Cauchy-estimate localisation in V4's translated representation; (1)–(4) give exact locality with an exact tilt instead.
- *Method.* Translate by a local real shift m̂ instead of m, and put the remaining linear term into the host potentials as a tilt. §3e(3.12) extends the device to complex backgrounds.

**(1) The Local Shift.**
- *Parametrix.* §3i(4) uses only a two-sided bound and a finite range on every principal restriction. H has γ ≤ H ≤ h_+ with γ = γ_L/2, h_+ = m_L, and entry range ρ_L, on every principal restriction ((I1)–(I3) of V4 Section c5-0; Lemma c5-4-0(a)). So §3i(4) applies at D′ = D(Λ), with (γ, h_+, ρ_L, μ_H) in place of (γ_L, h_L, R_H, μ_T), at s = 1 (R = 1). Fix once a partition of the torus sites into boxes with sides in [ℓ_s, 2ℓ_s), ℓ_s ≥ ρ_L + 1, independent of Λ. It gives the parametrix P (§3i(4)'s 𝒢) and K = Σ_ΔK_Δ with HP = 1 − K.
- *Row sums.* Each K_Δ has row sums ≤ ε_s := m_Lσ_H(ℓ_s − ρ_L), with σ_H(T) := γ⁻¹Σ_{t≥T}32(2t+1)³e^{−μ_Ht}: (I2) bounds the row sums of H by m_L, and the proof of Lemma c5-1-1, which uses only (I3) for the block and (I1), bounds the entries of G_Δ. A bond lies in Δ̂ for at most N_nb boxes, so ‖K‖_{∞→∞} ≤ q_s := N_nbε_s. Fix ℓ_s = ℓ_s(L, G) with q_s ≤ e^{−μ_Hℓ_s/2}.
- *Truncation.* For N ≥ 0 put m̃ := −PΣ_{n=0}^{N}Kⁿℓ_Λ. Then Hm̃ + ℓ_Λ = K^{N+1}ℓ_Λ =: δ_N. Since P = H⁻¹(1 − K), m̃ = −H⁻¹(1 − K^{N+1})ℓ_Λ = m + H⁻¹δ_N.
- *Rescaling.* m̂ := λm̃, λ := (r/4)/(r/4 + ε_N/2), with ε_N of (3). λ depends on N, r and fixed constants only, not on Λ, and m̂ is analytic in the background wherever m̃ is, as the carried slots of D26 6.4 need.

**(2) Locality.** The (b,c) entry of PKⁿ vanishes unless d(b,c) ≤ 5ℓ_s(n+1) (§3i(4) *Range of a walk*), and a walk term depends on D(Λ) only through D(Λ) ∩ (Δ̂₀ ∪ … ∪ Δ̂_n) (§3i(4) *Locality*). ℓ_Λ(c) depends on D(Λ) and on A^{Λ,Ψ} within ρ_L of c, and on A^{Λ,Ψ}|_{J₁(Λ)} only (Lemma c5-1-2). Put R_N := 5ℓ_s(N+2) + 2ρ_L: 5ℓ_s(n+1) ≤ 5ℓ_s(N+1) for a walk entry of PKⁿ, n ≤ N; ℓ_s + ρ_L for the box enlargement Δ̂; ρ_L for ℓ_Λ; in total ≤ 5ℓ_s(N+2) + 2ρ_L. Then m̃_b, m̂_b and δ_N(b) depend on Λ only through D(Λ) and A^{Λ,Ψ}|_{J₁(Λ)} within distance R_N of b. They vanish unless d(b, J₁(Λ)) ≤ R_N, since ℓ_Λ is supported within ρ_L of J₁(Λ).

**(3) Sizes.** sup_c h(ℓ_Λ(c)) ≤ c_ℓϑr with c_ℓ := 4(2ρ_L+1)⁴m_L, from the entry and count bounds in the proof of Lemma c5-5-1. Hence:
- sup_b h(δ_N(b)) ≤ q_s^{N+1}c_ℓϑr;
- sup_b h(m̃_b − m_b) = sup_b h((H⁻¹δ_N)(b)) ≤ γ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H)q_s^{N+1}c_ℓϑr (Lemma c5-1-1; the lattice count of Lemma c5-1-2);
- put ε_N := 2c_hγ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H)c_ℓϑr·q_s^{N+1}, with c_h of Remark c5-1-3. Since h ≤ |·|_σ ≤ c_hh there and |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4 ((c5-4-1)), |m̃_b − m_b|_σ ≤ ε_N/2. So |m̂_b|_σ ≤ λ(r/4 + ε_N/2) = r/4 and |m̂_b − m_b|_σ ≤ (1 − λ)|m̃_b|_σ + ε_N/2 ≤ ε_N for every b;
- since m̂_b = 0 when d(b,J₁(Λ)) > R_N: h(m̂_b) ≤ h(m_b) + ε_N𝟏{d(b,J₁(Λ)) ≤ R_N};
- δ̂ := Hm̂ + ℓ_Λ = λδ_N + (1 − λ)ℓ_Λ. With 1 − λ ≤ 2ε_N/r, sup_b h(δ̂(b)) ≤ q_s^{N+1}c_ℓϑr + 2c_ℓϑε_N = C_shϑr·q_s^{N+1}, C_sh := c_ℓ(1 + 4c_hc_ℓϑγ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H)) = c_ℓ(1 + 4(2ρ_L+1)⁴), a constant of L and the group, since ϑ = 1/(4c_hC_H^near) and C_H^near = γ⁻¹m_LN_lat(μ_H) (Remark c5-1-3; Lemma c5-1-2). δ̂ vanishes unless d(b,J₁(Λ)) ≤ R_N + ρ_L.

**(4) The t-Family at m̂.**
- *Identity.* In the density K^Λ_{ξ;t}(a) = 𝟏_{𝒟^Λ}(a)exp[−½⟨a,Ha⟩ − t⟨a,ℓ_Λ⟩ + P^Λ_t(a)] of V4 Section c5-4 put a = B + tm̂. Since H is symmetric and δ̂ = Hm̂ + ℓ_Λ,
  −½⟨a,Ha⟩ − t⟨a,ℓ_Λ⟩ = −½⟨B,HB⟩ − t⟨B,δ̂⟩ − t²(½⟨m̂,Hm̂⟩ + ⟨m̂,ℓ_Λ⟩).
  Like V4's constant e^{½t²⟨m,Hm⟩} in (c5-4-2), the constant is the same in the numerator and the denominator of every ratio (c5-4-3), so it cancels and is never estimated. The values of all normalised quantities are unchanged. Their expansion changes, and the species become new functions: Υ¹_W, the terms of 𝓑^Λ, and the leading word f_ξ(m̂) in place of f_ξ(m). From here on §2's bracket and D1 are read at m̂. m̂ is real and does not depend on t.
- *Cutoffs and pointwise uses.* χ̂^t_b(B) := 𝟏{|B_b + tm̂_b|_σ < r}, Θ̂^t := Π_{b∈D(Λ)}χ̂^t_b, Θ̂^t_{E(Y)} := Π_{b∈E(Y)}χ̂^t_b and 𝐖̂_t(Y,B) := 𝐕^Λ_{ξ,t}(Y, B + tm̂). (c5-4-1) holds for m̂ by (3).
  - V4's pointwise uses of m therefore hold verbatim with m̂: the translated boxes, the reserve (1 − t|m_b|_σ/r)²a_bad of Lemma c5-4-2, the shells S_b with ϖ_b ≤ C_Vr^{d_G−1}|m_b|_σ (Lemma c5-5-5, stated for every real tilt v), the segments sm in the box of radius r/4 (Lemma c5-5-4; proof of Lemma c5-5-7(c)), and the bond marks. They use only |m_b|_σ ≤ r/4 and the value at b.
- *The tilt.* As in §3e(3.12), put W̃_t(Y,B) := 𝐖̂_t(Y,B) − tΘ̂^t_{E(Y)}(B)L_Y(δ̂,B), with V4's probe term L_Y(λ,B) := Σ_{b:Y_b=Y}⟨λ_b,B_b⟩ (Section c5-5, before Lemma c5-5-2) at λ = δ̂. Each b ∈ D(Λ) has exactly one probe host, Y_b ∈ 𝐃₀(Λ), and b ∈ E(Y_b) (Lemma c5-4-0(d)); δ̂ is supported in D(Λ). So Σ_YW̃_t = Σ_Y𝐖̂_t − t⟨B,δ̂⟩ on Θ̂^t's support, and the identity is exact.
  - *Polymer inputs.* By V4's argument for 𝐖_t + L_Y(λ,·), the family W̃_t has the properties (i)–(iii) of the proof of Lemma c5-4-1: the tilt part depends on B|_{E(Y)} and is bounded on the translated box.
  - *Size.* On the translated box |B_b|_σ < r + |m̂_b|_σ ≤ 5r/4, and |⟨δ̂(b),B_b⟩| ≤ h(δ̂(b))h(B_b) ≤ h(δ̂(b))|B_b|_σ (Remark c5-1-3). A bond with Y_b = Y lies in the interior of Y, a rectangle of at most four M-cubes per side ((c-probe-host)), so at most 4(4M+1)⁴ ≤ 4(4M+2)⁴ bonds have Y_b = Y. With (3), the tilt part is at most τ̂_N := 5(4M+2)⁴C_shϑr²·q_s^{N+1}.
  - *Host bound.* Every host of 𝐃₀(Λ) is a rectangle of at most four M-cubes per side ((part1-potential-host), (c-probe-host); V4 Section c5-5, after (c5-5-T)), so d₀(Y) ≤ N₀(Y) − 1 < s_p = 5⁴ (Lemma c2-domain-geometry(i)). V4's d₀(Y) ≤ B_h + 4 (Lemma c2-potential-size) covers only the hosts that carry near primitives, and a probe host may carry the tilt alone.
  - Hence |W̃_t(Y,B)| ≤ ω̂_te^{−κd₀(Y)} with ω̂_t := ω_t + e^{κs_p}τ̂_N. ω̂_t ≤ ω_*/2 is a g₀-threshold once N = N(g₀) of (6) is fixed.
- *Tilt marks.* Without exterior input, the t-derivative of the tilt part is −L_Y(δ̂,B) on the cutoffs' support. In the host mark of Lemma c5-5-6 it adds 𝔪^{δ̂}_Y := (1 + ω̄̂)e^{ω̄̂}e^{κs_p}e^{1/8}ω_*⁻¹(5r/4)Σ_{b:Y_b=Y}h(δ̂(b)) ≤ Ĉ^δ_bd rΣ_{b:Y_b=Y}h(δ̂(b)), Ĉ^δ_bd := (5/2)e^{1+κs_p+1/8}/ω_*, by ω̄̂ ≤ 1 ((c) below). It is nonzero only for hosts within R_N + ρ_L of J₁(Λ).
- *The source-insertion expansion with W̃_t.* V4 uses the host potentials in Sections c5-5 and c5-5R and in Lemma c4-7-1 in five ways; Lemmas c5-5-7z, c5-5-7, c5-5-8, Prop. c5-5-9, Cor. c5-5-10 and Remark c5-5-11 use them only through the mark constants C_bd, C′_bd and the tilt-mark constant, treated in (c). Each holds with W̃_t:
  - (a) The host bound |𝐖_t| ≤ ω_te^{−κd₀(Y)} ≤ A_Y := (ω_*/2)e^{−κd₀(Y)} on the host's box. It is used in Lemma c5-4-2 (with (c5-4-T)), Lemma c5-5-2(b) (the Mayer factor ω_*e^{1/2}e^{−κd₀(Y)}), and Lemma c5-5-2(c), which holds "when 𝐖_t is replaced by any potentials 𝐖′ with |𝐖′(Y,B)| ≤ A_Y = (ω_*/2)e^{−κd₀(Y)} on the support of the host's cutoffs". It is also used in Lemma c4-7-1(a), which needs only |e^{𝐖} − 1| ≤ e^{A_Y} − 1 = v_ordinary, in the undifferenced factors of Lemma c5-5-6, and in Lemma c5-5R-2(c),(d).
    - With ω̂_t ≤ ω_*/2 these hold verbatim. At a probe host that carries no near primitive, the ordinary Mayer factor, zero in V4, becomes the tilt's; it obeys the same bound, and the probe labels still carry no factor.
    - In Lemma c5-5R-2(c), R_Υ := ω_*/(2ω̂_t) ≥ 1, and the class-(β) factor of the proof of Lemma c5-5R-3 becomes 2ω̂_t/ω_*.
  - (b) Locality on E(Y) and boundedness on the translated box (properties (i)–(iii) of the proof of Lemma c5-4-1; Lemma c5-5-2(a),(d)): *Polymer inputs* above.
  - (c) The host form 𝐖_t = Θ^t_{E(Y)}𝐏_t of Lemma c5-5-6. With the tilt, W̃_t = Θ̂^t_{E(Y)}P̃_t with P̃_t := 𝐏̂_t − tL_Y(δ̂,·) and 𝐏̂_t(Y,B) := Σ_{𝔭 near, Y_𝔭=Y}𝔭(B + tm̂ + tA^{Λ,Ψ}). So |P̃_t| ≤ ω̄ + τ̂_N ≤ ω̄̂ := ω̄ + e^{κs_p}τ̂_N, and the smooth part of a host mark is ≤ 2ω̄̂e^{ω̄̂}. Without exterior input, ∂_tP̃_t = Σ_𝔭D𝔭(B + tm̂)[m̂|_{E(Y)}] − L_Y(δ̂,B), and the second term gives the tilt mark above.
    - ω̄̂ ≤ 1 is required; since ω̄̂ − ω̄ → 0 with g₀ ((6)), it is a g₀-threshold.
    - The second branch of (c5-5-T)(iii) enters only the cutoff part, whose host factor is ∫₀¹𝐏₀e^{t_Y𝐏₀}dt_Y at t = 0, where P̃₀ = 𝐏₀. It is used only with ω̄ and is unchanged.
    - C′_bd needs only (1 + ω̄̂)e^{ω̄̂} ≤ 2e, which holds with ω̄̂ ≤ 1, and its Σh(m_b) becomes Σh(m̂_b) ((5)).
    - V4's C_bd := 2e·e^{κ(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}C₀/ω_* bounds the marks of the hosts with an exterior input through C₀g₀(1+3r)³ = ω̄. Such a host carries a near primitive, so d₀(Y) ≤ B_h + 4. Since ω̄̂ ≤ ω̄ + C_τg₀²r² ≤ (C₀ + C_τ)g₀(1+3r)³ for g₀ ≤ 1 ((6)), C_bd is raised to 2e·e^{κ(B_h+4)}e^{1/8}(C₀ + C_τ)/ω_*. Complex backgrounds: §3e(3.12).
  - (d) The cutoffs and the bond marks at m̂: *Cutoffs and pointwise uses* above.
  - (e) The evenness of Lemma c5-5R-2(a) at t = 0 with 𝒟 = ∅: no Mayer factor is selected, so the tilt does not appear. At t = 1 a class-(γ) root also has 𝒟 = ∅, and its measure is the t = 0 measure plus marked cutoff differences, as in Lemma c5-5R-2(b).
  - In Lemma c5-5R-2(c), ζ multiplies the whole W̃_t, tilt included ("every near primitive assigned to a host of 𝒟 multiplied by ζ", with the tilt part assigned to the probe host Y_b); so R_Υ := ω_*/(2ω̂_t) is the right radius.
  - So V4 Sections c5-5 and c5-5R and Lemma c4-7-1 hold with W̃_t, ω̂_t and ω̄̂, with C₀ replaced by C₀ + C_τ in C_bd (hence in C₂ of Prop. c5-5-9, the term 64C_bd, and in Cor. c5-5-10, Prop. c5-6-4 and Cor. c5-7-4(a)) and in the constants of Section c5-5R, under the choice of N in (6). The quantitative uses of h(m̂_b) and the tilt marks enter through (5).

**(5) The Decay Form of the Shift at m̂.** V4 uses the decay of m through the pointwise bound (c5-5-1) and the sum form (c5-5-2) with its I_C clause (Lemma c5-5-1). Put μ_x := μ_H/20.
- *A finite-range bump costs a fixed rate.* For R ≥ 0 and every bond b, 𝟏{d(b,J₁(Λ)) ≤ R} ≤ Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}𝟏{d(b,e) ≤ R} ≤ e^{μ_xR}Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}. And q_s^{N+1}e^{μ_xR_N} ≤ e^{−μ_Hℓ_s(N+1)/2 + μ_Hℓ_s(N+2)/4 + μ_Hρ_L/10} = e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/4 + μ_Hρ_L/10}, by q_s ≤ e^{−μ_Hℓ_s/2} and R_N = 5ℓ_s(N+2) + 2ρ_L.
- *Pointwise form (c5-5-1) at m̂.* By (3), h(m̂_b) ≤ h(m_b) + ε_N𝟏{d(b,J₁(Λ)) ≤ R_N}. With the bump bound and e^{−μ_Hd} ≤ e^{−μ_xd},
  h(m̂_b) ≤ [C_m + 2c_hγ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H)c_ℓe^{μ_Hρ_L/10}e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/4}]ϑrΣ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)} ≤ 2C_mϑrΣ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}
  for N ≥ N₀, the least N with 2c_hγ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H)c_ℓe^{μ_Hρ_L/10}e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/4} ≤ C_m, a constant of L and the group. This is (c5-5-1) with (2C_m, μ_x) in place of (C_m, μ_H), with no counting factor and no condition on the rate of any consumer.
- *Sum form (c5-5-2) at m̂.* V4's proof of (c5-5-2) from (c5-5-1) (the splitting e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}e^{−μdist(b,X)} ≤ e^{−μ_xd(b,e)/2}e^{−min(μ_x/2,μ)dist(e,X)} and the lattice count) gives, for every μ ≥ 0 and every set X,
  Σ_{b∈D(Λ)}h(m̂_b)e^{−μdist(b,X)} ≤ 2C_mϑrN_lat(μ_x/2)Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−min(μ_x/2,μ)dist(e,X)},
  and its I_C clause becomes Σ_{b∈I_C}h(m̂_b) ≤ 2C_mϑrN_lat(μ_x/2)Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xdist(e,I_C)/2}.
- *The real tilt δ̂.* By (3), h(δ̂(b)) ≤ C_shϑr·q_s^{N+1}𝟏{d(b,J₁(Λ)) ≤ R_N + ρ_L} ≤ C_she^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/4}ϑrΣ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xd(b,e)}. With the choice of N in (6) (e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/4} ≤ g₀) this is ≤ C_she^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}ϑrg₀Σ_ee^{−μ_xd(b,e)}: (c5-5-1)'s form at rate μ_x, small in the coupling.
- *Consumers.*
  - Lemma c5-5-4 and the source-shift marks of Prop. c5-5-9(ii) use the I_C clause: they hold with 𝔖̂₁(Λ) := Σ_{e∈J₁(Λ)}e^{−μ_xdist(e,I_C)/2} and N_lat(μ_x/2), 2C_m in C₁.
  - Lemma c5-5-5's consequence and the bond marks hold at rate μ_x with 2C_m in C_ϖ = C_Vc_hC_mϑ. Prop. c5-5-9's splitting then gives the bond and host marks at the rate min(μ_x/2, ν/(C_FLM)), for every ν. So V4's μ′ := min(μ_H/4, ν/(2C_FLM)) becomes μ̂′ := min(μ_x/4, ν/(2C_FLM)).
  - The tilt marks 𝔪^{δ̂}_Y ≤ Ĉ^δ_bd rΣ_{b:Y_b=Y}h(δ̂(b)) ((4)) have the form of V4's host marks without exterior input, with the prefactor Ĉ^δ_bdC_she^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}ϑg₀r² in place of C′_bdg₀(1+3r)²·2C_mϑr (both of order g₀ times a power of r). By the bump bound at the fixed rate μ_x they enter C₂ε̄_tr^{d_G}𝔖̂₂(Λ) with an added constant.
  - Prop. c5-5-9 at m̂: (c5-5-9) with 𝔖̂₁, 𝔖̂₂(Λ) := Σ_{e∈J(Λ)}e^{−μ̂′dist(e,C)}, and C₁, C₂ with 2C_m, N_lat(μ_x/2), the tilt-mark constants and the raised C_bd of (4)(c); C₂ is doubled for the label share of (6). Its constants still depend on L, the group, M, κ, ω_*, ν, ϑ, the character and the stencil ranges only.
  - Cor. c5-5-10: 𝔖̂₁ ≤ e^{μ_x(2L+1)/2}𝔖̂₂, since μ_x/2 ≥ μ̂′. The far-event bound is n_CN_lat(μ̂′/2)e^{−μ̂′(b−1)/2}, and V4's comparison r^{d_G}e^{−μ̂′(b−1)/2} ≤ e^{−μ̂′(b−1)/4} once b − 1 ≥ (4d_G/μ̂′)log r is a g₀-threshold, as b − 1 grows as a power of log g₀⁻². 𝔇_Λ ≤ |I∖D(Λ)| does not involve the rate.
  - Remark c5-5-11's fixed-loop restriction on n_C keeps its form, with μ̂′ for μ′. Prop. c5-5R-4 takes the constants of Prop. c5-5-9, with C_ph := (C′_χ/u_*)(C′_χ/u_* + C′_χ/2 + 2C₀′/ω_* + 1) read with C₀′ := C₀ + C_τ (*Constants fixed before the coupling* below).
  - Prop. c5-6-4 and Section c5-7 use μ′ only through N_lat(μ′) and the factor e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2} of Cor. c5-5-10. With μ̂′, N_lat(μ̂′) is a larger constant, and e^{μ_x(2L+1)/2} ≤ e^{μ_H(2L+1)/2}.
  - V4 Section c5-3-4 needs bounds Ce^{−cd(b,Γ_C)} with C, c independent of the labels, the fields and r. c = μ̂′ (and μ_x/2) qualifies.
- *η.* The consumers hold for every ν, in particular for ν = η, so no relation between η and μ_x is needed. V4 fixes η only by the upper bound 1000ηA_s < ϖ/8 (Section c4-8-2).
- *Constants fixed before the coupling.* V4's order (Section app:thresholds), with C₀′ := C₀ + C_τ placed at C₀'s place (after N_h and C₀ of (part1-host-multiplicity)), in the role of C₀ wherever the host bound enters: (c5-4-T), (c5-5-T)(ii)–(iii), Section c5-5R, and C_bd ((4)(c)).
  - This is not circular. C_τ = 5(4M+2)⁴C_shϑe^{κs_p} is built from M, κ, s_p = 5⁴, ϑ = 1/(4c_hC_H^near) of Remark c5-1-3 and C_sh; ϑ and C_sh depend on L and the group only ((3)); none of them depends on C₀.
  - Every constant after C₀ in V4's order (C′_χ, K_χ, u_*, η, C_pin, ρ_pin, N_host, C_M, "each depending on L, M, κ, ω_*, the group, the character and the constants earlier in this order only") is read from C₀′, and so is every constant that uses C₀'s value; all are still fixed before the coupling.
  - Added constants: ℓ_s, N₀, C_sh, C_τ, Ĉ^δ_bd. Derived quantities that change: μ′, C_ϖ, C₁, C₂, 𝔖₁, 𝔖₂, ω_t, ω̄, C_bd, C_ph, and V4's g₀-threshold, which now also depends on the added constants.

**(6) Choice of N, Range and Count Check.**
- *Choice.* Take N = N(g₀) the least N ≥ N₀ with e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/4} ≤ g₀. Then q_s^{N+1} ≤ e^{−μ_Hℓ_sN/2} ≤ g₀², so τ̂_N ≤ 5(4M+2)⁴C_shϑ·r²g₀² and ε_N ≤ 2c_hγ⁻¹N_lat(μ_H)c_ℓϑ·rg₀². Since r²g₀² ≤ g₀(1+2r)³ for g₀ ≤ 1, ω̂_t ≤ (C₀ + C_τ)g₀(1+2r)³ with C_τ := 5(4M+2)⁴C_shϑe^{κs_p}, a constant fixed before the coupling ((5)). So ω̂_t has ω_t's form with C₀ + C_τ in place of C₀; likewise ω̄̂ ≤ ω̄ + C_τg₀²r². The tilt bound of (5) is ≤ C_she^{3μ_Hρ_L/20}ϑrg₀Σ_ee^{−μ_xd(b,e)}.
- *Thresholds.* (c5-4-T) and (c5-5-T)(ii) hold with ω̂_t, (c5-5-T)(iii) and Lemma c5-5-6 with ω̄̂ ((4)), and (c5-5R-T)(ii) with ω̂_t: all for small g₀, since ω̂_t and ω̄̂ have the forms of ω_t and ω̄ up to constants. Class (β), and so ρ_ph ≤ C_phg₀²(1+2r)⁴ and the explicit second-order rate of Prop. c5-5R-4, hold with C_ph read with C₀′ ((5)). Complex backgrounds: §3e(3.12), with the real shift m̂₀ := m̂(U₁).
- *Range.* N(g₀) ≤ N₀ + 1 + 2log g₀⁻²/(μ_Hℓ_s), so R_t := R_{N(g₀)} ≤ (10/μ_H)log g₀⁻² + 5ℓ_s(N₀+3) + 2ρ_L. The tilt δ̂ reaches R_t + ρ_L.
- *Count check, per cube.* On the term labelled Λ, the t > 0 factors of a group W depend on Λ only within R_t + ρ_L of its envelope 𝔈(W) (§3g(1)(d′)). The envelope has at most A_Eσ(W) cubes of side LM, and its enlargement by R_t + ρ_L has at most n_t := (2(R_t+ρ_L)/(LM) + 3)⁴A_Eσ(W) cubes. The label polymers are the components of Λᶜ (§3g(2)). Let a_lab bound the summed weight of the label polymers that contain a given cube; then the label configurations meeting the enlarged envelope cost at most exp(a_lab·n_t).
  - *The share.* V4 spends the reserve e^{2νσ(W)} (ν = η) of Lemma c5-5-8 on the decay, e^{2νσ(W)} ≥ e^{νσ(W)}e^{νdist(Δ,C)/(C_FLM)}, and on σ(W) ≤ ν⁻¹e^{νσ(W)} in the differenced slot. Pay the latter instead with σ(W) ≤ (2/ν)e^{νσ(W)/2} (from x ≤ eˣ); C₂ doubles, and e^{νσ(W)/2} is free. The rates of D1(ii) (δ*_n and §3a(6)'s (31/8)η) do not change. This share absorbs the label cost if a_lab(2(R_t+ρ_L)/(LM)+3)⁴A_E ≤ η/2.
  - *The label weight.* Each component carries LFR II's factor e^{−κ₁(Z)−2p₀(g₁)} (p.384, the factors exp(−κ_j(Z) − 2p₀(g_{j(Z)}))), and the bad cubes carry part (ii) of §3f(2) R1, which joins (1.79)'s form. With the modified (1.76) of §3c(4‴), p.385's constant ¼γ₀(14)^{−d} becomes ¼c₄d^{−1}(434)^{−d}ϑ² (§3f(2) Minor 4, *p.385's step*).
  - So κ₁(Z) ≥ ¼c₄d^{−1}(434)^{−d}ϑ²A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀}(d₁′(Z)+1) − O(1)M^dR₁^{d+1}(d₁′(Z)+1) (p.385, the display for κ₁, modified), under the modified condition ¼c₄d^{−1}(434)^{−d}ϑ²A₁²(log g₁⁻²)^{2p₀} ≥ O(1)2(64)^dM^dL^{d+1}R₁^{d+2}, which holds for small g₁. LFR's p₀(g) is (log g⁻²)^{p₀} (§3f(2) R1 *Notation*). Hence a_lab ≤ Ce^{−2(log g₁⁻²)^{p₀}}; the tree count through a cube is paid by κ₁.
  - *Smallness.* R_t ≍ log g₀⁻², and g₁ ≤ (1+β₀)g₀ (CRE (2.6), p.255), so ½log g₀⁻² ≤ log g₁⁻² for small g₀ (as at V4 (eq:c5-3-1)). Hence (log g₁⁻²)^{p₀} ≥ 2^{−p₀}(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}, and a_lab·R_t⁴ ≤ C(log g₀⁻²)⁴e^{−2^{1−p₀}(log g₀⁻²)^{p₀}} → 0, a g₀-threshold. D26 6.5 consumes this per-cube weight in the label sum, and D26 7.4(c) uses a_lab·n_t.

**(7) Result.** The t > 0 species, the words and f_ξ(m̂) depend on Λ only within R_t + ρ_L of their footprints ((2), (4), (6)). V4's uses of the shift in Sections c5-4 to c5-7 are of three kinds, and each holds at m̂: the pointwise uses (|m_b|_σ ≤ r/4, values at b and the segments sm) verbatim ((4)); the expansion at the young variable B + tm, with its constant, by the identity of (4); and the sums of h(m_b), over E(Y), I_C and I_{𝓗(W)} or weighted as in (c5-5-2), by (5). The new conditions are N = N(g₀) ≥ N₀, ℓ_s = ℓ_s(L, G) and the g₀-thresholds of (4)–(6); the rates μ_H/2 and μ′ of Prop. c5-5-9 and its consumers become μ_x/2 and μ̂′.

## 4. The Class 𝔇₁ and the Target D1

**Definition.** A *dressed loop of step 1* for the square C_n on a labelled term (final region Λ, carrier D(Λ)) is a function of V and of the outer variables that the term holds constant, of the form

𝒲 = κ_C e^{E} [ f + Σ_{𝔅≠∅ compatible} Q(𝔅)·dim(π)⁻¹ tr(Y_{W₁}U₁ ⋯ Y_{Wₙ}U_n) ].

The outer variables are V₀ on Ω₁ᶜ, the exterior variable on J(Λ), and the de-younged bonds I∖D(Λ), which are constants of the term (V4 Lemma c5-4-4); on uncovered terms they also enter the contour links (§3d(1)). The ingredients:
- **groups** W of V4 Lemma c5-5-3(a), of size σ(W), and **families** 𝔅 = {W₁, …, Wₙ}, in the cyclic order of their intervals, compatible in V4's sense: "all their constituent domains (roots and cluster members) are mutually compatible and their intervals are disjoint" (V4 Section joint-extraction);
- **insertions** Y_W ∈ End(V_π), one per group;
- a **leading word** f, a unitary word of the background (f_ξ(m̂) on covered terms, §3j(4) *Identity*); on uncovered terms it also contains outer variables (§3d(1));
- **words** U_j, unitary words of the background and the outer variables;
- **interactions** Q(𝔅) = exp(−Σ_{Γ hits 𝔅} φ(Γ)), over the clusters Γ of the reference group gas, with "hits" as in V4 Lemma c5-5-3(d);
- a **scalar exponent** E = Σ_X e_X + E_const, a sum of local terms e_X and a constant E_const of the contour and the torus.

The norms are as follows, for some a > 0, every traversal n and every coarse segment j:
- |Q(𝔅)| ≤ ∏_{W∈𝔅} e^{θ°(W)} with θ°(W) ≤ νσ(W)/16 (§2(d));
- Σ_{W∋n} e^{θ°(W)}‖Y_W‖ ≤ ν_n, with ν_n bounded in D1(ii);
- Σ_{X within R_0 of K_j} |e_X|e^{a·diam X} ≤ Cτ²e^{−c′d_j} (§3b(4); the map's D(1) uses the thin form of §3i(7), map #MD2.0.3.51), and |E_const| ≤ Cτ²(4ne^{−cι_T} + (n+1)e^{−cn}).

**D1.** For the squares C_n under (c5-7-T), every n, and every labelled term, covered or not (§3d), F^Λ(1) is a dressed loop of step 1 such that:
- **(i)** κ_C = e^{−δm^σP(C_n)}(z_c^σ)⁴, V4's exact first-step coefficients (eq:extracted-loop-scalar; §2(g));
- **(ii)** for every traversal n,

  ν_n ≤ C_χρ δ*_n(U₁) + Cρ e^{−c(d(n)−1)/(LM)} + Cρ e^{−cN_min/(LM)} + Cρ_t[Σ_Δ 𝔪̂_Δ e^{−η dist(Δ,n)/(C_F LM)} + C′_χ g₀ Σ_b h(m̂_b) e^{−η dist(b,n)/(C_F LM)}],

  with the notation of §3a(6)–(7), the marks 𝔪̂_Δ including the tilt marks of §3j(4); on uncovered terms d(n) is replaced by d^Λ(n) = dist(Γ_n, E(B_ξ) ∪ (I∖D(Λ))) and ρ by ρ̂ = max(ρ, ρ_ph) (§3d(4)). The first term, the *deviation part*, is gauge-invariant local curvature and vanishes at flat data (§3, §3a(5)). The other terms, the *attached parts*, are attached to B_ξ, to the torus wrap and to the collar data with the decay shown, and carry no deviation (§3a(8));
- **(iii)** D1(iii)′ of §3e(0), proved in §3e(4): for real U₁ in the support of the term's indicators, which are kept as factors, the background-dependent factors (Υ¹_W, hence Y_W; the words U_j; f) extend analytically to 𝐔 = e^{ia′}U₁ with ‖a′‖_∞ < ϱ₁ = α_{1,1}/L, G-covariantly, and (ii) holds there with δ*^c_n(U₁) + a*_n(a′) in place of δ*_n(U₁); φ and e_X are numbers on each labelled term (§3b(5));
- **(iv)** all constants are independent of n, the torus and the labelled term.

No smallness of Σ_W ‖Y_W‖ is claimed. D1 is per labelled term; the assembly of F_ξ(1) into CRE's form Σχ₁T₁[· exp A₁] is §3c.

*Proof.* The form, (i) and (iv): §2 on covered terms, under (c5-7-T), and §3d on uncovered ones, read at m̂ (§3j(4)); the local form of E: §3b. (ii): §3a(6)–(8) with §3j(5), and §3d(4) on uncovered terms. (iii): §3e(4).
